## File drops while drafting during a running turn

Native composer/project drops listen on the owning Tauri Window. Main-content drops are emitted to that window after any embedded browser child is created, including when the child is hidden; a Webview-only listener misses those events. Running turns accept attachments into their next draft without sending it or stopping the current turn. Attachment staging retains the captured thread destination and existing liveness checks.

# Freddai — initial specification

Composer agent selection, November 4, 2026: choosing an explicit agent/account updates the new-thread composer immediately and must not acquire the global send/action lock. Remembering `last_selected` runs in the background with serialized writes so rapid selections persist in selection order. Snapshot polling refreshes the saved preference; preference failures remain visible. Sending uses the currently selected account without waiting for preference persistence or snapshot refresh. Existing launch validation and provider-specific model resets remain in place.

Verification: the actual selection-handler regression failed on the prior global-lock behavior and passes with deferred preference IPC. All 91 frontend logic tests, 15 component tests, and workspace typechecks passed. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the app, signed using the existing Developer ID, installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`, and verified its signature. Launch verification is pending: the approved `orca` native control is unavailable, and the running app window still maps an older bundle. Save drafts, quit Freddai normally, and reopen `/Applications/Freddai.app`. No live-provider turns or account mutations were used for verification.

Status: terminal-session workbench wireframe v3; frontend-only exploration.
Updated: November 3, 2026.

Desktop project ordering: each grouped sidebar project heading has a drag handle. Drop above or below a project group to change its position; Alt+Up/Down on the focused handle also moves it. The desktop saves the order locally across restarts and snapshot refreshes, preserves hidden projects during filtered reordering, and appends newly encountered projects. Thread recency ordering within projects is unchanged. Pointer capture keeps internal reordering separate from native folder import.

Project-order verification, November 3, 2026: workspace typechecks and 35 frontend logic tests passed. An isolated browser check passed pointer reordering, persistence across reload, and keyboard reordering, using empty account/session lists. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt, signed with the existing Developer ID, installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`, and verified its signature. Running processes still map older bundles. Native relaunch and native gesture verification remain pending because the approved `orca` control executable is unavailable (`command not found`). Save drafts, quit normally, and reopen `/Applications/Freddai.app`. No live-provider verification was performed.

Ordering reference inspected: Orca `tests/e2e/accumulated-workspace-order-fixture.ts` exercises persisted rank changes independently of activity updates. Freddai uses a local sidebar preference rather than mutating project records or shared host snapshots. No reference code copied.

Desktop thread list: the action to the right of each project heading is a plus icon labeled “New task in [project]”. It opens the existing new-thread flow with that project selected, including the existing account setup gate. Project configuration remains available in Settings → Projects.

Desktop project import: dropping local folders anywhere in the left thread navigation automatically registers each folder using its folder name, an assigned project color, and inherited account routing. The last imported project is selected and empty projects remain visible with their new-thread action. Canonical paths prevent duplicates, including symlink aliases; repeated drops preserve existing project settings. Regular files and inaccessible paths show errors, with valid folders in a mixed drop still imported. A drop overlay marks the navigation target. Modal dialogs block project import, and drops outside the navigation retain the existing conversation attachment behavior. Import does not create accounts, initialize repositories, modify project contents, or start an agent.

Implementation reference inspected for folder drops: Orca `src/renderer/src/components/sidebar/workspace-kanban-sidebar-drop.ts` uses explicit target hit testing and clears external drag target visuals; `src/main/ipc/dropped-path-resolution.ts` resolves platform paths at the runtime boundary. Freddai adapts those principles to Tauri native events, converting physical coordinates to CSS coordinates and validating/canonicalizing folders in Rust. No Orca implementation code was copied.

Project-drop verification, November 3, 2026: workspace typechecks and 24 frontend logic tests passed; all 60 Rust core unit tests, two host integration tests, and workspace doctests passed. The signed installed executable also passed an isolated host check for folder import, duplicate preservation, file rejection, and no account/session creation. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt, signed with the existing Developer ID, and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`. After normal Quit and relaunch, both UI and background host executable hashes matched the final installed bundle. A physical Finder drag gesture and live-provider turns were not exercised.

Account settings menus use the official shadcn/ui Dropdown Menu component from the New York v4 registry, with the existing Radix dependency and utility import. Portal content uses the same `dev-popup` theme scope as the existing project and model dropdowns, including background, foreground, hover, border, and focus colors. Escape dismisses the menu without closing settings.

Account settings presentation: each account row shows its name, connection type, sign-in status, a Default badge when selected, and a single accessible actions menu. Rename, Check connection, provider-specific sign-in/cancel actions, Set as default, and Remove connection live in that menu. Rename is independent of host-update and active-work state. Remove opens its confirmation even during active work, with explicit consent to stop that connection’s work before removal. Removal still requires the inline confirmation; rename retains its name editor and keyboard controls. Each installed provider has one Add account trigger, with current CLI sign-in and separate-account choices inside its menu where supported. Installation paths and provider setup instructions are collapsed under Setup details. This changes presentation only; it does not create connections, change defaults, or initiate sign-in.

Account settings verification: workspace typechecks and 24 frontend logic tests passed. The macOS app was rebuilt, Developer ID signed, installed, and relaunched. Native screenshots verified the open shadcn menu uses the existing dark palette, keyboard selection uses the dark highlight, and Escape dismisses only the menu and returns focus to its trigger. Active agents stayed on their existing background host. No account mutations, sign-ins, or live-provider turns were used for verification; rename remained guarded while the older host was active.

## Scope of this step

Maintain this specification alongside the interactive product wireframe as Denis describes use cases. Explore UX, layout, visual architecture, and system behavior only. Do not connect to a backend or run real coding agents. Freddai is the working name from the workspace; branding is not final. “Tori” is interpreted as Tauri.

## Confirmed direction

- Native product: Rust with Tauri and a React/TypeScript frontend.
- Desktop targets: macOS, Windows, and Linux.
- Mobile app planned from the beginning; iOS is explicitly anticipated. Android remains a target to confirm.
- UI direction: Tailwind CSS and shadcn/ui, with reusable UI primitives and styles.
- Marketing website: Next.js with Tailwind CSS and daisyUI themes, separate from the native product and its browser wireframe preview.
- Website scope: sales, documentation, thank-you pages, contact, and potentially a blog.
- Use the Recursive Solutions / Growth Engine SDK for the blog and probably contact. The exact package, supported APIs, authentication, and data handling still need verification before integration.
- Plan for macOS and iOS code signing. No signing credentials or release configuration are supplied yet.

## Workspace and boundaries

| Location | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `apps/web` | Next.js distribution landing page with Recursive Engine SDK foundation |
| `apps/native` | Vite/React entry point for the product; also runs in a browser for wireframing |
| `apps/native/src-tauri` | Tauri host shared by desktop and future mobile builds |
| `packages/app` | Shared product React screens, independent of Next.js and native APIs |
| `packages/ui` | Shared Tailwind tokens, utility functions, and shadcn component destination |
| `crates/core` | Platform-independent Rust domain logic; deliberately empty until features are defined |

pnpm manages JavaScript packages; Cargo manages Rust crates. Desktop and mobile use one Tauri application rather than duplicated native hosts. The Rust library includes the mobile entry point and static/dynamic library outputs. Native iOS/Android projects will be generated when their toolchains, identifiers, and signing choices are confirmed.

The marketing site must not depend on native APIs. Future product screens should access platform services through narrow adapters; desktop-only capabilities must be explicit and have defined mobile behavior. A browser preview will use clearly marked mock data where native functionality is unavailable. The wireframe uses illustrative projects, threads, agent names, and activity. All changes are in React memory and reset on reload.

Tailwind and shadcn configuration are present. Add actual shadcn components as the interface requires them; no component library inventory is generated prematurely.

## Required implementation reference: Orca

Primary reference: https://github.com/stablyai/orca

Before implementing a relevant feature, inspect how Orca implements it, identify the actual owning files, and record what we reuse, adapt, or deliberately do differently. Avoid independently reinventing behavior already solved there. This is a reference, not a requirement to clone every Orca feature.

Inspected baseline: `a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d` on November 3, 2026.

| Reference | What to examine |
| --- | --- |
| [src/main](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/tree/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/src/main) | Desktop runtime responsibilities and service boundaries |
| [src/preload](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/tree/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/src/preload) | Renderer/runtime bridge; adapt the boundary to Tauri commands and events |
| [src/renderer](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/tree/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/src/renderer) | Frontend organization and relevant UI behavior |
| [src/shared](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/tree/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/src/shared) | Shared contracts and types |
| [vite.web.config.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/vite.web.config.ts) | Separate web entry/build around a renderer |
| [LICENSE](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/LICENSE) | MIT terms; retain required notices if code is copied |

Confirmed: Orca uses Electron, with separate main/preload/renderer/shared directories. It is not a drop-in Tauri starter. Only the top-level structure, package metadata, web build configuration, and license were inspected at this stage; individual product features have not been audited. No Orca implementation code has been copied.

## Distribution and signing

- macOS: Developer ID signed bundle via `pnpm native:install`. iOS/Android: signed separately (Xcode team / Android keystore); not covered by the desktop updater.
- Windows and Linux: build and test on their respective supported environments. A successful Mac build does not verify these platforms.

### In-app updater (desktop only)

- Rust owns the network work (`apps/native/src-tauri/src/updater.rs`; the webview CSP stays local-only). Plugins: `tauri-plugin-updater`, `-process`, `-notification`, gated to non-mobile targets.
- Feed: `https://github.com/desduvauchelle/freddai/releases/latest/download/latest.json` (`plugins.updater` in `tauri.conf.json`, minisign public key embedded). CI publishes `latest.json` and signed artifacts per release, passing `bundle.createUpdaterArtifacts` as an override; the config leaves it off so local `pnpm native:install` builds need no signing key.
- Signing key: `~/.tauri/freddai-updater.key` (outside the repo); its password is in the login Keychain (service `freddai-updater-key`, account `freddai`). CI needs both as secrets (`TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`, `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY_PASSWORD`). Losing the key means installed apps can never auto-update again.
- Schedule: check 30 s after launch, then every 12 h, plus an on-demand "Check for updates" (More actions menu). macOS also exposes "Check for Updates…" directly below About in the native Freddai application menu, preserving the default system menus. It brings the main window forward and requests the same manual check, including up-to-date feedback and error/retry banners. Mobile companions retain their separate platform distribution flow; the desktop updater is not available there. Every check logs a structured event (`updater.check.start|up_to_date|available|error`); failures set the UI state to `error` ("Couldn't check for updates: ...") and show a banner with retry.
- State (`updater_state` command + `updater://state` event): idle | checking | available{version, notes, manual} | downloading{downloaded,total} | ready_to_restart | error{message}.
- Local builds (`pnpm native:install` sets compile-time `FREDDAI_LOCAL_BUILD=1`, read via `option_env!`) still run and log checks (`updater.check local_build=true remote=<v> outcome=skipped_notify`) but never notify or show an automatic banner, including when the release feed is unavailable; automatic failures remain in structured logs. Explicit manual checks still surface feed errors; a successful manual check shows "Local build - published version is <v>". Tauri's default comparator (offer only when remote > current) is kept for release builds.
- One OS notification per version ("Freddai X is available - open Freddai to update"), remembered in `updater-notified-version` in the app data dir.
- "Update & restart": download + install, persist composer drafts to localStorage (drafts are memory-only otherwise; restored on next launch), then restart the window process. The detached background host and its agent threads keep running; the existing `host_update_pending` / idle-upgrade logic moves the host to the new bundle once threads are idle.
- Linux without `APPIMAGE` cannot self-update: the banner says the version is available and opens the release page. Windows (NSIS) uses the same flow and finds nothing until Windows releases are published.
- Still planned: Developer ID notarization of release builds, and Android/iOS distribution.

- Both JavaScript app entry points typecheck and build.
