## Setup verification — November 3, 2026

Passed: dependency installation, workspace TypeScript checks, Next.js production build, Vite product frontend production build, `cargo check --workspace` on macOS, and Rust formatting check. Both local entry points were opened in the app browser and their placeholder content and layouts inspected. A neutral development icon was added because Tauri requires an icon even with installer bundling disabled.

Not verified: native app launch or release executable, Windows/Linux builds, iOS/Android builds, device behavior, signing, notarization, deployment, SDK integration, or full responsive/accessibility testing. No product features were implemented.

## Product direction — thread management

Freddai is a coding workspace for developers coordinating many AI coding threads across projects, locations, tools, and feature work. Repeatedly finding a project, opening a thread, checking output, and answering questions creates fragmentation. Recurring error-log checks and follow-up fixes add more threads to manage.

A thread is fundamentally a terminal session where the developer runs Claude Code, Codex, or another coding CLI. Session identity, terminal input/output, runtime status, and project context are the core model; it is not a generic assistant chat. Orca remains the implementation reference. Terminal execution and PTY integration are out of scope for this frontend-only wireframe.

### UX proposal: attention first, projects as context

The default landing view aggregates work across projects. Projects remain filters and context labels, rather than prerequisites to capturing an idea. A global capture action and persistent pet are available in every view. Thread details stay in a persistent, resizable adjacent pane, preserving the user’s place.

| Surface | Purpose | Wireframe behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Threads | Inspect work across projects | Flat session list ordered by most recently updated, status dots, attention/schedule filters, and an adjacent terminal pane |
| Board | Plan and manage work across or within projects | Backlog → Next → In progress → Needs you → Review → Done; drag cards or use a keyboard-accessible stage selector |
| Office | Make distributed agent activity understandable and playful | Pixel-style room with desks, question/review cues, clock shrine for scheduled work; hover labels and clickable agents |
| Thread details | Act without navigating through projects | Persistent pane with Terminal/Activity tabs, summary, question, stage, replies, review/continue controls |
| Capture pet | Reduce the friction of starting work | Open a composer anywhere, drop files to capture names, add a message, confirm a project, add to backlog |

These views share one local thread collection. Search and project filters apply across views. Stage changes update the dashboard, board, and office consistently. Repeated avatars currently represent thread assignments, not a finalized model of unique agent identity.

### Capture and routing

Users can write a thought before choosing a project. Explicit known project names in the message produce a deterministic suggestion; the user can override it. If no project matches, require a project selection before creating a card. Capture creates Backlog work and does not start execution. A file drop on the pet opens the same composer; only names are retained, no file contents are read or uploaded.

A future desktop companion could respond to a wake word and accept file drops outside the app. The prototype companion is confined to the page. In the prototype page, “Try a sample voice capture” inserts illustrative text and involves no microphone or speech recognition. (The shipped app has separate, user-initiated push-to-talk dictation, see Voice dictation, 2026-11-04, and a user-started hands-free Voice call mode on the Mac, see Voice call mode, 2026-11-09. Neither listens unless the user starts it, and there is no wake word outside the dashboard.) The wake word is decided for the dashboard on desktop and companions: see Dashboard command center (Phase 2) in the Commander-in-Chief spec. Always-listening outside the dashboard, consent beyond that, routing ambiguity elsewhere, and OS integration remain undecided.

### Attention and lifecycle

Needs you means the agent is blocked on a decision and should show the question and an answer control. Review means output is ready for examination. Saving direction queues the thread in Next; accepting a review moves it to Done. Sending work back to Backlog clears it from the attention section. Every detail view also exposes a stage selector as a non-drag alternative.

“Continue” is an action that returns work to Next, not a separate column in this first proposal. Done does not mean committed, pushed, merged, uploaded, or deployed. Whether delivery deserves a separate tracked state is an open product decision. No deployment action is present.

### Routines and office

Scheduled threads show an illustrative next-run label and a clock shrine. The sidebar keeps the office compact; expanding it opens the Office view. Selecting or focusing an agent exposes its thread context. The first office is static pixel-style CSS geometry, not an animated simulation. Walking to desks, waiting animations, richer hover cards, a true desktop pet, and a schedule editor are follow-up explorations.

### Confirmed office foundation — Pixel Agents

Use [Pixel Agents](https://github.com/pixel-agents-hq/pixel-agents) as the open-source foundation for the office view. Denis confirmed this is the intended project on November 3, 2026. The primary outcome is to let users build/customize an office and see their agents represented inside it, with location and activity making their current state understandable.

- Adapt its office layout editor, pixel-art rendering, character movement, desk assignments, and activity animations rather than independently rebuilding those systems. Preserve editable floors, walls, furniture, and saved layouts.
- Connect Freddai's agent runtime through an adapter that maps real agent identity, activity, and waiting states into the office. Pixel Agents currently documents Claude Code as its reference integration; support for Freddai's other agents must be implemented and verified rather than assumed.
- Keep the office synchronized with the same agent/thread state used by Threads, Board, and thread details. Selecting an agent opens its associated thread context; visual animation must not imply execution or progress without supporting runtime events. The relationship between persistent agent identity and thread executions remains an open decision.
- Preserve the optional compact sidebar office and expanded Office view, plus accessible text/status and keyboard/touch access to agent context.
- Pixel Agents is an existing application, not an assumed drop-in library. Before integration, inspect and pin the relevant source revision, identify reusable renderer/editor and event contracts, and define the boundary with Freddai's React/Tauri architecture. Retain required [MIT license notices](https://github.com/pixel-agents-hq/pixel-agents/blob/main/LICENSE) and verify the separate provenance and reuse terms of any bundled or external artwork we adopt.

This records a product and implementation decision only. Pixel Agents has not been installed, integrated, or locally tested; the current CSS office remains the wireframe placeholder. Real agent execution remains outside the current frontend-only exploration.

### Visual and mobile direction

Desktop developer tool: viewport-filling dark workbench, compact typography, narrow navigation, recently updated session rows, resizable panes, persistent thread context, and a bottom status bar. Use actual shadcn components rather than merely its configuration. No marketing headlines, slogans, hero spacing, assistant-oriented copy, or SaaS dashboard treatment. The office is optional and secondary. On narrow screens the selected thread takes the main space; closing it returns to the list. The board scrolls horizontally. No information or action should depend solely on color, hover, or dragging.

### Orca implementation evidence for this iteration

Inspected at the pinned reference commit:
- [AgentKanbanBoard.tsx](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/src/renderer/src/components/dashboard-popout/AgentKanbanBoard.tsx): snapshot-fed cards grouped by status, shared filtering, board-level detail hosting.
- [AgentKanbanCard.tsx](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d/src/renderer/src/components/dashboard-popout/AgentKanbanCard.tsx): card context and status presentation; explicit comment explaining why dialogs belong outside cards that move between columns.

Adapted concepts: shared state across views, project context on cards, and detail dialog independent of card lifetime. Deliberately different: capture before project navigation, a planning-oriented six-stage board, and an optional office view. No Electron IPC, PTYs, backend services, or Orca code copied into this prototype.

### Open questions for the next walkthrough

- Should capture launch work automatically, or always enter a triage queue?
- Is an agent a persistent character, a provider, or one execution of a thread?
- Which statuses are essential, and how should review, continuation, and delivery differ?
- How should an ambiguous project suggestion be handled in voice capture?
- Should routine findings propose fixes or start them after user-defined approval?
- Which office interactions improve comprehension versus simply adding atmosphere?

### Prototype boundaries

No backend, native commands, terminal agents, microphone, network requests for product data, real scheduler, file uploads, durable persistence, or SDK integration. Sample projects and activity are fictional. The website and Rust runtime remain unchanged in this iteration. The workbench uses registry-generated shadcn components in `packages/ui/src/components`: Button, Input, Textarea, Badge, Tabs, Dialog, Select, and Resizable. App CSS defines layout and density; shadcn owns interactive primitives. The generated Separator is available for future composition.

### Wireframe v1 verification

Passed workspace TypeScript checks and both frontend production builds. After final dialog-label and office-filter refinements, repeated TypeScript and product frontend build checks. Local state checks cover immutable stage updates, preserving unrelated threads, project-name matching, and unmatched capture text.

Browser-verified: dashboard, board, expanded office, agent-to-thread navigation, answer submission moving a thread to Next and removing it from attention, simulated voice text suggesting Freddai, and capture creating a new Backlog card. Reload restores sample data. Screenshot: `docs/previews/workspace-v1.png`.

Not yet browser-verified: drag/drop gestures, file picker, mobile viewport, all keyboard paths, or full accessibility audit. Native/device builds and backend execution remain out of scope.

## Revision v2 — desktop developer workbench

Denis explicitly rejected v1’s SaaS-site appearance and lack of actual shadcn usage. This correction supersedes the earlier visual direction. Freddai is a coding/development tool, not a personal assistant or marketing site.

- Replace the scrolling dashboard and large headings with a full-height three-part workbench: project/filter explorer, thread browser, selected-thread context.
- Use real shadcn components generated from the official registry, backed by Radix and react-resizable-panels. Adjust generated utility imports to the existing shared `cn` helper and declare dependencies directly in the UI package.
- Superseded in v3: Threads is flat and ordered by most recent activity. All threads, Needs attention, and Scheduled provide cross-project filtering. Project filtering and text search remain available.
- Thread selection persists across Threads, Board, and Office; replies and state updates do not dismiss the context pane. A close control expands the browsing area.
- Add keyboard shortcuts: Cmd/Ctrl+K opens New thread; Cmd/Ctrl+F focuses the thread filter. Scope these shortcuts to this local application.
- Keep quick capture and file drops in the status bar, with a small pixel companion. Keep the compact office in the sidebar, expandable via Office navigation. No ambient assistant prompts.
- Preserve local capture/routing, file-name attachments, sample voice text, replies, stage changes, review actions, filters, and board drag/drop. None connects to a backend.
- Activity tab explicitly reports the disconnected runtime; do not invent terminal execution or logs.

Verification: workspace typechecks and both frontend builds passed. Browser verified initial workbench rendering, reply submission moving a task to Next while preserving its pane, attention count updates, shadcn capture dialog focus, sample prompt routing and backlog creation, and Board switching while preserving the selected thread. Mobile and drag/drop remain unverified in this pass. Native launch, execution, and backend integration remain out of scope.

## Revision v3 — sessions, recency, and one header

- A thread represents a terminal session running a coding CLI (Claude Code, Codex, or another agent). The current Terminal pane is an illustrative mock, not a functioning terminal emulator or PTY. Office character names are separate from CLI identity.
- Threads is an ungrouped list ordered by `updatedAt` descending. Creation, replies, and stage changes update that timestamp; opening a session or changing filters does not. Future real terminal activity should update this field under a defined event policy. Ties use session ID for deterministic ordering.
- Status uses a small dot with a text label available to assistive technology and on hover: pulsing gray = running; green = done; yellow = needs input or review; outlined gray = backlog/queued. Green for done interprets the correction in Denis’s spoken color description. Reduced-motion preference disables pulsing.
- Board retains stage columns. Changing the Threads ordering does not remove Board organization or attention filters.
- One top application bar contains the brand, sidebar toggle, Threads/Board/Office tabs, search, and New thread. Remove Workspace text, project/view breadcrumbs, Prototype badge, duplicate New control, and the second toolbar row. Keep project context on the session pane and in filtering controls.
- The selected session stays open as recency changes. Keyboard shortcuts and local capture remain available.

Verified: workspace TypeScript checks, product frontend build, deterministic recency and immutable update checks. Browser verified the unified header, flat initial ordering, accessible status labels, and a reply moving its session to the top while preserving selection. Native terminal execution remains intentionally disconnected; mobile and reduced-motion behavior have not been browser-tested in this revision.

## Revision v4 — streamlined thread rows

Threads uses a single compact scan line: status dot, session title, project, CLI icon, optional schedule icon, and last-update time. Remove the session ID, descriptive preview, question preview, and written CLI name from list rows. Keep those details in the selected terminal pane. The project label is omitted when an explicit project filter already supplies that context. Titles truncate visually with the full text available on hover and to assistive technology. CLI icons have accessible names and hover labels; they are generic interface glyphs, not vendor logos. Recency ordering, selection, filters, and Board/Office behavior are unchanged.

## Provider accounts, local sessions, and usage

Support multiple independently authenticated local profiles for each coding provider, including Claude, ChatGPT/Codex, Gemini, Grok, and extensible additional providers. Denis’s concrete example is one Claude subscription and two ChatGPT subscriptions. “Cloud” is interpreted as Claude. A provider account/profile is distinct from a thread: multiple terminal threads may use the same profile and share its usage allowance. Multiple sessions on one account must not imply multiple subscriptions or independent quotas.

Footer: individual account controls with a provider glyph inside a circular remaining-capacity meter, a stable account color, provider/name, explicit percentage left, and reset time. A full ring means all capacity remains; an empty ring means exhausted. Unknown usage has an empty track labeled Usage unknown, never a zero percentage. Low capacity also has an amber text warning, independent of the account identity color. Account colors remain stable when other profiles are removed. Each account opens the shadcn management dialog; a plus control provides access even with no profiles. The footer prioritizes these controls and quick capture; remove Local workspace, Runtime disconnected, and Local session mock text. Transient action announcements remain available to assistive technology. Compact widths hide reset text and allow horizontal account scrolling. Opening it shows a compact account list: provider, user-defined label, usage per available limit window, and when that window resets. Keep accounts separate; do not average unlike provider quotas or present one misleading global capacity percentage. The current mock uses three illustrative single-window meters for the example setup. All percentages and reset durations are explicitly sample values, not Denis’s actual usage.

Allow adding multiple profiles for the same provider. The wireframe supports adding labeled Claude, ChatGPT, Gemini, Grok, and Other profiles and removing them locally. New profiles show usage/reset unavailable rather than fabricated values. No sign-in, credential access, subscription lookup, or terminal switching occurs. Profiles and changes reset on page reload.

Future implementation requirements:
- Give each profile a stable ID, provider, user label, connection/auth state, and isolated local credential/configuration context; changing one profile must not sign out another.
- Bind each terminal session to an explicit profile. Show the profile in session details and offer selection at launch. Never silently switch an existing terminal to another account when a quota is exhausted.
- Retrieve usage only through verified supported provider/CLI mechanisms. Availability, exact semantics, permission requirements, and refresh policy must be researched per provider before implementation; do not assume ChatGPT subscription usage equals Codex API usage or that all providers expose quota data.
- Support multiple independently labeled windows (for example short-term and weekly) where actually provided, including model-specific limits. Keep usage units and denominator clear.
- Show last refreshed time, absolute reset time with timezone and a relative countdown when known. Mark unknown, stale, disconnected, exhausted, and refresh-error states explicitly. Never infer zero usage from missing data.
- Distinguish provider-reported reset timestamps from estimates. Refresh before treating a passed reset time as restored capacity.
- Account switching, routing recommendations, automatic failover, real sign-in, and account-to-terminal binding are future decisions, not implemented in this UX pass.

Account setup dialog layout (native app): a summary bar at the top reflects real state (for example "2 accounts ready · New threads use Claude Code · Work", or "No accounts ready yet — sign in to one below to start.") with a Scan again button. Below it is one card per provider (Codex, then Claude Code), each owning everything for that provider: an install chip (Installed / Checking… / Not found / Open desktop app) with the installation location in a disclosure; when not found, only the install instruction. Otherwise the card lists that provider's accounts, each row showing colour dot, name, a connection tag ("Uses CLI sign-in" or "Separate login"), status (last-checked time is a tooltip), one contextual action (Sign in with browser / Sign in in Terminal / Cancel sign-in; connected-CLI accounts show "Managed by the {name} CLI"), small Check and (Codex, signed in) Sign in again buttons, and a "Default" radio (disabled until signed in, titled "Sign in first"; one default across all providers). Beneath the rows: a highlighted "Found an existing {name} sign-in on this Mac" suggestion with "Connect it" when a CLI sign-in is detected and none is connected (it shares the CLI's current account), compact existing-sign-in check status, and "Add {name} account" / "Add another {name} account" which expands the inline create form (name, colour, "Create account & sign in") inside the same card. The footer button is "Done" when at least one account is ready, otherwise "Set up later".

The UI remains frontend-only. Account controls open a shadcn dialog with matching colors, remaining-capacity meters, reset labels, and local profile controls. Sample-data labeling stays in that dialog.


## Revision v5: conversation surface and mobile companion

The thread remains backed by an agent process on the host computer, but its primary interface is a conversation, not a terminal emulator. This supersedes v3's default Terminal presentation. Use proportional text, clear agent/user authorship, readable message spacing, contextual input requests, attachment chips, and a rounded persistent composer. Preserve thread status, review controls, project context, Activity, and recency behavior. Future tool calls and changes should be expandable structured cards; raw process output belongs in optional diagnostics, not the default reading path.

Wireframe: Conversation replaces Terminal. Attach accepts multiple files, and dropping files anywhere in the conversation stages removable attachment chips. Sending text, attachments, or both appends a queued user message to this thread and moves it to Next. Cmd/Ctrl+Enter sends. Attachment names only are retained in memory; no bytes are uploaded, parsed, executed, or sent to an agent. Switching threads resets the current composer as before. Before production, preserve drafts by thread and show explicit preparing/ready/failed attachment states. Pending uploads must retain their original thread/account/host destination even if the user switches views.

### Orca implementation research (2026-11-03)

Inspected public source at commit `f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f`. This is reference evidence, not code executed or a guarantee of compatibility with Freddai. Orca currently includes both terminal and native chat paths. The relevant finding is that a rich chat surface is backed by structured provider events, not merely a visual skin over ANSI terminal output.

Source map (permalinks to inspected files):
- [src/main/claude/claude-stream-json-connection.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/claude/claude-stream-json-connection.ts): Claude connection loads the Anthropic Agent SDK, resolves the local CLI executable, and exposes message, permission, dialog, fault, and exit callbacks.
- [src/main/claude/claude-agent-sdk-process-spawn.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/claude/claude-agent-sdk-process-spawn.ts): The SDK spawn hook retains Orca ownership of the provider child process and tracks its managed lifecycle. A closed view is not proof the child stopped.
- [src/main/codex/codex-structured-item-translation.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/codex/codex-structured-item-translation.ts): Codex provider items are translated into text/image blocks, tool calls, questions, and file diffs. Unknown or partial provider payloads need explicit handling.
- [src/main/codex/codex-structured-launch-resolution.test.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/codex/codex-structured-launch-resolution.test.ts): Launch tests explicitly cover the Codex app-server invocation and launch argument variations.
- [src/shared/agent-session/journal/agent-session-journal-types.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/shared/agent-session/journal/agent-session-journal-types.ts): Versioned journal render-model records and bounded tool/diff payloads provide a shared representation rather than a terminal screenshot.
- [src/renderer/src/components/native-chat/use-native-chat-file-drops.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/renderer/src/components/native-chat/use-native-chat-file-drops.ts): Desktop drops distinguish workspace/external files and bind asynchronous attachment capture to pane, draft, session, worktree, and runtime identities. Retargeting invalidates the captured destination.
- [mobile/src/session/mobile-agent-session-attachment-upload.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/mobile/src/session/mobile-agent-session-attachment-upload.ts): Mobile attachment transfer uses uploadStart, chunked uploadAppend, uploadCommit, and best-effort uploadAbort after failure; commit returns a host path/name/byte length.
- [mobile/src/session/use-mobile-structured-agent-session.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/mobile/src/session/use-mobile-structured-agent-session.ts): Mobile projects structured host sessions into messages and questions, uses host capability flags, and separates session state, mutations, queued messages, and stopping.
- [mobile/src/transport/pairing.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/mobile/src/transport/pairing.ts): Mobile parses bounded pairing offers from a custom-scheme link or pairing code and validates their schema.
- [mobile/src/transport/mobile-relay-rpc-streams.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/mobile/src/transport/mobile-relay-rpc-streams.ts): Relay stream management tracks subscriptions, routing envelopes, cancellation, and unsubscribe identities. This is evidence for explicit stream lifecycle management, not proof of security properties.

### Freddai integration direction (proposed, not implemented)

Use provider adapters that normalize supported structured output into a versioned event model: host ID, account ID, session ID, turn ID, event ID/sequence, message blocks, tool calls/results, diff references, approval/question requests, and lifecycle facts. The Rust host owns sessions and durable history; React renders projections. Evaluate a supervised Node sidecar for Claude's TypeScript SDK versus a supported Rust-accessible protocol before choosing the boundary. Orca's Electron/TypeScript process layer cannot be copied verbatim into Tauri/Rust. Verify current provider protocol/SDK contracts before implementation. CLI-only providers without structured events need an explicitly limited fallback, not guessed chat reconstructed from arbitrary terminal text.

Before building the full integration, prove one vertical slice for Claude and one for Codex: launch using the selected account, stream a response, submit a follow-up, handle a permission/question, attach a file and image, cancel with confirmed process outcome, resume after UI reconnect, and replay without duplicate messages. Test split frames, partial output, provider errors, process death, unsupported events, duplicate/out-of-order delivery, and switching threads during attachment preparation. Provider acceptance, UI queued state, and successful execution must remain distinct. The prior failed attempt's actual cause is unknown; this research identifies a better boundary but does not diagnose that attempt.

### Mobile app connected to the MacBook Pro

Required companion app: the MacBook Pro is the execution host and source of truth. The phone does not run the coding CLIs or duplicate their credentials. Mobile reflects desktop projects, recent threads, attention states, conversation content, and account capacity. Use a phone-appropriate single-column thread list and conversation detail; retain Board and Office as secondary views. Shared domain types and event projection live outside desktop-only filesystem/process modules. Mobile packaging/native framework remains a separate implementation decision within the Rust/React multi-app plan.

First release scope: pair to the MacBook, see host connection/last-seen status, monitor running agents, read conversations and tool/change summaries, and identify agents waiting for input. A host disconnect must display cached data as stale rather than implying the agent stopped. Mac sleep, lost network, and app termination are explicit states; remote monitoring requires the host service to remain reachable. Do not promise that a phone connection wakes the Mac.

Pairing should require explicit approval on the Mac, short-lived single-use offers, per-device credentials stored in platform secure storage, and device revocation. Start with authenticated encrypted LAN access; remote access outside the LAN uses the end-to-end-encrypted mailbox relay, and localhost preview traffic uses the relay's blind tunnel (see "Companion preview tunnel"), each subject to identity verification and threat review. Do not expose a raw shell or unauthenticated host port. A relay must not receive provider credentials. These are Freddai requirements, not claims established by the Orca files inspected above.

Use snapshot plus cursor-based event updates, reconnect/backoff, deduplication, capability negotiation, and bounded history pagination. Reconnecting must not launch a second agent or replay a command. Initial monitoring permissions should be read-only. Future replies, approvals, stop actions, and attachments require explicit control permission, stable request IDs, host acknowledgements, and conflict handling across desktop/phone; disconnected drafts must never appear delivered. Push notifications for completed work or required input are future infrastructure, with configurable content privacy.

Acceptance scenarios: pair/revoke a phone; desktop and mobile show the same session; reconnect without losing/duplicating messages; survive network changes and sleeping host; distinguish stale usage from current usage; show an input request on mobile; deny commands from a monitoring-only device. No host service, pairing, relay, mobile binary, or live backend is built in this wireframe pass.

Verification for v5: workspace typecheck and product production build passed. Browser verified Conversation rendering, selecting a local attachment, submitting it with text, the queued message retaining its filename, composer clearing, attention count decreasing, and recency/stage update. Browser drag/drop gesture, mobile viewport, device pairing, and real agent execution remain unverified. Attachment drop handling is implemented but not claimed as browser-tested. Preview: `docs/previews/conversation-v5.png`.


## Revision v6: navigation and agent/account routing

### Navigation

Remove the duplicate All projects sidebar row. All threads is the cross-project inbox; Needs attention and Scheduled are also cross-project shortcuts. Selecting any shortcut clears the project filter. The Projects section contains only actual projects; selecting one resets the status scope to All threads for that project. Keep the list's Project filter for combining a scope with a project explicitly. Sidebar active styling must not imply a global shortcut is active while restricted to a project. Text search remains independent and stays visible.

### Preference contract (specification only)

An agent choice is a tuple of provider adapter, account profile, and optional supported model, scoped to an execution host. A UI label such as ChatGPT refers here to a ChatGPT-authenticated Codex profile, not a browser ChatGPT session. Different accounts may run concurrently in separate provider processes with isolated authentication/configuration contexts. One running process must not be made to impersonate multiple accounts by overwriting shared credentials.

Global Settings → Agents:
- New-session preference: Prefer last used (recommended) or Use fixed default.
- Fixed/default fallback: agent + compatible account + optional model. Last used means the last successfully launched interactive session on this host, not the last clicked thread, failed launch, background usage probe, automation, or mobile reconnect. Persist it across app restarts. Never carry a model selection into an incompatible provider.
- When no prior successful launch or fallback exists, require selection in the composer.

Project Settings → Agent:
- Inherit global preference.
- Always use a specified agent/account (and optional model). Example: Freddai → Claude Code / Personal; Orbit API → Codex / Account 2.
- Remember last used for this project, falling back to global policy on first use.

Resolution order for a NEW interactive session:
1. Explicit composer selection for this launch.
2. Project's fixed tuple, if configured; global last-used never overrides it.
3. Project's last-successful tuple, when that project uses Remember last used.
4. Global last-successful tuple when global mode is Prefer last used.
5. Global fixed/fallback tuple.
6. Ask the user to choose.

Validate the chosen tuple before launch: host availability, installed adapter/version, account existence/auth state, organization restrictions, model compatibility. A configured but unavailable project account blocks launch with Sign in / Choose another actions; do not silently fall through to another subscription. No silent fallback on exhausted quota, unknown usage, or expired credentials. An explicit override affects this launch only unless the user chooses Save as project default. New-thread composer must display the resolved agent/account/color and reason (Project default, Last used, Global default, or This session). If project inference changes, re-resolve inherited defaults; retain an explicit selection and flag incompatibility.

Existing sessions always use their stored binding. Resuming, replying, changing views, changing project defaults, or opening on mobile must not rerun the new-session resolver. Switching provider/account in an existing conversation is an explicit new-session/fork flow; provider-supported transcript continuation is a separately verified capability, not assumed. Scheduled tasks store a deliberate binding when configured rather than inheriting a changing global last-used account at execution time.

### Host session management

Rust host services: AccountRegistry (profile/auth references and health), PreferenceResolver (pure deterministic selection), ProviderAdapter (login, validation, launch/resume, events, cancellation), SessionSupervisor (process ownership and lifecycle), and UsageService (per-account observations). UI/mobile receive public metadata and typed events, never credential material. Reuse the v5 structured-event model.

Persist these separately:
- AccountProfile: stable ID, provider, host ID, provider identity/workspace ID when verified, label/color, auth method, managed config location/credential reference, connection status. Email alone is not an identity key.
- ProjectAgentPolicy and GlobalAgentPolicy: mode, complete compatible tuple, policy revision; last-successful records separate from preferences.
- SessionBinding: stable session/thread ID, host, project/worktree, adapter/version, account ID, resolved model, provider session ID, config namespace, creation time and resolution reason. Immutable account binding for the process lifetime.
- RuntimeLease: process identity, generation/start token, live lifecycle state and last acknowledged event cursor. A PID alone is not enough to reclaim ownership after restart.

Lifecycle: draft → resolving → authentication required / starting → running → waiting for input / idle → stopping → exited, with explicit failed/disconnected states. Board stages remain user-facing workflow state and are not proof of process lifecycle. A transport disconnection does not prove the process exited. Cancel requires acknowledgement/observed exit; restarting the UI attaches to the supervised session rather than launching a duplicate.

Allocate a stable per-account namespace beneath Freddai's platform app-data directory, keyed by generated IDs, never email/path supplied by a prompt. Use provider-specific child environment variables; do not modify the parent shell's environment or the user's default CLI credentials. Login, validation, usage reads, launch, and resume must resolve the SAME account namespace. Keep workspace cwd/worktree separate from config home. Config isolation is not an OS sandbox; provider tools still need their normal permission policy.

Serialize account login/logout/credential mutations and coordinate token refresh according to provider behavior. Do not make disconnected credential copies per thread and race token refreshes back into a shared store. Concurrent sessions under one account share its allowance. Removing/logging out an account with live sessions requires an explicit stop/re-auth policy and must not remap those sessions. App-owned credential files require restrictive permissions and must stay out of Git/logs/telemetry. A secure-store adapter must prove account-scoped lookup, not assume it from a directory name. Existing system CLI profiles are opt-in imports/references; never silently rewrite them.

### Provider evidence and design implications

Research date: 2026-11-03. Official documentation is mutable; pin adapter/CLI compatibility during implementation and recheck these contracts.

- [Claude authentication: multiple accounts](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/authentication#log-in-with-multiple-accounts) documents independent configuration directories selected with CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR for claude.ai logins/API keys. It explicitly excludes isolation of two Console sign-ins without API keys. Freddai must model auth method and mark that unsupported case rather than promise universal isolation.
- [Codex authentication and credential storage](https://developers.openai.com/codex/auth/) documents CODEX_HOME/auth.json for file storage, OS credential storage, and ephemeral storage. Directory isolation alone does not establish keyring isolation. The initial managed-profile proof should use supported account-scoped storage, verify actual provider identity before launch, and respect administrator-enforced auth settings. ChatGPT subscription and API-key billing remain distinct.
- [Orca: src/main/claude-accounts/claude-profile-paths.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/claude-accounts/claude-profile-paths.ts): Defines an account/host/runtime profile descriptor and generated profile home. Its login/launch path normalization is explicitly tied to Keychain identity. Reuse this stable-identity principle; verify platform credential behavior.
- [Orca: src/main/claude-accounts/claude-profile-launch-preparation.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/claude-accounts/claude-profile-launch-preparation.ts): Requires successful initial setup before launch; later refresh is bounded. Do not launch against half-created profiles.
- [Orca: src/main/codex-accounts/runtime-home-per-account-homes.test.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/codex-accounts/runtime-home-per-account-homes.test.ts): Tests self-contained managed homes and isolation of two accounts, including matching emails. This is source/test evidence; those upstream tests were inspected, not run here.
- [Orca: src/main/codex-accounts/runtime-home-service-paths.ts](https://github.com/stablyai/orca/blob/f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f/src/main/codex-accounts/runtime-home-service-paths.ts): Separates managed-account roots, system-default paths, runtime metadata, and legacy migration pointers. Freddai should use per-account homes from the start instead of adopting legacy shared-auth swapping.

- [codex-accounts project](https://github.com/marivaldojr/codex-accounts#how-it-works), an additional open-source reference, describes switching by replacing the active home's auth file and reloading VS Code, while using isolated temporary homes for usage queries. Its documented environment-inheritance limitations show why switching a global active account is not our concurrent-session design. Reference its distinction between account inspection and active-session selection; do not adopt global auth-file replacement. This secondary project was reviewed through its README, not executed or audited.

Gemini, Grok, and other adapters remain capability-gated placeholders until their supported authentication, storage isolation, structured output, and resumption contracts are researched. Do not invent equivalent environment variables or claim simultaneous-account support for them yet.

### Proof required before backend implementation is considered ready

- Deterministic resolver tests covering every precedence branch, first use, explicit overrides, project changes, missing/deleted accounts, stale policy revisions, incompatible models, and successful versus failed launches updating last-used history.
- Run Claude A and Claude B concurrently, then Codex A and Codex B; verify each process's reported identity, including profiles with the same email but different workspace identities. Refresh/re-authenticate one without altering another. Check macOS Keychain and Windows/Linux storage behavior separately.
- Confirm launch/usage/login resolve identical namespaces and inherited auth variables cannot select an unintended billing identity. Observe actual command acceptance and resulting provider identity, not only the requested environment.
- Restart UI/host, reconnect mobile, retry a timed-out launch request, and recover with one process/session per idempotency key. Show uncertain outcomes without retrying blindly.
- Delete/logout one account while sessions exist; confirm explicit handling and zero cross-account reassignment. Check exhausted quotas pause/ask rather than switch subscriptions.
- Concurrent agents editing the same project need separate worktrees or an explicit shared-workspace policy; auth isolation does not prevent file conflicts.

This revision changes sidebar navigation only. Settings forms, resolution logic, account login, process supervision, and concurrent authenticated sessions are specified, not connected or implemented. Mobile resolves through the host's policies and immutable session bindings; it does not keep an independent last-used account or replicate credentials.


## Revision v7: two-pane workspace and thread visibility

Supersedes v6 sidebar navigation: remove the entire first explorer column and sidebar toggle. The workspace is now the thread list plus the selected conversation. Put Project and Status filters at the top of the list, with a Thread view settings control. Status options: All threads, Needs attention (Needs you or Review), Running (In progress), Scheduled (has schedule), Finished (Done). Project, status, and search combine. Threads/Board/Office remain in the top header; Office remains available as a full view after removing the sidebar miniature. Quick capture and account capacity stay in the footer.

Thread view settings:
- Group by project, default on. Order projects by their newest matching thread and threads within each group by latest update. Ungrouped mode restores one recency-ordered list.
- (Removed) The Threads per project setting no longer exists; the live list is governed by the Thread list status rule at the end of this document. Expansion is temporary and resets when project/status changes.
- Hide finished threads after reading, default on. Only Done plus read-through-current-update is eligible. Keep the selected thread visible until the user leaves it. Unread completion, running, waiting for input, and review remain visible subject to filters/limits. A newer update invalidates prior read state. In this wireframe opening a Done thread marks its current update read; production must acknowledge the rendered completion event, not merely opening a thread before it finishes.

The Finished filter bypasses completed-read hiding. A visible hidden-count link also opens Finished, and disabling the setting restores hidden items. Project groups with no visible matching threads disappear from the list, but their project remains selectable in the project filter. Hiding is a presentation rule, not archiving, deleting, stopping an agent, or removing a project. The selected conversation is preserved across filter changes. Board and Office use the project/status/search filters but not the list's per-project limit or completed-read hiding.

Preferences are in-memory for this wireframe and explicitly reset on reload. Production should persist user-level view preferences and per-user read cursors (including mobile acknowledgements) separately from provider lifecycle and thread history. Completion must be a verified application event; a silent process or disconnected host must not imply Done. A finished thread with later activity reappears according to its new state/read cursor. Read acknowledgements must not update thread recency. No backend is connected in this revision.

Verified v7: workspace typecheck and product build pass. Browser verified project groups, settings dialog, a two-thread project limit and Show more, keeping a completed thread visible while selected, hiding after leaving, and retrieving it through Finished. Mobile layout and all filter combinations were not exhaustively tested. Screenshot: `docs/previews/threads-v7.png`.


### v7 refinement: project separation and older-thread discovery

Desktop project groups have 28px of top padding (14px for the first) and additional heading padding, applied inside the scroll viewport content wrapper. Folded groups reduce the top padding to 11px (7px for the first); expanding restores the larger gap. Show older threads (N) appears only when matching records fall outside the status rule (see Thread list status rule); otherwise render no older-thread control or empty-history label. There is no per-project row count and no "threads per project" setting; a previously persisted `freddai.limit` value is ignored. The same action reveals all non-live records; it does not imply remote history loading. In flat mode, history controls include their project names.


### Single new-thread entry point

Remove Quick capture and its drop target from the bottom-right footer. New thread in the top-right header is the single visible creation entry point; Cmd/Ctrl+K remains available. The new-thread dialog retains its attachment picker, and conversation file drops remain supported. The footer is dedicated to provider accounts/capacity. This supersedes prior quick-capture footer requirements.


## Revision v8: app-owned schedules and project identity

### Scheduling belongs to Freddai

Freddai owns schedule definitions, timers, due occurrences, and run history independently of provider apps, login sessions, and child processes. Logging out of Claude/Codex, changing an account, closing a conversation, or restarting a provider must not delete, reset, or transfer the schedule. Do not rely on provider-app scheduled tasks or an interactive terminal remaining open.

Persist schedule ID, project ID, prompt/attachment references, recurrence, IANA timezone, enabled state, execution-host ID, routing policy, next due time, missed-run/overlap policy, and revision. Store run records separately with occurrence ID, scheduled time, resolved account/session binding, state, attempts, and outcome. Credentials are references to the account registry, never embedded in schedules.

Routing choices: explicitly pin an agent/account, or follow the project's agent policy at dispatch. Resolve project policy on the Mac host at due time, never whichever provider happens to be signed in most recently. For projects inheriting global Prefer last used, unattended runs require an explicit stable fallback choice at schedule setup; they must not follow incidental interactive account changes. Freeze the resolved binding per run. This supersedes v6's fixed-binding-only schedule proposal while preserving its no-silent-switch rule.

If the intended account is logged out, unavailable, or exhausted, retain the occurrence as Blocked with a reason and show it in Needs attention. Allow re-authentication, an explicit reroute, retry, or skip; do not silently change provider, subscription, or billing mode. Re-authentication resumes eligibility without duplicating a started run. Editing a schedule affects future occurrences; deleting or disabling it does not implicitly kill a running agent.

A host scheduler service must run independently of the desktop UI for closed-window operation; its installation/start-on-login behavior is a future implementation choice. A sleeping/offline Mac cannot execute local agents. Default missed-run policy: report missed occurrences and ask before catch-up; optionally allow one catch-up run, never an unbounded backlog. Default overlap policy: skip a new occurrence while the previous one is running and record why. Store timezone explicitly, define DST behavior (skip nonexistent local times, run once for repeated local times), and display next-run time in the schedule's timezone.

Use durable occurrence IDs, atomic claim/lease ownership, and idempotent dispatch. Reconcile process/run identity after crashes before retrying an uncertain launch. Separate Queued, Blocked, Starting, Running, Completed, Failed, Missed, and Skipped. A provider disconnect is not completion. Mobile monitors and manages the same host-owned schedules, with cached state visibly stale when the host is unreachable. No mobile or provider login becomes the schedule owner.

Verification required before live scheduling: logout/login and account switching preserve definitions; UI/provider restart preserves due runs; two dispatch attempts do not create duplicate sessions; sleeping-host catch-up follows policy; DST behavior is deterministic; blocked authentication never runs under another account. This is spec-only: no scheduler, login integration, or background service is built in this iteration.

### Project colors and group presentation

Keep project-group top spacing, remove the border above each project name, and omit all No older threads labels. Freddai demo data includes enough older completed conversations to demonstrate the existing expansion action. Each group heading has a Project settings control with six named color choices; selection immediately updates its heading/icon, flat-list project label, and board project label. Labels remain visible so color is not the sole identifier. Project identity colors are distinct in meaning from status dots and account identity colors. Colors are in-memory in this wireframe; production stores color on the stable project ID and syncs it to mobile. No backend is connected.

## Proof-of-concept focus and implementation readiness

Confirmed product intent: unify AI coding agents behind one intuitive developer conversation interface. Users manage projects, threads, provider accounts, capacity, and Freddai-owned schedules without navigating separate provider apps. Provider processes are an execution detail; the primary experience is structured conversation and actionable requests.

For the proof of concept, focus exclusively on Threads. Board and Office are deferred and are not acceptance requirements; their existing wireframes may remain as reference but receive no implementation effort. The long-term mobile companion and cross-platform architecture remain requirements, not evidence that those clients work today.

Current readiness: the spec maps account isolation, agent routing, structured command/event transport, attachments, lifecycle, scheduling, and mobile-host boundaries to reference sources and acceptance scenarios. The current product is still a frontend-only wireframe. No live login, account switching, provider command execution, usage retrieval, persistent session recovery, or scheduler has been proven. Do not treat documented architecture or the successful frontend build as backend readiness. The prior instruction not to connect a backend remains in force until the user explicitly authorizes implementation.

Recommended first technical slice, pending scope confirmation: macOS host; Claude Code and Codex; create/select local projects, choose an explicit provider/account, launch a thread, stream structured messages and tool activity, handle questions/permissions, send follow-ups and attachments, stop/resume, and persist/reopen conversations. Validate simultaneous isolated accounts and project defaults. Capability-test provider slash commands and interactive controls rather than presenting every terminal command as a supported chat action. Unsupported commands must be explained without silent execution or dropped input.

Open product decisions before live implementation:
- Existing external CLI conversations: discover/import/resume in the first proof of concept, or start with sessions created by Freddai? Recommendation: Freddai-created sessions first, with external session import as a separate compatibility milestone.
- Parallel threads in one repository: separate Git worktrees by default, or a shared working folder? Recommendation: isolated worktrees for concurrent code-changing threads, with an explicit shared-folder option and handling for non-Git projects.

Proposed sequencing after the first live slice: real per-account usage where supported, durable app-owned scheduling, then mobile monitoring and additional providers. This sequencing is a recommendation, not a user-approved deferral of those product requirements. Board/Office are explicitly deferred by the user. Agent account switching always distinguishes new-session account selection from migration of an existing provider conversation; mid-thread migration is not assumed.

## Revision v9: authorized macOS implementation

The user has authorized native backend implementation for macOS, Claude Code, Codex, multiple Codex accounts, task-level account selection, and comprehensive keyboard operation. This supersedes the earlier frontend-only restriction. Threads are the only implemented native view. The older `App` wireframe remains in source as reference; `apps/native` now renders `DesktopApp` with the real Rust host. The browser preview displays the native layout but cannot start processes. No sample conversations or fabricated usage are inserted into the live workspace.

### Implemented architecture

- `crates/core/src/runtime.rs`: account/project registry, routing, persistent transcript store, process supervision and lifecycle. `protocol.rs`: provider request construction and explicit approval replies. The Tauri shell exposes typed commands to the local webview; no remote server is opened.
- Codex: one account-scoped app-server process per active turn, JSON-RPC initialization, thread/start or exact thread/resume, turn/start, message deltas, completed items, and turn completion. The native view renders Markdown replies and collapsible tool records. Command/file-change approval and user-input requests have explicit UI responses. Unsupported server requests receive an error, never implicit approval.
- Claude Code: CLI stream-json stdin/stdout with initialization and permission control messages, structured assistant content, can_use_tool requests, AskUserQuestion responses, result detection, and exact `--resume`. This is a direct CLI protocol adapter, **not the official Agent SDK**. Its private control surface is a compatibility risk; validate on each supported CLI release or replace behind the same adapter with a packaged official SDK bridge. Do not describe the adapter as stable just because initialization succeeds.
- Both adapters maintain a queue of permission requests and scope replies to the current request ID. Stop interrupts the owned process group and clears pending requests. Completion requires a provider completion event. An unexpected exit is Failed; crash recovery marks persisted active turns Interrupted. Quit requests stop active supervisors before exit. Freddai does not yet run agents after quitting the app.
- Claude turn completion: a `result` finishes the user's turn only after the prompt was written and assistant text or a tool call was seen for it (or the result carries text, e.g. slash commands). Results emitted for CLI-injected turns on resume (such as a stale background-shell `<task-notification>`) are ignored and logged. A second empty result, or 45s of silence after an ignored empty one, fails the thread with an explicit error instead of an empty Done. Every `result` (`ev=turn.result`) and turn end (`ev=turn.finish`) is written to `logs/preferences.log` under the app data root.
- Background commands hold the turn: a Bash `run_in_background` (or auto-backgrounded) command and a Monitor task are tracked like background sub-agents (`kind` `bash`/`monitor`, recognised from `task_started` with the launching tool call or from the "running in background with ID" tool result). A `result` while one runs keeps the Claude process and thread `running` ("Waiting on background command: <description>") until its completed/failed/stopped notification wakes the agent and it answers; Stop ends a held turn and marks the task stopped. Detached jobs (`nohup`/`&`/`disown`) are invisible to Freddai and still end the turn. Event names are inferred from the existing frame handling and the session tool result, not captured from a live `task_started` for a bash task; Freddai does not yet append any system prompt telling Claude this.
- Workspace JSON is atomically replaced in the macOS app-data directory `com.freddai.desktop`, with private directory/file modes. Provider IDs, account bindings, preferences, and transcripts are persisted. Provider stderr is not persisted because it may contain sensitive material. This JSON store is an initial implementation, not a scalable journal/database or a transactionally durable process-launch ledger.

### Account switching and defaults

An account is a stable generated profile ID with provider, user-visible name, and identity color. The user creates one profile per subscription and signs in through the provider CLI. Codex uses a distinct CODEX_HOME and explicitly selects file credential storage for both login and execution. Claude uses the same stable CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR for login and execution. Inherited API credentials, provider endpoint overrides, and alternative-cloud selectors are removed from child environments. Existing user CLI homes are never overwritten, and credentials are never imported; session transcripts there can be imported read-only (see Import sessions).

Sign-in opens an explicit provider login in Terminal; Freddai neither asks for passwords nor copies tokens through React. Check sign-in runs the provider's status command with a timeout. This proves authentication status only, not subscription identity. Re-authentication into a different subscription within a named profile is not yet detected; identity fingerprint verification is an outstanding acceptance gate. Sign-in is blocked while the profile has active turns.

New thread shows both project and agent/account. Defaults resolve explicit selection, fixed project binding, remembered project account, global last successful launch (when enabled), then configured global fallback. A fixed project policy with no usable account blocks. No silent fallback on missing authentication, quota, or provider failure. Last-used updates on accepted interactive launch, not selection or sign-in checks. A resumed thread always uses its saved profile and provider session ID; selecting a different account creates a new conversation, not migration of existing provider state.

Settings persist global Prefer last used and fallback account. Project settings persist inherit/fixed/remember-last routing plus project color. View settings are persisted in webview localStorage. Read cursors are no longer device-local: see Shared read state.

The footer retains individually colored provider icons. A dashed outline represents unknown usage and has an accessible label; it must not look like a full or empty quota gauge. Live quota/reset collection is still pending. Do not substitute token counts, estimated budgets, or invented reset times for subscription limits.

### First-slice operational limits

Start with Freddai-created sessions and explicitly chosen canonical local folders. Multiple conversations can run concurrently in the same folder. Separate Git worktrees are optional, as detailed below; importing Claude Code and Codex sessions started outside Freddai is specified under Import sessions. Permission selection is specified in the composer permission settings section below, superseding the initial fixed approval settings.

Native file selection and native drop events attach absolute local file references to the prompt. The UI says these are references and the agent may need permission to read them. Files are **not** copied into an immutable attachment store, uploaded, or encoded as image content yet. Consequently attachment accessibility, provider image handling, and missing/moved-file behavior remain acceptance gaps. `/` commands are explicitly rejected with guidance rather than silently treated as arbitrary terminal input.

Board, Office, mobile remote connection, background scheduler, auto-worktrees, account removal/export/import, quota refresh, signed distribution, and exhaustive provider-command coverage are not implemented by this slice. Prior scheduling and mobile requirements remain in force for later milestones.

### Keyboard contract

The shared `packages/app/src/desktop/shortcuts.ts` registry drives both keyboard handlers and the searchable help dialog. Command is used on macOS; Control is also accepted. Global commands are suppressed during IME composition, repeat events, conflicting modifiers, and open dialogs except the applicable attachment shortcut. Dialogs retain Escape dismissal and focus containment. No shortcut grants permission, accepts a tool action, or changes accounts silently.

| Action | Mapping |
| --- | --- |
| New thread | Cmd+N or Cmd+K |
| Search threads | Cmd+F |
| Settings | Cmd+, |
| Accounts | Cmd+Shift+A |
| Add project | Cmd+Shift+P |
| Home | Cmd+1 |
| Focus thread list | Cmd+2 |
| Focus composer | Cmd+3 |
| Needs attention filter | Cmd+Shift+I |
| Attach files to active composer | Cmd+Shift+U |
| Stop selected turn | Cmd+Shift+. |
| Searchable shortcut help | Cmd+/ |
| Send or start thread | Cmd+Enter inside composer |
| Move focus between thread rows | Up / Down / Home / End |
| Open focused thread, activate controls | Enter / Space |
| Traverse controls | Tab / Shift+Tab |
| Close modal or return to thread list | Escape |
| Select menu | Up / Down, Enter, Escape |
| Resize focused pane separator | Left / Right (component keyboard behavior) |
| Dictate into the visible composer (when voice dictation is enabled and a model is installed) | Hold the configured key (default Right Option; Right Command, Right Control, Left Option selectable in Settings > Voice) while Freddai is focused; release to transcribe; any other key, window blur, or an open dialog cancels |

Native text editing and OS shortcuts (copy, paste, select all, undo, redo, hide, minimize, quit) remain reserved. Statuses have text alternatives; colors never carry meaning alone. Visible focus, skip-to-composer link, reduced-motion support, labeled controls, and focused error/permission surfaces are required. Permission controls are keyboard reachable through Tab and standard activation, without a global one-key approval shortcut. Shortcut remapping, menu accelerator integration, VoiceOver, non-US layouts beyond the shifted-period regression, and a full 200% zoom audit remain future verification work; do not call this exhaustive accessibility compliance.

### Sources and evidence boundary

- [Orca reference repository](https://github.com/stablyai/orca), inspected at `f22231dc2959bc687ed29bbc5a7b22ec26b5d03f`: `src/main/claude/claude-stream-json-connection.ts`, `claude-agent-sdk-process-spawn.ts`, `claude-agent-sdk-contract-pins.test.ts`, and `src/main/codex/codex-structured-item-translation.ts`. Orca uses the SDK for Claude; Freddai's direct control adapter is a consciously narrower implementation, not a claim of parity.
- [Codex app-server documentation](https://developers.openai.com/codex/app-server/) plus JSON schemas generated by the installed CLI establish initialization, requests, and approval shapes.
- [Claude streaming input documentation](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode) establishes the persistent structured-input approach and recommends the SDK. Private CLI control frames were checked against Orca's contract fixtures and the installed CLI initialization response.
- [Tauri Rust command documentation](https://v2.tauri.app/develop/calling-rust/) describes the local invoke boundary.

Local compatibility checks used `codex-cli 0.165.0` and Claude Code `2.1.308`. Both accepted isolated-profile initialization without signing in or sending a model request. Scripted child-process tests cover streaming projection, explicit approval, cancellation, and completion; resolver tests cover precedence and fail-closed missing accounts; persistence tests cover interrupted recovery. These are local contract checks, not live-provider success.

Before claiming a working multi-account coding workflow: manually sign in two Codex subscriptions and one Claude subscription; verify identity per profile; complete actual edits under each account; test concurrent profiles on separate folders; resume after restart; deny and allow file/command requests; test quota/auth failure without fallback; stop a running tool; verify descendants exit on quit; and exercise attachments through the built macOS app. Native app UI automation was unavailable in this session because computer-control permission did not include Freddai. Signing/notarization and mobile device tests have not been performed.

Verification for this implementation: 11 Rust tests and 5 TypeScript/Node tests passed; workspace typecheck passed; macOS debug app bundle built. Both installed CLI initialization handshakes passed with temporary isolated profiles. Browser UI checks exercised Cmd+K, task prompt autofocus, keyboard access to the account selector, Escape dismissal, Cmd+/, and shortcut search. Screenshot: `docs/previews/desktop-keyboard-v9.png`. Vite reports a non-blocking bundle-size warning (the main bundle is approximately 621 kB uncompressed). No real subscription login, paid model turn, macOS-window interaction, or VoiceOver run was performed.

### Local signed installation, 2026-11-03

The current macOS debug bundle was signed using the existing Keychain identity `Developer ID Application: Denis Duvauchelle (BSH2UEDLU5)`, with hardened runtime, Apple timestamp, and signing identifier `com.freddai.desktop`. No new certificate was created or existing certificate revoked. Installed at `/Applications/Freddai.app`; strict recursive code-signature verification passed on the installed copy. This build has not been notarized. Future builds are not automatically signed by this one-time signing operation. Launch/window verification remains blocked by computer-control access to Freddai; the user can open the installed app directly.

## Revision v10: agent discovery and account onboarding

A fresh native workspace with no account profiles opens **Set up agents & accounts** once at startup. Users may defer setup and create project folders first. Project settings with no accounts show an actionable setup panel instead of an empty routing-policy selector. Returning from setup preserves the pending project or new-thread draft.

The setup sequence distinguishes three states: (1) CLI installed, (2) a named Freddai account signed in, (3) default account selected. The Rust discovery command checks executable files using the same resolver as process launch (Homebrew, local installation directories, and inherited PATH). Show Codex and Claude Code as Installed/Not found, with the resolved executable location under disclosure and a Scan again action. Detection neither installs software nor imports existing authentication. Browser previews cannot inspect local CLI installations and must say so.

Each detected provider offers Set up or Add another account. Creating an account collects its name and identity color, allocates the existing isolated provider profile, and opens explicit sign-in in Terminal. A second Codex subscription requires adding a second profile and signing in with the other ChatGPT account in Codex's browser flow. Profile creation is not proof of sign-in; Check sign-in verifies the selected profile. Account setup checks existing profiles when opened. Signed-in profiles expose Use as default, selecting a fixed global account and disabling global Prefer last used. Provider identity verification limitations from v9 remain: local sign-in status does not prove which subscription was selected in the browser.

Routing labels show the resolved provider and account, for example **Global default: Codex · Work subscription**. If none exists, say **No account configured**, with a setup action. Explain fixed, remembered-project, and inherited behavior inline. New-thread defaults also identify the resolved provider/account. All setup controls are keyboard reachable, errors remain in the dialog, and switching to account setup returns to the originating form without losing its fields.

Verification: executable detection regression covers files without execute permission and directories; default-label tests cover missing accounts and provider/name labels. Native provider authentication and multi-subscription identity remain manual acceptance steps, not claims established by discovery.

v10 delivery: 12 Rust tests and 6 Node tests passed, frontend/native build passed, and the updated macOS debug app was Developer ID signed and installed at `/Applications/Freddai.app`. Strict signature verification passed on the installed copy. The prior application bundle was preserved during replacement; running instances were not force-quit. Browser verification confirmed the empty-account setup action, navigation into onboarding, and restoration of the entered project name on return. Screenshot: `docs/previews/project-account-onboarding-v10.png`. The installed window remains unverified because computer-control access was denied, and no actual subscription sign-in was performed. Save any current draft, quit, and reopen Freddai to use the new bundle.

### Delivery and empty-account recovery

Standing project requirement: every application change must be rebuilt, signed, and reinstalled before delivery. `pnpm native:install` preserves the previous bundle, verifies the installed signature, and checks the running executable location. An existing process can continue executing the preserved previous bundle after replacement; installation is not relaunch. Native controls must relaunch and verify the update where permitted, without discarding drafts or interrupting active turns. If tool access is denied, explicitly surface the required user restart.

New thread now routes directly to account setup when no account profiles exist, then returns to the new-thread draft. If setup is deferred, the form's primary action is Connect an agent account instead of an unexplained disabled Start. No placeholder accounts or bypass of authentication are introduced.

Investigation on 2026-11-03 confirmed the running Freddai executable was the preserved pre-onboarding bundle, while `/Applications/Freddai.app` already contained the signed onboarding update. Saved workspace contained one project and zero accounts. This explained the immediate mismatch between delivered source/install and the user's running interface.

## Revision v11: sign-in completion and visible feedback

Confirmed report: the account was already signed in, but Check sign-in only replaced the same small status text. The external `.command` script left Terminal at Process completed and Freddai did not own its completion. The existing Codex LC profile was checked with the account-scoped CLI status command and returned signed in; no tokens were read into the UI and no model task was launched.

Codex login now runs as a supervised child process with the same isolated CODEX_HOME and file credential configuration. It opens the provider's browser login without creating an external Terminal window. After a successful process exit, Freddai independently checks that profile's authentication status before reporting Ready. Process exit alone, or credentials left from a previous failed attempt, do not establish success. Login state is queryable across closing/reopening the setup dialog. Permit only one managed sign-in at a time to avoid the provider localhost callback port conflict. Cancel and ten-minute timeout terminate the owned process; app quit requests cancellation and waits for shutdown. Launching a thread while its profile is signing in is blocked.

The account panel watches managed login progress automatically and refreshes account status when the app regains focus. Show a prominent green provider/account Ready confirmation with a continuation action. Manual checks show Checking and a last-checked timestamp, including repeat checks of an already-authenticated account. Serial checks prevent stale results overwriting more recent login progress. Do not automatically dismiss setup while users may be adding another account.

Claude retains its existing explicit Terminal login for this revision; status refresh on app focus applies to it as well. No broad Terminal settings are changed and existing completed Terminal windows from older builds are not closed automatically. The old window in the report can be closed by the user; future Codex sign-ins create no such window.

Verification: scripted process regressions cover successful exit plus verified auth, successful exit with failed verification, nonzero exit without reusing prior auth, duplicate login rejection, cancellation, and timeout. These tests do not prove a fresh live OAuth browser round trip. The existing real Codex account status was verified locally. The shared UI build passes. macOS reinstall and strict installed-signature verification are required by the standing delivery rule.


## Revision v12: integrated macOS title bar

Replace the separate macOS window-title strip with an overlay title bar and a hidden native title. Keep the system close, minimize, and fullscreen controls. The existing Freddai header occupies the top of the window in a single 46px row, with a macOS-only 86px left inset for the traffic lights. Preserve Threads, thread filtering, Settings, New thread, keyboard access, and focus indicators. The brand area and a flexible empty region support native window dragging; inputs and buttons remain outside the drag regions. Grant only the required start-dragging window permission. Browser and other-platform headers retain their normal left inset. Existing account-footer layout is a separate workstream.

Verification: workspace typechecks, six existing Node regressions, web production build, and native frontend/Rust build passed. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt, Developer ID signed, and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`; strict installed-signature verification passed. Quit the idle prior app normally through native controls and observed the replacement running, with its mapped executable verified against the installed bundle. Native UI inspection confirmed a single top bar and accessible system close/minimize/fullscreen controls. Search accepted and cleared temporary text; Settings and New thread opened and closed without changing settings or starting a turn. Header drag and resize gestures were exercised, but the available cropped-window capture did not establish window displacement or minimum-size behavior, so those remain manual acceptance checks. Vite reports the existing bundle-size warning. No account creation, sign-in, or live provider turn was used as a test fixture.


## Revision v13: account setup dialog sizing

The reported older-build screenshot showed horizontal scrolling. Current source already constrains card contents and prevents horizontal dialog scrolling, but native inspection reproduced avoidable vertical overflow at the default 1100×760 window: the narrow 512px dialog wrapped agent actions and the Ready message, placing Continue below the viewport. Give account setup its own 640px width, bounded by the viewport, and reduce its section spacing from 16px to 12px. Remove excess status-note top margins and slightly tighten the step list. Preserve all controls, wrapping long account names and paths, the single-column small-screen layout, and vertical scrolling for expanded forms, many accounts, or short windows. Other dialogs keep their current dimensions.

Native frontend/Rust build and Developer ID signing/install passed through `pnpm native:install`, including strict installed-signature verification. Native screenshot at 1100×760 confirmed the normal signed-in setup state fits with Continue visible, both agent buttons on one line, and neither horizontal nor vertical scrollbars. Expanded forms, many accounts, and minimum-size-window behavior remain unverified. No account creation, sign-in, or live provider turn was performed.


## Revision v14: account usage overview before management

Footer account buttons and Accounts & usage open a read-only overview of every configured account. The account shortcut opens the same overview. List account name, provider, global default, provider-reported plan when available, each quota window's percent used, reset countdown plus local reset date/time and timezone, and last update time. Refresh retries metadata queries; individual errors do not hide other accounts. Missing values remain unavailable, never zero or fabricated. Past reset timestamps ask for refresh instead of implying renewed capacity. Manage accounts opens the existing setup/management UI and returns to the overview on completion/close. Existing project/new-thread/settings setup return paths and empty-account onboarding remain intact.

Codex usage uses the installed CLI's documented `initialize` / `initialized` / `account/rateLimits/read` exchange with the existing isolated account profile and credential isolation. No model prompt, thread, login, reset-credit redemption, or account mutation is requested. Each read is bounded to fifteen seconds with child-process cleanup; stderr and provider error details are not surfaced. Normalize all returned buckets with the older single-bucket fallback, preserving unknown resets and zero usage. Claude Code usage reporting is not integrated yet and is labeled unavailable, including its reset time. Reference: https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server#6-rate-limits-chatgpt .

Footer account buttons use independent sizing classes, fixing the earlier 36px icon-class collision. Account buttons can scroll within their own row while the overview and shortcut controls remain visible.
