## Existing local CLI sign-ins (2026-11-03)

Agent setup checks the default local Codex and Claude Code configuration, or their inherited CODEX_HOME / CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR override, using only the provider's status command. Use existing sign-in explicitly registers a shared local connection after a fresh status check, without running login, copying credentials, or changing the CLI configuration directory permissions. The resolved canonical configuration path is persisted on that connection. Status, usage, launch, and resume resolve the same path; shared Codex connections respect the CLI's configured credential storage instead of forcing Freddai's file-storage setting. Connecting the same provider/path again returns the existing connection. A failed check never creates a connected profile. Discovery does not search arbitrary custom directories or infer browser-only authentication.

Shared connections follow whichever account is currently signed into that CLI configuration. The UI explains this behavior. They have no Sign in again control, and the backend rejects login requests for them. Manage their authentication in the CLI; Add new / Add another account retains the existing isolated profile and explicit sign-in flow. Legacy profiles deserialize with no shared path and preserve their isolated behavior. No provider identity or subscription claim is inferred from a successful status exit.

## Desktop execution expansion, November 3, 2026

This revision supersedes earlier milestone statements that worktrees, attachments, usage readers, background execution, schedules, and additional providers were only wireframes. Mobile, Board, and Office remain deferred. Desktop implementation is macOS-first; Windows host transport is still pending.

### Accounts, quotas, and resets

The footer displays a provider icon inside a circular capacity meter, with the account's stable identity color and a numeric percentage **left**. The limiting reported window determines the ring; the overview shows every returned window with a remaining-capacity circle, percent left, and reset countdown/date/time. Amber means little capacity remains. Unknown is explicitly unavailable; stale values say last known. Crossing a reset timestamp does not fabricate replenished capacity. Footer metadata refresh runs every two minutes; the overview offers an explicit refresh. No automatic account switching or usage-credit purchase occurs.

Codex uses the existing isolated/shared CLI configuration and documented `account/rateLimits/read`. Claude Code uses its local `/usage` command in structured stream-json mode and parses `usage_report.rate_limits.limits`, retaining server scope labels and reset timestamps. This requires a recent CLI exposing that report; unavailable reports fail explicitly. It never substitutes session token counts for subscription allowance. Gemini and Grok adapters currently have no supported quota readers and display unknown usage/reset values. No direct scraping of subscription dashboards or secret/private quota endpoints is used.

Sources: [Codex app-server](https://developers.openai.com/codex/app-server/), [Claude structured usage report](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkusagereport), [Claude local command output](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdklocalcommandoutputmessage). Provider reads and real subscription values require separate live validation; fixture success is not that validation.

### Shared folders and optional parallel work

**Worktrees are off by default.** New thread includes a keyboard-accessible toggle and persists the last selection globally immediately. Shared-folder execution allows multiple conversations to run concurrently in that canonical folder, without an opt-in setting. Each conversation still permits only one active turn; additional messages use its queue. Selecting a worktree creates an app-managed Git worktree on a unique `freddai/<thread UUID>` branch from the current committed HEAD; uncommitted files are not copied. Non-Git/unborn repositories receive an actionable error only when worktree mode is requested. Separate worktrees optionally isolate concurrent threads in the same project. Model processes receive the actual selected working directory.

The conversation indicates worktree use and exposes removal only when idle. Removal uses Git's normal safety checks and refuses uncommitted/untracked changes; it never uses force and retains the Git branch. The conversation is archived after removal and cannot resume into a deleted directory. There is no automatic merge, commit, dependency installation, or cleanup of user work. Conversations using a shared folder see the same files and may edit them concurrently.

Verification (2026-11-03): the offline runtime regression reproduces the former folder rejection and now accepts new and moved conversations alongside an active conversation, while rejecting a duplicate turn in the same conversation. Workspace Rust tests, app logic tests, and typechecking passed. The macOS app was rebuilt, Developer ID signed, installed, and relaunched; the installed signed-host startup check passed. The existing background host retained an active user turn, so activation of the new backend and simultaneous live-provider execution remain unverified until that turn finishes and Freddai is reopened.

### Real attachments and agent controls

Native file picker and drag/drop copy files into private app-owned storage, recording filename, MIME type, size, and SHA-256. Limits: 20 files/message, 25 MB/file, 100 MB/message. The original file can be moved or changed after attaching; the retained copy is immutable by contract and checked before delivery. If the retained bytes change, sending fails rather than silently substituting content. Asynchronous attachment completion is scoped to its composer; switching conversations cannot route it to another conversation. Metadata chips appear in the transcript.

Images use Codex local-image input, Claude base64 image blocks, or negotiated ACP image blocks. Text up to 1 MB is embedded as reference content. Claude PDF input uses document blocks. Other files use a path to the retained copy for provider file tools, subject to provider access approval. These are copied files, not merely references to the original path. There is no automatic upload to a separate Freddai service. Abandoned staged copies are retained; storage garbage collection is a future maintenance task.

New thread and the reply composer expose a Model and effort menu beside Attach. Codex model choices and per-model effort options come from account-scoped, bounded, paginated `model/list` metadata requests without starting a thread or turn. Claude offers Sonnet, Opus, Haiku, Fable aliases, custom model IDs and low/medium/high/xhigh/max effort. Gemini/Grok accept custom model IDs and hide unsupported effort controls. Defaults defer to the CLI; unavailable catalogs leave default/custom controls usable with a retry hint. Incompatible IDs/settings produce provider errors rather than silent substitutions. Replies may update model/effort for their next turn while preserving account, provider session, working directory, and schedule flags; legacy callers retain saved settings unless they explicitly request a control update. Draft selections are scoped to each conversation. Changing the new-thread provider clears incompatible selections. Stop and explicit permission/input controls remain. Fork creates an editable new-thread draft containing up to the last 24,000 characters of textual conversation context, with a fresh account choice. It is a contextual handoff, not a provider-native history clone; attachment bytes are not automatically forwarded to a different account. No arbitrary terminal slash command is forwarded as a model task.

Keyboard additions: Command-Shift-S opens Schedules; Command-Shift-F forks the selected conversation. Existing Command-N/K, Command-Enter, Command-Shift-U, Command-Shift-Period, pane navigation, menus, and dialog controls remain. Fork requires review and Start; keyboard actions never approve agent tools automatically.

### Background host and recovery

The macOS UI connects over an owner-only Unix socket to a detached Rust host. A filesystem lock ensures one host owns the workspace. The protocol is versioned, requests are bounded, and the host binary is copied by content hash so application replacement does not invalidate a running executable. Closing the UI leaves agents and schedules running. Settings can stop all active agents and opt into a per-user LaunchAgent for the next login; enabling it does not change agent permissions. The launch entry is refreshed to the latest host binary on app startup.

On reopening, the UI reconnects. An idle older host can upgrade; active work blocks replacement, and the UI explains that the host update is pending. A pipe-lease watchdog terminates each supervised agent process group when the host dies unexpectedly. Persisted in-flight sessions become Interrupted on host restart; pending approval cards are cleared and never replayed automatically. The user inspects project changes before explicitly resuming the same provider session. Unsupported provider resume is an error with a Fork alternative. Client launch request IDs prevent a timed-out retry from creating a second turn. The host restarts on GUI reconnect; automatic daemon restart while the GUI remains closed is not implemented.

### App-owned schedules

Schedules live in Freddai's persisted workspace, independent of provider sign-in state. The UI includes create/edit, pause/enable, Run now, next occurrence, and run history with links to generated conversations. Each schedule pins an account or follows project routing with an explicitly stored fallback. A project-fixed missing account fails closed. Scheduled runs never change interactive last-used account preferences or the remembered interactive worktree toggle.

Use five-field cron and an IANA timezone. Numeric weekdays follow ordinary cron: Sunday 0/7, Monday 1, Saturday 6. Day-of-month and weekday cannot both be constrained in this first implementation. Minimum interval is one minute. Repeated local wall-clock minutes are deduplicated for automatic runs. A run over one minute late is recorded missed; there is no catch-up burst. Overlapping occurrences are skipped. Busy shared folders and unavailable accounts fail explicitly. Run now is a one-off occurrence and does not enable a paused schedule. Provider permissions still wait for the user.

An occurrence is persisted before dispatch. Recovery links an already-created session by occurrence request ID; an uncertain dispatch without a matching session is shown as uncertain and is not automatically retried. Schedules run only while the host is running and the Mac is awake/logged in. Mobile remote monitoring and remote approvals remain a later milestone.

### Additional providers and capability boundaries

Gemini CLI and Grok Build have ACP v1 adapters with structured messages, tool activity, one-time permission replies, stop, and exact-session resume only when the agent advertises support. The client advertises no filesystem/terminal delegation it does not implement. Unknown interaction requests fail closed; approvals never silently become permanent grants. Installed CLI detection, named account creation, isolated profile paths, and explicit existing-login connections include both providers.

Gemini uses `GEMINI_CLI_HOME`, with `.gemini` beneath each account's directory, and `gemini --acp`. Its initial sign-in uses the CLI's Google login interface. Setup labels cached OAuth material as “Credentials saved”; it is not proof that a token remains valid. The session authenticates through ACP. Gemini remains experimental and requires a compatible installed CLI. Grok uses `GROK_HOME`, browser OAuth sign-in, and `grok agent --no-leader stdio`; disabling leader reuse prevents a shared agent daemon from undermining per-profile isolation. Its cached-token ACP handshake verifies authentication without a model turn. Neither provider is automatically installed or signed in by Freddai.

Sources: [Gemini ACP](https://geminicli.com/docs/cli/acp-mode/), [Gemini profile isolation](https://geminicli.com/docs/cli/enterprise/#user-isolation-in-shared-environments), [Grok settings/profile directory](https://docs.x.ai/build/settings), [Grok ACP](https://docs.x.ai/build/cli/headless-scripting), [ACP initialization](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/initialization), [ACP session setup](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/session-setup). [Orca](https://github.com/stablyai/orca) remains the architectural reference from the original spec; this expansion uses provider protocols directly and does not embed or scrape a terminal UI.

### Verification and remaining limits

Checks cover routing and legacy data compatibility, protocol fixtures for all four providers, copied attachment delivery after deleting the original file, scoped permission decisions, cancellation, worktree refusal on dirty data, quota parsing, schedule crash reconciliation and missed occurrences, idempotency, standard weekday semantics, host exclusivity/persistence across process death, and watchdog termination when its pipe closes. No real accounts, credentials, subscriptions, or model turns are test fixtures. End-to-end login, live quotas, real-provider turns, macOS login-start behavior, and native drag/drop remain separate user-machine validation boundaries. Gemini is not installed on the development Mac; Grok is installed but was not signed in or used for a model task during this implementation.

Delivery verification for this expansion: 35 core unit/protocol tests plus two host/watchdog process tests passed (37 total; two helper entry-point tests are intentionally ignored in the parent runner and exercised as child processes). Six desktop shortcut/routing regressions passed. Workspace typechecks, Next.js production build, and native production frontend build passed. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the macOS executable, signed it with the existing Developer ID, installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`, and verified the installed signature. The existing Vite chunk-size advisory and website SDK-disabled/no-credentials message remain.

The **installed signed executable** also passed a headless smoke test in a temporary empty workspace: host startup, socket IPC, actual file staging, preference persistence across host restart, idle shutdown, and watchdog cleanup. Its bytes matched the built signed bundle. Browser review confirmed the new Schedules entry, background settings, four provider sections, and worktree-off New thread controls. No user workspace data, accounts, login, or model turns were used in these checks.

Native-app inspection/control was denied in this thread. The installer confirmed that the already-running GUI still maps an older preserved bundle. The new GUI was therefore not relaunched or tested natively. Required user action: save any draft, quit Freddai normally, and reopen `/Applications/Freddai.app`. Live-provider quotas, real attachments received by a provider, actual account switching, background operation after GUI quit, and login-start behavior remain unverified at the user-visible boundary.

### Header dormouse integration, November 3, 2026

The native `DesktopApp` now shows the selected v3 dormouse in a 68 px circle at the window's top center, inspired by the supplied Muse screenshot. Existing search, schedules, settings and New thread controls remain; below 1000 px they occupy a second header row to avoid overlap.

`Dormouse.tsx` loads the locally bundled `assets/brand/dormouse/freddai-dormouse.riv`, Rive Canvas runtime and both WASM variants. The asset is copied from the verified local Rive build. No CDN fetch is needed. Tauri CSP permits local fetch and WebAssembly compilation. The owning native startup cause is unconfirmed, so installed Rive playback is not verified. No account was created or signed in and no live provider task was run for this change.


## Native launch crash correction, November 3, 2026

The repeated native launch abort was traced to the detached host cache. Copying only the bundle-signed Mach-O discarded the signed Info.plist context. `codesign --verify --strict` rejected that file with “invalid Info.plist,” and macOS AMFI killed it before host startup. The GUI setup hook then panicked because the host never became ready.

macOS now caches the complete signed app bundle in a content-addressed `.app` directory, verifies its signature, and atomically publishes the copy before launching its executable with `--host`. It does not re-sign or weaken the copied signature. Unbundled development executables retain the existing standalone cache path. Host build identity uses the full executable path, including the content-addressed bundle directory, so different versions remain distinguishable and active-host upgrade deferral remains intact. Previous cache entries are preserved.

Finder-launched GUI stderr is saved locally to `~/Library/Logs/Freddai/native.log`, created with mode 0600, with one previous log rotated after 1 MiB at startup. Host startup stderr inherits that destination, and an early child exit reports its status. No credentials, account sign-in, or model turns are used as crash-test fixtures.

Regression: `cargo test -p freddai-native --lib cached_signed_host_starts -- --ignored --nocapture` requires the installed Developer ID signed bundle. It exercises the actual cache function, signature verification, cache reuse, host launch, and version/build IPC in an empty temporary workspace. The original implementation failed signature verification; the bundle-preserving implementation passed startup. Final delivery verification is recorded below after rebuild and native launch.

Final verification: 35 core tests and two host/watchdog integration tests passed; eight desktop regressions passed; workspace TypeScript checks and `cargo check --workspace` passed. The signed-cache regression was explicitly run against the final installed signed bundle and passed, including cache reuse and the full executable build identity. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the native frontend and Rust executable, signed with the existing Developer ID, verified the signature, and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`. The frontend's existing chunk-size advisory remains.

Native launch was performed through the approved app controls. The new GUI displayed the desktop, and a subsequent accessibility read returned the running UI beyond the earlier 8–12 second crash window. `lsof` verified the GUI maps `/Applications/Freddai.app/Contents/MacOS/freddai-native` and the host maps the new content-addressed `.app` cache. The installed executable's SHA-256 matched the signed build (`a8bbb16a9b7895af45418f778785f97fbc47a8e794fdd420840fc43bc784b39c`). The app was left running. No sign-in, account creation, or agent turn was initiated for verification; live-provider behavior was not validated.


## Connection reuse and removal, November 3, 2026

Account setup presents **Add current connection** when an installed provider has a detected CLI sign-in. This action revalidates and connects the current CLI home without opening login or copying credentials. The connected account follows CLI account changes. **Add a separate account** explicitly creates an isolated profile and starts provider login for a different subscription. Detection errors remain visible; they do not imply a signed-in account. No connection is created merely by opening setup.

Each connection has **Remove**, followed by an inline explanation and confirmation. Removal is refused while the connection has an active turn or tracked pending sign-in. It removes the registry entry and clears its global default/last-used and remembered project selections. CLI credentials, isolated profile files, conversation transcripts, and schedule definitions remain intact. Pinned project, session, and schedule bindings retain the missing ID and fail closed without switching accounts. Historical conversations show Connection removed and direct the user to Fork thread to continue under an explicitly chosen connection.

Verification: removal regressions cover persistence, retained profile files/history/schedules, default clearing, blocked resume/routing, and refusal during active work. Native delivery and UI verification results follow after installation.

Delivery verification: 37 core tests and two host/watchdog process tests passed, along with eight desktop regressions and workspace TypeScript checks. The signed-host cache regression passed against the first installed build in this change. The final `pnpm native:install` rebuilt frontend/Rust, signed using the existing Developer ID, verified the installed signature, and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`. The existing Vite bundle-size advisory remains. Installed and built executable SHA-256 values matched (`c6965ebc3953ae4657a3b501558a54f3bc5307690bcd47ca43005bf011604d0f`).

The final GUI was launched through native controls and its mapped executable was verified at `/Applications/Freddai.app/Contents/MacOS/freddai-native`. A user-started agent turn was active on the previous host, so Freddai correctly deferred host replacement and displayed the pending-host-update notice. That work was preserved. Finish the active turn, then quit and reopen Freddai to activate the new host-side removal command. Live connection/removal and provider login were not exercised, and the account-management controls were not fully inspected natively in this run. No actual account or credential was created, removed, or used as a test fixture.

### Native conversation density (November 3, 2026)

One-to-one native chat omits visible user and provider sender labels, retaining accessible message labels and distinct user bubbles. Freddai notices remain identified. Transcript items use a 12 px gap. Consecutive tool records share one collapsed “Tool activity” group with a call count. Tool groups and individual calls are borderless, with compact disclosure padding and 80% opacity; hover or keyboard focus restores full opacity. Disclosures retain a 24px minimum height and visible focus outlines. Expanding it reveals individually expandable, numbered calls with the original content and order. User, assistant, and notice messages end a group; stable group and call IDs preserve expansion during streaming updates. Tool details, attachments, and approval controls remain available.

### Native account footer reset visibility (November 3, 2026)

Each footer connection is a content-width, borderless button with two lines: connection name, then “32% left - 4d until reset” (using the reported remaining allowance and a compact countdown). Unavailable usage or reset values use “??”; when both are unavailable the second line is simply “??”. Full explanations remain in the tooltip, accessible label, and overview. The separate Accounts & usage footer button is removed when connections exist; each connection still opens the overview, and empty-account Connect accounts remains. The footer is 42px high with 34px connection buttons, 26px capacity rings, and tightly spaced two-line labels. It has no vertical scrolling; extra connections remain horizontally scrollable. The countdown uses the same most-used provider window as the remaining percentage and updates every 30 seconds. Missing reset timestamps display “Reset unavailable”; stale values are marked last known. Full window details remain available on hover and in account overview.


## Banked free reset balances and expiry, November 3, 2026

The Accounts & usage overview includes a **Banked free resets** section for each Codex connection. The existing read-only `account/rateLimits/read` request supplies `rateLimitResetCredits.availableCount` and optional detail rows. Preserve the authoritative count even if the service caps the details. Show available reset titles and local expiry dates with year/timezone, sorted by earliest expiry. Explicit `expiresAt: null` means Does not expire; an omitted or invalid expiry means Expiry date unavailable. A past date is labeled Expired without inventing a new balance. Missing summaries mean unavailable, never zero. Count-only and partial responses explain missing expiry details. Credits-only responses remain displayable when quota windows are absent. Other providers retain their current quota behavior.

Following the user's clarification, available reset detail rows also include **Use reset**, then an explicit confirmation for that account and reset. Confirming calls `account/rateLimitResetCredit/consume` with that exact credit ID and a generated idempotency key. Retries within the open overview reuse the key after an uncertain error; the selected credit ID never falls back to a different reset. Definitive outcomes distinguish reset, already redeemed, nothing to reset, and no credit. Then refetch usage and notify the footer to refresh its rings; never infer a replenished limit from the redemption response. Expired rows are disabled. A pending host update alone does not disable reset actions; an unsupported host command explains how to restart. Usage timestamps use the device’s current local timezone, named beside the last-updated time. Count-only responses remain visible with expiry details unavailable; redemption requires a returned reset ID.

Existing per-account sign-in isolation and manual refresh are retained. No reset is redeemed automatically, and provider login, account creation, live model turns, and real redemption are excluded from implementation test fixtures.

Sources: [official app-server rate-limit response](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server#6-rate-limits-chatgpt) and the installed `codex-cli 0.165.0` generated `GetAccountRateLimitsResponse` schema. ### Native composer and message queue (November 3, 2026)

App/background build mismatches do not display a global restart warning or Show active threads action. Existing compatibility guards remain in effect. Account reset guidance explains restarting locally in the account view without referring to a global banner. Do not claim a version mismatch proves work is still running.

The thread list's unified Show older threads action is a discreet secondary control: 11px, regular weight, and muted text, with brighter hover/focus feedback. Show older threads, Show more, and Hide older threads span the full navigation row so their hover background and click target extend across the available width, while text stays aligned with thread titles. Preserve keyboard focus, hidden-row count, and project/status/search filters.

Enter sends an idle conversation message; during an active turn or with an existing queue it appends to that conversation’s FIFO queue. Command/Ctrl+Enter explicitly queues; Shift+Enter inserts a newline. The composer includes an active-turn Stop control; there is no separate Queue button. Stop cancels the turn and pauses pending messages. A visible queue panel shows pending prompts and attachment counts, pause/resume, individual deletion, and clear queue. Pausing affects future dispatch, not an active turn.

Queues persist in the local host workspace and run without the window open. Each entry retains its attachment IDs, model/effort settings, and stable request ID. Dispatch uses the existing session binding and launch validation; failures retain the message with an actionable paused error. Failed/interrupted turns pause pending work. Restarted hosts retain enabled queues after successfully completed turns; interrupted/failed threads and explicit pauses/errors remain paused for review. Previously accepted request IDs prevent replay. Live provider execution and visual verification require the updated app and host to be running.

The installed signed-host cache/startup regression passed in an empty temporary workspace. Installed executable SHA-256 matched the final signed bundle (`cb39aa675c48c196886c55e9b39087fc172544ee405ae7a9f035ba33080ac507`). The existing Vite chunk-size advisory remains. A transient native rustdoc dependency failure during overlapping earlier builds passed when the full suite was rerun sequentially.

## Inline thread renaming

Double-click a thread name in the left thread list or conversation heading to replace it with a focused input and select its existing name. Keyboard users can focus the name and press F2, Enter, or Space. Enter or blur saves a trimmed, nonempty name; Escape cancels; blank or unchanged input leaves the original name. Save failures retain the input and display an actionable error. Both locations read the persisted session title. Renaming changes only the title, preserving activity timestamps, read state, provider sessions, messages, and active work.

Reference inspected: Orca `src/renderer/src/components/sidebar/WorktreeTitleInlineRename.tsx` at `a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d`. Adapted the focus/select and trimmed-name commit behavior conceptually; Freddai uses its existing Tauri/local-host persistence boundary. No implementation code copied.

Verification: workspace typechecks and 13 desktop tests passed; native build, Developer ID signing, strict signature validation, and installation passed. Relaunched the idle app through native controls and confirmed its mapped executable is under `/Applications/Freddai.app`. In the existing conversation, scrolling upward exposed “Scroll to latest message”; clicking it returned to the bottom, removed the button, and focused the transcript. No live provider turn was sent. Streaming, resize, and VoiceOver behavior were not separately exercised.

### Composer permission settings

The conversation composer and new-thread dialog expose a keyboard-accessible Permissions dropdown alongside model controls. Codex and Claude offer Ask for approval and Bypass permissions. New drafts default to Bypass permissions, as requested by Denis. Existing stored explicit choices are restored per thread. Changing the selector applies to the next sent or queued message, never the currently running turn. Queued messages retain their own chosen mode.

Codex Ask uses workspace-write with on-request approvals; Bypass uses danger-full-access with never approvals on both thread/start and exact thread/resume. Claude uses default or bypassPermissions via --permission-mode. Provider restrictions may still reject bypass. Gemini/Grok show a disabled agent-default selector; the host rejects explicit bypass for these adapters. Questions and unexpected provider requests retain existing explicit response handling. Legacy stored options and schedules with no mode preserve previous provider defaults. No account or live provider turn is used as a test fixture.

### Composer context and cache status (November 3, 2026)

A compact context ring above the composer shows capacity left for the last provider-reported request, with an explicit “Context …% left” label. Codex `thread/tokenUsage/updated` is scoped to the provider thread; context occupancy uses `tokenUsage.last.totalTokens / modelContextWindow`, not cumulative billing totals. The cache summary label is omitted when expiry is unknown; current provider events contain no expiry timestamp or guaranteed retention lifetime. Raw cached token counts remain in the details as last-request reuse, never as a cache-lifetime signal. The desktop usage row shares the composer’s centered 800px maximum width and responsive gutters. Unavailable context/cache fields are omitted, including their ring or divider; when neither is available, the entire row is hidden. Valid zero context values remain visible. Expanded details omit unavailable counts. A miss never implies caching is disabled. Details expose raw token counts and clarify that unsent drafts are excluded. Usage persists per conversation, and older history remains unknown until a new usage event arrives. Claude assistant-message usage exposes cache reads and token counts, including cache creation and reads in its total input; its context percentage remains unavailable without a reported window size. Gemini/Grok currently omit these indicators because usage is unavailable. No cache toggle or inferred model limit is introduced.

Protocol evidence: official app-server docs https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server and installed CLI generated `ThreadTokenUsageUpdatedNotification` schema; Claude usage field semantics https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/typescript/messages/create . Live-provider validation requires an explicitly authorized turn; no account or live turn is a test fixture.

## Thread context actions, November 3, 2026

Thread rows support right-click, the Context Menu key, and Shift+F10. Their menu provides **Review decision…**, **Stop**, **Resume…**, **Archive / Unarchive**, **Change project…**, and **Delete…**. Availability follows the actual thread state, connection availability, and pending host updates. Opening the menu does not select another thread or clear the current composer draft.

Review decision opens the clicked thread's current request with its full request context and existing approve/decline or answer controls. Opening review never approves anything. Replies carry the exact thread and request ID and rely on the existing stale-request guard. Resume opens an editable message, defaults to Continue where you left off, and requires explicit submission before launching the saved provider session with its original account and agent settings. Existing queues are paused before this explicit continuation. Stop uses the existing cancellation path.

Archive and Delete require an idle thread and pause its queue. Delete is recoverable metadata, not file deletion: retain transcript, staged attachments, worktree, provider conversation and schedule history. The Archived and Deleted status filters expose hidden threads; Deleted offers Restore thread. Restoring or unarchiving does not launch an agent or resume its queue. Archived/deleted threads cannot launch or queue messages until restored/unarchived. Archive metadata is separate from the legacy archived status for a removed worktree, which remains non-resumable.

Change project regroups an idle thread. It records the original execution project and preserves account, provider session, working directory, model/effort/permissions, attachments and queue. Resumes continue using the original execution project. Worktree removal also uses the original repository. The dialog explains this behavior before confirmation; it does not move files or migrate a provider conversation to a different folder.

Regression coverage includes metadata persistence, history and provider identity retention, active-work refusal, archive/delete launch and queue guards, restore recovery, shared-folder concurrency after moving and duplicate-turn rejection, legacy metadata compatibility, and menu state/visibility rules. Native and browser verification results follow after final checks and installation.

Installed-app verification: `pnpm native:install` completed the native frontend and Rust build, Developer ID signing, strict signature validation, and installation. The idle prior instance was quit normally and the installed app reopened through native controls. A concurrent workstream subsequently replaced and relaunched the bundle; the final installed bundle and build bundle hashes match, its strict signature validates, and the running UI executable maps to `/Applications/Freddai.app`. Native UI verified dedicated Agents & defaults, Thread display, Projects, individual project detail, and Schedules pages, preserved project controls, heading focus, and Escape from project detail to Projects. A native screenshot confirmed sidebar layout and active-section highlighting at 1100×760. Source inspection confirms the conversation remains mounted; draft preservation was not exercised with temporary text because concurrent native UI interaction made that check unreliable. Narrow-window behavior, VoiceOver, account setup navigation in the new shell, and live-provider execution were not separately exercised. The existing Vite bundle-size warning remains.

### Antigravity CLI migration (2026-11-03)

New Google-provider setup now offers Antigravity CLI (`agy`) instead of Gemini CLI. Install via `brew install --cask antigravity-cli`; start `agy` to sign in. If Gemini was installed with Homebrew, `brew uninstall gemini-cli` removes that executable without deleting its configuration. Freddai never installs, uninstalls, migrates credentials, or signs in automatically.

Antigravity is a distinct provider with `agy acp`, ACP v1 messages, scoped one-time permission replies, cancellation, and capability-gated exact-session resume. Cached credentials are reused directly; background turns never send `authenticate` to open browser login. Accounts use the shared CLI/Keychain sign-in, with no invented home/profile isolation. Creating a separate Antigravity login is rejected. Connecting the current CLI sign-in explicitly verifies it via ACP initialize/session-new without sending a prompt; checking a connected account does the same and may create an empty provider session. Initial discovery does not check credentials or create a session. CLI help must expose ACP before a connection can be used; the initial agy 1.1.11 did not expose it; a later local update to 1.5.0 exposed ACP and was verified using help only.

Existing Gemini account IDs, credentials, threads, schedules, and resume IDs are preserved under the legacy adapter. They are not relabeled as Antigravity or silently moved across providers. Accounts lists legacy Gemini only when existing records require it. Antigravity model controls use default/custom model IDs applied via the advertised model config option via `session/set_config_option` before the prompt, and no effort selector; quotas remain unavailable. Tests use synthetic protocol fixtures only. Live Keychain login, subscription access, and a real Antigravity model turn remain unverified.

References: [Homebrew CLI cask](https://formulae.brew.sh/cask/antigravity-cli), [Google ACP integration and authentication](https://antigravity.google/docs/ide/extensions/acp), [Google sign-in and Keychain storage](https://antigravity.google/docs/install/).

### iPhone companion — first implementation (November 3, 2026)

Confirmed scope: a developer away from the desk can browse the Mac’s projects and threads, filter/search/sort, inspect conversations and progress, send follow-up direction, and launch new threads. The iPhone has no provider setup, account management, project configuration, model/permission settings, or office editor. Execution and configuration stay on the Mac; new work initially selects the project’s default assistant and allows choosing another assistant already configured on the Mac. Initial navigation is Threads / Projects, with full-screen conversations and a project, coding assistant and prompt composer. Filters: project, all/needs-you/running/finished; sorting: latest activity, oldest activity, title.

Implemented foundation: `packages/app/src/mobile/MobileApp.tsx`, a separate touch interface with safe-area spacing, 44px controls, Markdown conversations, thread-specific drafts, connection/error states, polling cancellation, and stable request IDs for unchanged retries. `companion.ts` defines the narrow injectable snapshot/start/reply boundary. The snapshot excludes account records, local project paths and configuration. Read/write capabilities must be supplied by the Mac. Waiting threads requiring a provider approval must disable ordinary replies until a dedicated decision flow is implemented. Replies to running threads must queue on the Mac with existing FIFO semantics. Sending is never treated as acknowledged before the client resolves; disconnect disables writes and retains loaded history/drafts in memory. Drafts do not yet survive process restart. No fabricated accounts or demo work are added.

Preview: product Vite URL with `?companion=1`. This is an explicitly disconnected interface preview, not a functioning remote client or installed iPhone app. Desktop entry and narrow desktop windows continue using DesktopApp; resizing never swaps the runtime or loses active drafts. No endpoint, network listener, pairing secret, or provider call is added. Native iOS project generation, iOS-safe host startup, authenticated Mac/phone transport, background/foreground reconnect, secure device association and simulator/device verification remain outstanding. The current Tauri host starts a local desktop process and is not yet iOS-safe; its existing mobile entry annotation alone does not establish mobile support.

Orca reference inspected at `a29d2e3f7f25e5f867fee9f0f0d2b94f7a1a961d`: `src/main/runtime/pairing-endpoint.ts` (reachable endpoint normalization and local-only defaults) and `src/main/runtime/mobile-cloud-relay/relay-pairing-authorization.ts` (authorization derived from trusted connection metadata). Adapt the separation of mobile presentation and authenticated host execution; defer endpoint/device association rather than expose the desktop RPC surface. No Orca code copied. Freddai deliberately limits mobile scope to the companion flows above.

### Sidebar read and finished lifecycle (2026-11-03)

Completed replies with no read timestamp for the latest update appear as Unread reply and match Needs attention. Finished contains completed replies read through their latest update. Opening a completed conversation while the app is focused marks it read; returning focus or visibility also performs this check, with settings/action dialogs excluded. A completed thread offers Mark as read in its context menu. With Hide completed threads after reading enabled, a selected read conversation stays visible until leaving it, then remains available through Finished. Projects remain visible when all their matching threads are finished. Each project has one Show older threads action with the total number of hidden matching rows, including every row outside the status rule. Clicking it expands all those rows in place, preserving project/status/search filters and active rows. Hide older threads collapses back to the normal list. Grouped and ungrouped layouts use the same discreet per-project control; there is no separate finished-history action. New replies become unread again; running, pending-input, failed, and interrupted states retain their runtime meanings. No provider state or conversation history is modified by read tracking.

### Native iPhone installation and Mac connection (November 3, 2026)

This implementation supersedes the disconnected-only milestone above. The user explicitly authorized building, signing and installing on the paired iPhone. Physical target: DiPhone, iPhone 16 Pro Max, iOS 27.2, Developer Mode enabled. Identifier: `com.freddai.companion`, with the existing Apple Development identity/team. The separate iOS Vite entry bundles only the companion UI. The native Rust library selects an iOS-only client instead of the desktop process host; the core process runtime is excluded from iOS dependencies. Generated Xcode project and local-network permission metadata are present.

Connection: a dedicated signed Mac listener on HTTPS port 49744 forwards only snapshot/start/reply/respond operations through the existing local host socket. A private local CA validates the Mac hostname; the phone trusts only that CA and sends its dedicated random bearer credential. Redirects are disabled. The Mac compares authorization in constant time and excludes accounts, provider credential directories, project filesystem paths and desktop configuration from the companion snapshot. Server-side route allowlisting, request-size bounds, and serialized dispatch restrict the surface. No generic desktop RPC endpoint is exposed. Private keys and provisioning data live outside the repository with private filesystem permissions. The installer provisions this phone directly; there is no phone setup UI. This first delivery supported a reachable Mac on the local network only (superseded: remote access now uses the encrypted relay, and localhost preview traffic uses the relay tunnel; see "Companion preview tunnel"). Push notifications and remote wake were not part of it. Certificate renewal and a multi-device credential registry remain follow-up work.

New work resolves the Mac's existing project/global account routing and worktree default; it does not fabricate accounts or change provider configuration. Follow-ups use the persistent host FIFO with request IDs. Waiting threads expose the existing shared question/permission renderer. An answer targets the precise JSON request ID and the host revalidates that it is still current; no viewing or polling approves anything. Unsupported/resume-less conversations remain unavailable for ordinary replies. Archived/deleted threads are excluded. Polling recovers after network failure while retaining in-memory transcripts; native requests have timeouts. Per-thread drafts persist in app-local storage; stable request IDs cover unchanged in-process retries. Exactly-once recovery across app termination is not yet guaranteed. Conversation scrolling follows near the bottom and exposes Jump to latest when reading earlier content. Queue paused/error state is visible.

Mac lifecycle: the listener runs separately from the desktop GUI and agent host, with an exclusive process lock. Normal Mac startup ensures the signed cached binary can launch the listener. Installation can start it without quitting active GUI sessions or provider work. The already-running desktop UI may still require a normal user restart after bundle replacement; listener verification is reported separately from GUI relaunch.

Repeatable path: `pnpm ios:install` invokes `scripts/install-ios.sh`; the installer uses `pnpm native:install` for macOS, builds/signs an iOS archive with automatic Xcode development provisioning, installs with `xcrun devicectl`, and launches through approved device controls. A locked phone is an explicit launch blocker, never bypassed. Development builds are personal device installs, not an App Store/TestFlight distribution release.

## New-thread selection preference (November 2026)

This revision supersedes v6's mandatory project-first precedence for interactive new threads. Settings → Agents & defaults provides three strategies: Last selected across projects; Project preferences; Global default everywhere. An explicit composer connection always wins. Last selected uses the latest explicit agent/account selection in the new-thread composer, persisted on the host immediately without launching a provider; it takes precedence over project policies. With no remembered selection, resolve project policy, then the global fallback. Project preferences uses the project's fixed account or project last-successful account, then the global fallback. Global default everywhere ignores project policies. A fixed project policy with no account remains unresolved; missing or unavailable configured connections block launch rather than silently switching subscriptions.

Persist strategy and selected account across restart. Do not infer selection from browsing threads, replies, resumed conversations, probes, or scheduled work. Existing data without a strategy retains its previous project-first behavior, including the legacy prefer-last-successful fallback, until the user changes the strategy. Removing a connection clears the new last-selected pointer; existing pinned-route deletion behavior is preserved. Model and effort controls reset across provider changes. Existing threads retain their stored binding; schedules retain their existing explicit/project dispatch behavior. The composer shows the resolved connection and its source; an explicit composer selection remains when the project changes. Project settings explain when global strategy overrides them. No account or provider traffic is needed to configure or test precedence.

Each verifies the unique action, additive reveal beyond the five-row limit, independent older-thread reveal, collapse and reopen. The fixture never creates accounts or launches a turn. Signing, installation, relaunch, native interaction verification and live-provider validation were not completed for this change.

## Automatic thread titles

New native-runtime threads receive a compact local title from the opening prompt (conversational request prefixes removed, first nonempty line, at most eight words / 56 characters plus ellipsis), rather than the previous raw 100-character prompt excerpt. This immediate fallback does not perform semantic AI summarization. Codex `thread/name/updated` and ACP `session_info_update` titles replace automatic names when emitted by the active provider session. For a new automatically named Claude thread, Freddai requests `generate_session_title` with `persist: false` through the existing control stream. This uses Claude’s tool-free title helper, independently of the working turn, and may consume provider usage. Unsupported CLI versions or missing responses retain the local fallback. Claude `session_title_changed` metadata is also accepted.

Codex app-server never emits `thread/name/updated` for Freddai-started threads (confirmed: 50 Codex threads, none present in `~/.codex/session_index.jsonl`), so Codex threads are named by Freddai. When a Codex thread's first turn completes successfully, and the thread has exactly one user message, is not manually renamed and still carries the local prompt-derived title, Freddai starts a detached, throwaway `codex app-server` process (same account configuration) and runs an `ephemeral` thread there: read-only sandbox, approval policy `never`, `low` reasoning effort, prompt = first user message plus the first 400 characters of the first assistant reply, asking for a 3 to 6 word title. The side thread never touches the user's connection or transcript and does not delay the turn. The result is sanitised (first line, quotes/markdown/trailing punctuation removed, at most eight words), applied as an automatic title, and written to the real Codex thread with `thread/resume` + `thread/name/set`. It uses a small amount of provider usage. Outcomes are logged to native.log as `ev=thread_title.requested`, `thread_title.applied`, `thread_title.skipped reason=...` and `thread_title.failed reason=...` for Codex, Claude and ACP paths; failures keep the local fallback title but are never silent.

A manual rename persists ownership and permanently blocks automatic title updates, including late provider events. Legacy custom names are preserved; legacy names matching the original prompt excerpt remain eligible for provider updates. Title updates persist without changing messages, execution status, or activity ordering. No historical threads are rewritten on load, and naming never starts another working agent turn or grants tools to the title helper.

Legacy history lacks message timestamps: initialize its send cursor once from existing recency on host load, preserve it across restarts, and replace it on the next user send. Row activity timestamps and unread behavior remain unchanged.

### Native window placement (November 2026)

### Thread list ordering, active list, and older-thread paging (November 3, 2026)

- Order everywhere (active and older lists) is newest user message first (`last_user_message_at`, falling back to `updated` for legacy records), ties by id. Assistant output, status changes, and read state never reorder the list. Queued messages stamp `last_user_message_at` when the user queues them; dispatching from the queue does not bump it.
- Per project, the live list follows the Thread list status rule (end of document): working, pending/queued, needs-you, unread, unviewed-failed, and the selected thread; no row-count cap. A read completed thread leaves the live list as soon as another thread is selected or a new thread is started. Selecting a thread marks it read. The hide-read preference and the done/failed/archived/deleted filters keep their semantics.
- "Show older threads (N)" reveals the next 10 older threads for that project; the button then reads "Show 10 more (N)" (or "Show K more" when fewer remain) and pages by 10 per click. "Hide older threads" collapses back. Page counts are per project and reset with the list view settings. Never more than the requested pages are rendered.
- Revealed older threads are grouped by local calendar day of the last user message. Today has no header; then "Yesterday"; then a short muted uppercase date such as "Mon, Oct 27" (year added when not the current year). Logic: `dayBucketLabel` / `groupThreadsByDay` in `packages/app/src/desktop/types.ts`.
- Rows show the agent icon (account color) left of the title and the status dot at the right end; the relative-time label is removed. No Claude/OpenAI logo assets exist in the repo, so the existing lucide icons are kept. Project headings have no gap above their first row and extra top padding above every project after the first.

### iPhone thread-list overflow correction (November 3, 2026)

Reproduced the actual MobileApp with a synthetic long spaced title, an unbroken title, and a long project name. At 320px, the thread rows expanded to roughly 8,130px and moved the date offscreen, even though the title already declared `text-overflow: ellipsis`. The cause was Radix ScrollArea’s intrinsic-width table wrapper measuring the full text width. A one-variable browser probe changing only the wrapper to a width-constrained block restored row width and title truncation; the remaining metadata overflow required a separate constraint.

Fix: scope the same block-content sizing pattern already used by desktop to the companion body viewport. Clip the row’s shrinking text column, and render project/status metadata as a single ellipsized line. Titles and project names expose their full value through the standard title attribute and remain intact in the DOM for assistive technology. Date/status placement, filtering, sorting, conversation state and scroll ownership are unchanged. No global ScrollArea behavior is replaced.

Regression: `packages/app/tests/thread-overflow.browser.mjs` renders the actual MobileApp plus the desktop ScrollArea/ThreadTitle composition using synthetic data with no accounts, credentials, native bridge or providers. The check failed before the fix and now passes at 320/390/430px on mobile and 240/320/500px on desktop. It asserts bounded viewport/row width, actual title overflow within an ellipsis box, visible dates and desktop icons, and project-name truncation. The fixture is separate from application build entry points; execution instructions are in `packages/app/tests/README.md`. Screenshot: `output/verification/thread-overflow-fixed-390.png`. Prevent recurrence by testing the intrinsic scroll-content wrapper alongside row truncation, rather than checking only that an ellipsis declaration exists.

Reference: Orca’s `src/renderer/src/components/activity/activity-thread-list-pane.tsx` at the existing inspected baseline was checked for the list containment boundary. Freddai’s confirmed failure occurs in the shared Radix scroll wrapper; adapt the existing local desktop constraint rather than copy Orca’s virtualized activity list. No Orca implementation copied.

## Git history view (per project)

- Each project row in the left navigation has a branch-icon button ("Git history for <project>") between the project name and the "+" new-thread button. It does not select or toggle the row; it sets the main-pane selection to `{ kind: "git", projectId }` and shows `aria-pressed` while active. Selecting a thread, or opening the new-thread pane, returns the main pane to normal.
- The pane (`GitHistoryPanel`) is read-only: header with project, branch and ahead/behind of the upstream ("No upstream" when none); "Uncommitted changes (N files)" grouped Conflicts / Staged / Unstaged / Untracked with M/A/D/R/C/T/?? badges (renames show `old → new`); GitGraph-style table (up to 300 topologically ordered commits across local and remote refs, colored parent lanes and merge edges, branch/tag badges, description/date/author/hash columns); branch selector and search that dims nonmatches without breaking graph continuity; working-tree changes collapsed by default; refresh button. No commit/push/stage actions.
- Backend: Tauri command `git_history(path)` (`apps/native/src-tauri/src/git_history.rs`) runs `git status --porcelain=v1 -z`, `branch --show-current`, `rev-parse @{u}`, `rev-list --left-right --count @{u}...HEAD` and `log --all HEAD --topo-order -n 300` with full hashes, parent hashes, and decorations (unborn repositories omit HEAD) with `--no-optional-locks`. Any git failure (not a repository, missing folder, unparseable output) is returned as an error string containing git's stderr and rendered in a visible alert; there is no silent empty state.
- Verification (November 4, 2026): workspace typechecks passed; four frontend Git tests (including merge-lane convergence) and four Rust Git parser/error tests passed. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the macOS app, signed with the existing Developer ID, installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`, and verified the installed signature. Approved native controls are unavailable (`orca: command not found`), so relaunch and native visual verification remain pending: save drafts, quit normally, and reopen `/Applications/Freddai.app`. No live-provider turns, sign-ins, or account mutations were used.

## Sign-in timeout recovery — November 4, 2026

Thread authentication preflight waits up to ten seconds for the Codex `login status` or Claude Code `auth status` command. A timeout means the CLI did not respond; it does not establish that credentials expired. New timeout errors identify the provider, explain the unknown status, and direct users to Accounts to check the connection, or update/check the provider CLI in Terminal if the problem persists. Failed desktop threads expose an Open Accounts button for sign-in errors. Persisted legacy `Sign-in check timed out` messages receive the explanation and recovery action too. Opening Accounts preserves the selected thread and messages; retrying remains an explicit user action. The underlying cause of this user's CLI delay is unconfirmed; no live account or credential was inspected.

### Delete stalled sessions (2026-11-04)

Delete is available from the desktop thread context menu for starting, running, waiting, failed, and idle sessions. Confirmation moves the thread to Deleted immediately, clears pending decisions, marks it interrupted, and pauses its queue. Persistence must succeed before cancellation is sent. An existing worker receives cancellation and its supervisor reaps the process group in the background; missing or disconnected workers do not prevent deletion. History, files, and provider identity remain available for restoration. Restore and resume remain gated while the previous worker is still registered, preventing overlapping turns. Archive and move retain their active-work guards.

Status and actions keep their existing behavior.

## Legacy active-host thread sorting compatibility

Desktop and iPhone normalize snapshots before displaying threads. A background host from before send timestamps may remain active to preserve agent work. When its snapshots omit `last_user_message_at`, each client freezes the initial activity time and advances that cursor only when the latest user-message ID changes. Assistant output, title/status updates and reloads retain the cursor. Versioned local storage contains only thread/message IDs and times; storage failure retains in-memory behavior. An authoritative host send timestamp always takes precedence. Historical send times cannot be reconstructed from legacy messages without timestamps; initial legacy ordering is preserved until a subsequent user message is observed. Queue acceptance timing remains authoritative only on upgraded hosts; old-host clients observe the user message when dispatched.

## iPhone navigation simplification (November 4, 2026)

The iPhone opens directly into the project-grouped Threads list, without the bottom Threads/Projects navigation or a separate Projects screen. ## Split view, November 4, 2026

The conversation area can show two threads side by side. A Split view icon button in the conversation header (accessible name "Split view" / "Close split view", tooltip with shortcut) and Cmd+\ (Ctrl+\ elsewhere; no existing shortcut used it) toggle it; it is listed in the Cmd+/ help. Enabling split keeps the current thread in the left pane and focuses an empty right pane ("Select a thread from the list"); no thread is auto-picked. Panes are two resizable panels (default 50/50, keyboard-resizable like other separators), each rendering the full conversation (transcript, composer, agent controls). The focused pane has a ring on its header and `aria-current`; clicking or focusing inside a pane focuses it, and choosing a thread in the list opens it in the focused pane. A thread open in the other pane is never duplicated: selecting it moves focus there. Each pane has a close control; closing either pane leaves split mode and the remaining pane's thread becomes the single selected thread. Toggling split off keeps the focused pane's thread. The list marks the focused thread with the primary selection style and the other open thread with a muted style and a screen-reader description.

Reply drafts and attachments are stored per thread (not one shared composer), and are dropped once a thread is in no pane, as switching threads always did. Reply permission and model/effort were already per thread. Staged attachments return to the thread that requested them, even if another pane is focused; if that thread is no longer open they are discarded with the existing notice. The window file-drop target and the Cmd+Shift+U shortcut apply to the focused pane. "Copying attachments" disables Send in both panes. At the 720px breakpoint the toggle is hidden and only the focused pane renders. Split on/off and the right pane's thread id persist in browser/webview storage (`freddai.split`, like other view preferences); the left thread is not persisted, so after a restart the left pane is empty. Board and Office are unchanged. Pure pane logic lives in `packages/app/src/desktop/split-view.ts`.

## Revision: approved iPhone pairing and encrypted remote transport (2026-11-04)

This revision supersedes the earlier installation-shared token and LAN-only transport descriptions. The Mac GUI adds Settings → Phone pairing: Pair phone, cancel/deny, explicit approval, device list, per-device revoke, and HTTPS relay configuration. Pairing generates a random six-digit code, expires after 180 seconds and allows five attempts across the entire offer. Only one request can claim an offer; approval opens a 60-second retrieval window. The phone supplies a separate 256-bit claim secret so knowing a request identifier cannot retrieve its credential. A successful retrieval consumes the offer. Cancelling or revoking a pending/approved request removes its retrieval path. All pairing exchanges use the installer-provisioned Mac's pinned local TLS authority; pairing never traverses the relay. The code is not a persistent authentication credential.

Each phone receives separate random 256-bit device and mailbox secrets and an opaque device identifier. iOS stores its credential and relay address in Keychain. A new iOS build embeds only the Mac endpoint and public trust certificate, never the old installation token. The Mac keeps a locked, atomically updated, mode-0600 registry inside its mode-0700 companion directory. Existing shared-token installations must pair through the new UI. Revocation is checked for LAN requests and again immediately before remote dispatch. Up to sixteen approved devices are supported; device discovery across Macs and certificate renewal are still follow-up work.

Remote transport uses an independently hosted ephemeral HTTPS mailbox relay. The personal deployment is `https://freddai-companion-relay-production.up.railway.app`, Railway project `freddai-companion-relay`, project ID `03d65cae-53b4-487c-be47-a93597a7f34e`, service ID `7e42bb44-c91e-4edd-82f4-5c420b505e27`, one replica, target port 8787. It uses the existing Railway workspace. This address is saved on this Mac. Changing it requires one local snapshot on a paired phone to refresh its stored address. Relay configuration can also be saved through the signed binary's `--companion-relay <root> <https-origin>` CLI. HTTPS origin validation rejects credentials, paths, queries, fragments and plaintext HTTP.

The relay authenticates random mailbox capabilities whose hashes identify mailboxes. It receives no end-to-end keys. AES-256-GCM envelopes bind direction, device and request identity as authenticated associated data and use fresh OS-generated nonces. The encrypted request includes its ID, operation, parameters and short expiry. The Mac allows only snapshot/start/reply/respond, enforces expiry and keeps durable pre-dispatch mutation receipts to reject uncertain replays across listener restart. In-memory response caching permits retransmission without redispatch. Server-side existing start/reply idempotency semantics remain in place. No generic desktop RPC or shell is exposed. Pairing grants, provider credentials and accounts never reach the relay. The relay can observe sizes/timing/mailbox identities and deny service; no availability or forward-secrecy claims are made.

The phone probes local snapshots, then falls back to the relay when local access fails. Following remote selection, writes go directly to the relay. It does not automatically retry an uncertain local mutation over the second transport. Sends time out with an instruction to check the conversation before retrying. The Mac must remain awake, online and logged in, with its listener running. Sleep/host-unavailable/revoked-device cases do not imply a completed agent turn. Remote wake, push notifications and queued offline user actions are not implemented. Phone drafts retain their existing local persistence.

Relay messages expire after 120 seconds and memory is swept every ten seconds, with 64 MiB retained storage, bounded mailbox/queue counts, per-request size limits, eight concurrent body uploads, a 32 MiB global body-buffer cap and request-rate limits. It is single-replica, memory-only; restart loses in-flight messages. Its container uses a non-root user. HTTPS termination is provided by Railway. `services/relay/README.md` documents deployment, limits, configuration and synthetic checks. The installer refreshes only the standalone listener with normal termination, preserving the GUI, independent provider supervisor and ongoing agent work.

Verification: workspace TypeScript checks passed. macOS and aarch64 iOS Rust checks passed. Native unit checks (15 passed, one unrelated signed-host check ignored) cover a synthetic host snapshot, expired/unknown/revoked remote routes, durable replay receipts, pairing approval, single use, wrong claims, five-attempt lockout, expiry/cancel/revoke, credential-free status output, authenticated encryption tampering/direction/keys and HTTPS origin validation. Relay unit checks cover authorization, ciphertext-only storage, mailbox isolation and retry handling. The public relay passed an authenticated encrypted synthetic request/response round trip. Browser pairing checks passed for invalid input, incorrect code, waiting for Mac approval, successful transition, Mac approval and revocation; screenshot `output/verification/companion-pairing-iphone.png`. These checks use synthetic boundaries, not real accounts, connected-device credentials or provider turns.

Build/sign/install: `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the macOS bundle, signed with the existing Developer ID and verified installation in `/Applications/Freddai.app`. The separate signed listener was refreshed and its mapped executable verified at the installed path. The iOS archive built, passed signature verification, installed on DiPhone and launched through `devicectl`; its process executable matches the newly installed container `2A9F0013-DB23-46EB-BFBC-9F8EC341DA61`. Native GUI control was unavailable (`orca: command not found`); the Mac window remains an older mapped bundle and was preserved. Save drafts, quit Freddai normally, and reopen `/Applications/Freddai.app` before using new Mac settings. Actual phone pairing, Keychain persistence across physical relaunch, changing Wi-Fi/cellular networks, real pending approval responses and live provider turns have not been acceptance-tested. No new account, sign-in or provider turn was used as a test fixture.

### Companion preview tunnel (2026-11-09)

A paired phone or iPad can open a web app running on the Mac at `http://localhost:<port>` inside the companion app, on Wi-Fi or cellular. Agents share a localhost link in a thread message or through the `freddai_share_preview` MCP tool; mobile shows a Preview chip that calls `preview_open`. The Mac proxies only to `127.0.0.1:<port>`, and only for a paired device, a port of 1024 or above that is not a Freddai port, and a port shared in that thread or via the MCP tool (in-memory grant, 12 h expiry). Traffic is multiplexed as frames over a relay WebSocket tunnel (`/v1/tunnels/<mailbox>/<session>?role=mac|device`, bearer-authenticated with the mailbox capability); the relay forwards binary frames only, never sees plaintext, and bounds sessions, idle time and frame size. The phone serves the page to a native full-screen web view through a loopback server inside the app. Failures are named (`preview_not_granted`, `preview_port_unreachable`, `preview_tunnel_unavailable`). Android native plumbing exists but is not installed or verified. Details, wire format and limits: [docs/preview-tunnel.md](preview-tunnel.md).

### Permission display and companion defaults (2026-11-04)

Existing conversations with an absent/null permission mode use the host's Ask default; both the desktop selector and follow-up submission must show/preserve Ask rather than advertising Full access. New phone conversations explicitly choose Bypass for Codex/Claude, matching the desktop new-thread default; unsupported providers keep their existing default. Phone follow-ups preserve the saved conversation mode. Do not migrate existing threads to Bypass or approve pending requests implicitly. Permission changes apply to the next submitted message.

Approval cards lead with the human reason, action label, command when available, and working directory. Raw provider JSON remains available in collapsed technical details. Allow once and Decline stay explicit and request scoped. User-input requests retain their required-answer validation.

### Immediate send and decision feedback — November 4, 2026

Desktop replies now clear the submitted composer and attachments immediately and display a local user message with Sending status before IPC completes. Acknowledgement changes that status to Sent/syncing until the host transcript or queue confirms it; stale snapshots retain the preview. Queue confirmation uses the exact request ID, and transcript reconciliation counts matching user messages so repeated prompts are not mistaken for earlier messages. Rejection removes the preview and restores the prompt and attachments while retaining any newer draft. Existing request IDs and host queue ordering remain authoritative. New desktop thread launch retains its existing immediate starting preview and preserves the draft pane until a real session ID is returned.

Mobile clears the composer on submission while retaining its existing immediate message preview, allows drafting the next message during the request, and restores rejected text ahead of any newer draft. Sending another message still waits for the pending submission to finish. Both clients show submitted decisions immediately through the shared permission renderer: Allowed once, Declined or Answers submitted, with Sending/Waiting status. Duplicate submission is blocked locally. Failed decisions restore controls and entered answers with an error; stale snapshots of the same decision cannot re-enable submission. A new request resets the decision state. These are local optimistic states, not claims that a provider has accepted a turn.
