## Desktop rendering performance (2026-11-04)

Streaming fix delivery (November 4): desktop incremental polling now retries the existing idle-upgrade handshake when a host lacks `snapshot_since` or reports another executable build. Attempts are serialized and limited to once every five seconds; a busy host's refusal preserves its agents and the full-snapshot compatibility fallback. Host startup verifies the expected executable rather than accepting any responsive host. Claude launches request `--include-partial-messages` (confirmed in installed CLI help); text stream events update the same message-id/block-index keys as final assistant blocks, so final text replaces the partial text without duplication. Thinking, tool JSON and nested subagent partial events are not rendered as assistant text. The desktop's 350 ms polling interval is unchanged; this is not an Orca-style terminal push rewrite.

Streaming verification: the Claude subprocess regression failed before the fix with absent text and passes after it, asserting visible partial text while the fixture is paused for permission, plus one final `Hello world` message. The legacy-host socket regression failed before the fix with no upgrade request and passes after it, including busy refusal and retry throttling. Workspace typechecks, 86 frontend logic tests, 15 Vitest tests, 75 core tests, 24 native tests and two host integration tests passed. The separate installed-signed-host regression passed a synthetic idle legacy-host takeover with 45 sessions and no connected accounts: initial reply 16,225,172 bytes; unchanged reply 278 bytes in 0.34 ms (JSON result sizes, excluding the RPC wrapper). This measures host RPC, not provider speed or UI paint. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt macOS, signed with Denis Duvauchelle's existing Developer ID (BSH2UEDLU5), verified the strict signature and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`. Native GUI controls are unavailable (`orca` absent), and the installer confirms the running window/agent host retain older bundles. Save drafts, let active work finish, quit Freddai normally and reopen `/Applications/Freddai.app`. Running-window and live-provider verification remain pending; no real agent turn, account creation, sign-in or forced termination was used.

Runtime audit (November 4): the current detached host rejects `snapshot_since`, forcing the native compatibility fallback to full snapshots (15,203,122 bytes / 45 sessions; one read measured 1450.85 ms). Its executable hash differs from the installed app. Existing timing logs show median host RPC 1308.26 ms and median refresh start interval 1977.0 ms. Current-source incremental snapshots must not be treated as active-host verification. Orca's inspected terminal path pushes batched output with a 2 ms batching constant and explicit backpressure; it does not repeatedly transport the workspace for each terminal update. Claude partial text events are also unhandled in current source. Exact ten-second-per-word behavior remains un-reproduced; no live provider turn or process mutation was used. See [streaming investigation](streaming-investigation-2026-11-04.md) for evidence, owning files and verification limits. This audit makes no application change.

Performance-only change; behavior is unchanged. (1) React Compiler (`babel-plugin-react-compiler`, enabled through `@vitejs/plugin-react` in `apps/native/vite.config.ts`, so it also compiles the source-imported `packages/app`) memoizes components; components using unsupported syntax (`try/finally`, `??=`/`||=`, including `DesktopApp` itself) are skipped silently and run as before. (2) Reply draft text lives in an external store (`packages/app/src/desktop/drafts.ts`, `useSyncExternalStore`), so a keystroke re-renders only that thread's composer textarea and send button, not `DesktopApp`; split panes keep independent drafts, drafts are still in-memory only and dropped when a thread leaves every pane. Attachments, permission and agent selections remain in `DesktopApp` state. (3) `acceptSnapshot` passes each polled snapshot through `reconcileSnapshot` (`reconcileSnapshot.ts`), which keeps the previous object for every unchanged session, message, project and account (arrays of `{id}` items match by id) and returns the previous snapshot itself when nothing changed, so an idle 350 ms poll causes no re-render; `visibleSnapshot`/`db` are memoized and `trackThreadRecency` reuses results for unchanged threads. (4) `MessageMarkdown` splits message text into blocks at blank lines outside code fences (`markdown-blocks.ts`; loose lists, reference/footnote definitions and raw HTML stay in one block) and renders each block through a memoized component with module-level `components`/`remarkPlugins`, so streaming re-parses only the changed last block. Tests: `packages/app/tests/render-perf.test.mjs`.

Streaming latency (2026-11-04): (5) Host persistence is debounced. A streamed message delta only mutates memory, bumps a non-persisted revision and marks the database dirty; a background persister clones the database under the lock (about 1 ms for a 13 MB workspace, versus about 9 ms to serialize it) and serializes and writes `workspace.json` (same pretty format, tmp + rename) with the lock released, at most every 750 ms. Writes are still immediate and synchronous for status or provider-id changes, every explicit user mutation, host upgrade (`upgrade_idle`) and SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP; a host crash can lose at most about 750 ms of streamed text. (6) The desktop poll uses the host RPC `snapshot_since {revision, hostId}`: an unchanged host answers `{revision, hostId, unchanged: true}` (the UI does nothing); otherwise it sends `sessionIds` for every session in order, bodies only for sessions changed since that revision, and every other Database field in full. Revision 0, another host boot id or a future revision returns `full: true`. `applySnapshotDelta.ts` merges the reply, then `reconcileSnapshot` runs as before; polls and post-mutation `refresh()` calls are serialized. The native layer makes one socket call per poll (`hello` only runs when the host is unreachable) and falls back to the full `snapshot` against an older host. The `snapshot` RPC is unchanged for other clients (companion server). Tests: Rust `snapshot_since_is_incremental_and_host_scoped` and persister tests in `crates/core/src/runtime.rs`; `packages/app/tests/apply-snapshot-delta.test.mjs`.

## Screen grab (2026-11-04)

A camera icon button ("Screen grab", same tooltip) sits next to Schedules in the desktop header. It opens a menu with "Attach to current thread" (disabled when no thread is selected or the new-thread composer is open) and "Start new thread". In the browser build the button is disabled with the tooltip "Screen grab is only available in the desktop app".

After a choice the native command `capture_screenshot` runs `/usr/sbin/screencapture -i -x` into a private temp directory (drag a region; Space toggles window mode). Nothing is hidden or minimized. The PNG is staged through the same host `stage_attachments` import used by the file picker and drag-drop (so it is copied and de-duplicated by SHA-256), then the temp directory is deleted whether or not staging succeeded.

- Esc, or no/empty file: cancel. Nothing happens, no error, and "Start new thread" does not open a composer.
- Failure (non-zero exit, a non-PNG result, spawn error, or Screen Recording permission missing per `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess`, which also triggers the system prompt): the command returns an error that names the cause and says to grant Screen Recording in System Settings > Privacy & Security, shown in the existing red error banner. The host also logs a structured JSON event on stderr (`screen_grab.captured`, `.cancelled`, `.failed`, `.stage_failed`); the UI logs `screen_grab.failed` to the console.
- "Attach to current thread" adds the image to the selected (focused-pane) thread's own draft attachments and focuses that composer. If that thread closed during the capture, a notice says to attach again.
- "Start new thread" opens the new-thread composer the same way New thread does (selected project context), attaches the image to it, and focuses it. Nothing is sent automatically.

## iPhone send visibility and refresh latency — November 4, 2026

Mobile renders the submitted user message immediately with Sending status while dispatch is unresolved. Acknowledged messages remain locally visible as Queued on your Mac until the authoritative transcript includes them; a queue acknowledgement does not imply provider execution. Stale snapshots retain previews. Matching-user-message ordinals reconcile repeated identical prompts separately without duplicate rendering. Rejected submissions remove their preview and preserve the draft. New-thread prompts remain visible through dispatch and the gap before the returned thread appears in snapshots. These local previews last for the mounted app session. Foreground snapshot polling waits one second after each request completes, background polling waits three seconds, and returning to visibility triggers an immediate refresh. Native transport and provider latency still contribute to actual update timing.

### Thread list status rule (supersedes the per-project row limit)

Each project's live (top) section shows a thread when ANY holds, using the same fields as the row status dot (`threadIsLive` in `packages/app/src/desktop/types.ts`, shared by Mac and iPhone):

- status is starting, running or waiting (agent working or a turn waiting to start);
- it has a pending approval/input request (`pending`);
- it has queued follow-up messages (Mac: `queues[session].messages`; iPhone: `queued`);
- it is an unread completed reply (`done` with read cursor older than `updated`);
- it failed and its latest update has not been viewed (`failed` with read cursor older than `updated`; opening a failed thread now records the read cursor like a completed one);
- (iPhone only) it is the currently selected/open thread. The Mac list ignores selection when bucketing, so opening a thread never moves it between buckets.

Everything else (read completed, viewed failed, interrupted, idle) is non-live. The Mac list is two buckets, one after the other, never interleaved (`bucketThreads` in `packages/app/src/desktop/types.ts`): the Active bucket (the live rows above, recency order), then an unlabeled continuation of the same list (same row styling, no heading or gap), holding the N most recent non-live threads in recency order (setting "Recent finished threads to keep visible", `freddai.recentFinished`, integer 0-50, default 5; `splitOlderThreads`/`sanitizeRecentCount`). Grouped mode applies N per project; flat mode (group-by-project off) has one Recent bucket of the N most recent across all projects, and the older buttons page only what remains after that global N. The rest sit under Show older threads (N), which excludes Recent rows and does not render when nothing remains; pagination by 10 is unchanged. Selection never changes a row's bucket: a selected Recent thread stays in Recent; a selected thread that would be hidden under older is appended to the end of Recent so the open thread stays visible. An unread thread opens, becomes read, and moves from Active to Recent (intended). Setting 0 puts all non-live rows under Show older threads (plus the selected one). iPhone uses `splitOlderThreads` with the fixed default of 5 and still treats the selected thread as live. With "Hide completed threads after reading" off, or a Done, Failed, archived or deleted filter, all matching threads form one list in the Active bucket.

### iPhone connection grace period

After a successful companion snapshot, a failed snapshot starts a 60-second grace period. During that interval the last snapshot and connected status remain visible, connection errors and the pairing button remain hidden, and existing actions remain available (requests still require actual transport success and retain their existing error handling). Success cancels the pending disconnect. Repeated failures, refresh, and foreground retries do not extend the deadline; a timer expires even while another snapshot is pending. At expiry the phone shows Mac disconnected and the latest snapshot error. Initial network/protocol failures receive the same grace period; pairing/authentication failures appear immediately. Unmount cancels the timer. Polling, transport fallback, message delivery, and draft persistence are unchanged.

Verification: source implemented in the mobile connection hook and wired into snapshot polling. All 15 Vitest tests and workspace TypeScript checks pass; regression reproduces immediate disconnect with the former behavior and covers recovery, deadline, repeated failures, initial failure and timer cleanup. Build/sign/install/launch results recorded below after delivery. No live provider turns used.

When red a dismissible, non-modal alert banner appears under the header ("Can't reach your Mac — retrying…" or "This iPhone is no longer paired with your Mac" with Pair iPhone and Refresh); it clears itself on green and returns only on the next green-to-red transition. ## Shared read state (November 4, 2026)

Read state is stored on the Mac host, not per device. Each session carries `read_at` (the thread `updated` value the user has seen; absent until first read) in workspace.json and in both the full and incremental host snapshots. The cursor is monotonic: host ops `mark_read {thread, at}` and `merge_read {cursors: {id: at}}` set `read_at = max(existing, at)` and never move it backwards; unknown threads are reported, not created. Only sessions whose cursor changed are marked changed for delta snapshots, and `updated` is untouched (read acknowledgements do not alter recency).

The desktop thread list derives unread/live status from the snapshot's `read_at` plus a short in-memory optimistic overlay, and calls `mark_read` when a completed or failed thread is viewed (focused window) or "Mark as read" is chosen. The iPhone companion receives `read_at` per thread in `/v1/snapshot`, derives status the same way, and calls the new allowlisted `/v1/read` route (`companion_mark_read` / `companion_merge_read`; thread+at or a cursors map, max 5000) when a conversation is opened. Because both clients share the same cursor, a thread read on one device collapses under "Show older threads" on the other after the next snapshot.

Migration: on first start with host support, each client sends its legacy localStorage map (`freddai.read` on desktop, `freddai.companion.read` on iPhone) via `merge_read` and removes the key only after the host accepted it. Failures are logged with `console.error("[read-sync] ...")` including thread id and error; the key is kept and migration retries on the next start. A host started by an older build rejects the new ops with "Unknown host command"; that error is logged loudly and clears after the host restarts on the new build. Verification status: source/unit tests only until the app is rebuilt, installed and the running host restarted.

### iPhone native thread drawer (November 4, 2026)

In the native shell the thread list is a native drawer instead of a web screen. `DrawerContainerViewController` uses the reveal pattern: the drawer (90% of the screen wide) sits underneath, and the content (navigation controller with web view and composer) slides right with the finger up to 0.9 x screen width, leaving 10% of the thread visible at the right edge under a light dim (alpha up to 0.15) with a soft shadow on the content's left edge. The drawer has a parallax, moving from -30% of its width to 0 as progress goes to 1.

- Gestures: a `UIScreenEdgePanGestureRecognizer` (left edge) opens; a pan on the drawer and on the dim layer closes; a tap on the dim sliver closes. Progress follows `translation.x / drawerWidth` 1:1, clamped at 0, with light rubber-banding past 1. On release the final position is projected (`x + velocity * 0.2`) and settles to the nearer end with a spring (`UIViewPropertyAnimator`, damping 0.9) seeded with the gesture velocity. Grabbing mid-animation stops it at its on-screen position and continues from there. A light haptic fires when it settles open. The web view's scroll pan waits for the edge gesture to fail, so scrolling and text selection away from the edge are unaffected; the web left-edge swipe-back is disabled in native mode. The nav bar's left button is a `sidebar.left` icon that opens the drawer. VoiceOver: the open drawer is modal, content is hidden from accessibility, and the escape gesture closes it.
- Contents (`ThreadDrawerViewController`, `UICollectionView` list, `.sidebar`): header with mascot, "Freddai", connection icon (green/red/gray, same as `set_connection`) and a New thread button; a search field (200 ms native debounce, event `search`) and a filter button with a project and status `UIMenu` (event `setFilter`, same options as the web selects); per-project sections with a `+` (event `newThread`), rows with a 1-2 line title and a status indicator in a fixed 20pt leading slot centred on the title's first line (8pt dot / ~12pt spinner; read rows keep an empty slot), a subtle 9pt-radius selection fill, (spinner for running/starting, orange dot for waiting/interrupted, red for failed, blue for unread, none for read); a "Show older threads (N)" row per project (event `showOlder`); an empty state. Tapping a row emits `selectThread` and closes the drawer. Updates use `NSDiffableDataSourceSnapshot` keyed by thread id with item reconfiguration, so polling does not flicker or reset scroll.
- Data flow: JS stays the source of truth. `packages/app/src/mobile/native-threads.ts` builds a compact model from the existing `selectThreads` / `groupCompanionThreads` logic and `set_threads` pushes it only when the serialized model changes (the snapshot polls every second). Native events map to the same state setters the web list used (query, project/status filter, older pages, selected thread, start thread). Read-state storage is unchanged; opening a thread still marks it read.
- Modes: `set_mode` takes `conversation | new | other` and an optional `openDrawer`. On launch (first successful snapshot) the app opens the last-viewed thread (`freddai.companion.lastThread`) or the most recent one with the drawer open, or New thread when there are none. Archiving or deleting a thread opens the next most recent thread, or New thread.
- Not done: no "Hide older threads" control in the drawer (older pages reset when a filter or search changes); before the first snapshot (or while the Mac is unreachable at launch) the phone shows the plain web loading/disconnected screen with the connection banner.

Verification: app typecheck and Vitest passed (model building, change-only sending, event mapping, launch selection); `cargo check` for desktop and `aarch64-apple-ios` passed; `pnpm ios:install` built and signed the Mac app, installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`, built the iOS archive (Swift compiled), installed to the paired iPhone and launched it. Gesture feel, VoiceOver and the drawer on a real screen were not exercised by automation, and no pairing, sign-in, message or agent turn was used. The running Mac GUI was not restarted; save drafts, quit and reopen it to load the update.

### Queued-message steering and editing (2026-11-04)

In the desktop reply composer, Enter sends immediately when idle, or adds a FIFO follow-up when a turn or queue is active. Command-Enter (Control-Enter on other keyboards) steers the active Codex turn; when idle it sends normally. Shift-Enter inserts a newline. IME composition and Alt-Enter retain normal text behavior.

Each queued row offers Steer instead, Edit, and Delete, while preserving pause/resume and More. Edit saves in place with the same queue ID, order, and attachments; Cancel retains the original. Failed saves retain the editor text. Steer instead requires an active Codex turn and sends the selected message, including attachments, to `turn/steer` with the expected active turn ID. Only provider acknowledgement records the user message and removes its queued copy. Rejections or turn-end races preserve the queued message, and steering failures do not fail the existing turn. Steering keeps the active turn's model, effort, and permission controls. Other providers report unsupported steering and retain the draft/queue. No live accounts or provider turns are used for regression tests.

Verification: source and spec updated; workspace typechecks passed, followed by a final app typecheck. All 41 app UI tests and 4 desktop send-handler tests passed. All 77 core unit tests and 2 host integration tests passed (2 helper worker tests intentionally ignored). The isolated Codex process fixture exercises the actual protocol loop, rejected steering without ending the turn, in-place queue edits preserving attachments, accepted steering removing only its queued copy, and acknowledged request retry idempotency. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the final sources, signed with the existing Developer ID, verified the strict signature and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`. A guarded native AppleScript normal quit and Launch Services open relaunched the UI after confirming visible textareas were empty; the running UI executable hash matches the installed bundle. The existing background host and its one active turn were preserved; that host still uses the previous build and upgrades through the normal idle handshake. New steering/editing require that host upgrade. No live-provider request was sent for validation, and populated queue layout was verified through component tests rather than live queue interaction.


Mobile new threads offer a Coding assistant selector after Project, listing only existing Mac-configured assistant IDs, display names and providers. The initial choice follows the project default; an explicit choice is kept per project while composing, scopes model/effort controls to that assistant, and is passed to host resolution for both model metadata and launch. No credentials or configuration paths are sent. Projects with configured assistants and a valid folder can start even when no default is set; missing or removed selections cannot enable Start. Older companion hosts without assistant metadata retain their existing default behavior.

### Thread row CSS constraint correction (2026-11-04)

The animated ThreadList grid wrapper must constrain its content to the navigator width: `.thread-presence-content` has `min-width: 0` in addition to `min-height: 0`. Without this, CSS grid's automatic minimum expands the entire row to the full title width, clipping text and trailing status outside the navigator before the title's ellipsis can apply. The existing single-line `overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap` on `.session-title` remains the truncation mechanism.

The isolated desktop overflow fixture now renders the actual ThreadList wrappers. `thread-truncation.browser.mjs` reproduced the defect before the CSS change (a 2322px row in a 240px viewport) and passes afterward at 240/320/360/500/700px, asserting constrained rows, overflowing title boxes, ellipsis and visible trailing metadata. Browser screenshots confirm the ellipsis is visible.

## iPhone New thread home and native attachments (November 4, 2026)

In the native iOS shell the app always opens on a New thread home with the thread drawer closed (the former "reopen the last thread with the drawer open" launch behaviour and `lastThread` storage were removed; threads are one hamburger tap away). The nav bar shows the hamburger, the mascot avatar, "New thread" and the project/connection line; there are no thread actions. The WebView content is centred above the native composer: the animated Dormouse (`MascotHero`, shared with the Mac, 112px) with the Mac's copy "What should we work on today?", then pill pickers for Project (defaults to the last used project, stored in `freddai.companion.lastProject`), Coding agent (the project's configured assistants, label "Provider · account"), Model (with reasoning effort) and Permissions. Pills open a native iOS action sheet through the plugin's `pick_option` command. Model choices come from the shared `modelChoices`/`effortChoices` helpers also used by the Mac `AgentControls` (Codex loads `client.models`; Claude uses the Sonnet/Opus/Haiku/Fable aliases). Non-native (browser) behaviour, including the web project `<select>` and web composer, is unchanged.

The native composer is shown in both New thread and conversation modes. Sending in New thread reuses `send()` -> `client.start({project, assistant, prompt, requestId, options})` and then opens the new conversation. A failed send restores the text and attachment chips to the native composer and shows the error. In a conversation, Model and Permissions pills sit above the composer and feed the same `options` (`update_agent_controls: true`) as the web controls.

Attachments: a "+" button at the composer's leading edge opens a menu (Photo Library via PHPicker, Take Photo when a camera exists, Files via the document picker). Picked items are copied into `<tmp>/freddai-attachments/<uuid>/<name>` and shown as removable thumbnail/file chips inside the composer. The send event carries file paths only (`{path,name,mime,bytes}`). JS calls `client.attachPath` -> the Rust command `companion_upload_path`, which verifies the path is inside the attachment folder, reads it and runs the existing begin/chunk/finish upload (24 KB chunks) to the Mac, returning the attachment; its id goes into `options.attachments`. Limits (20 files, 25 MB each, 100 MB per message) are enforced with a native alert on selection and re-checked in JS and Rust. Tmp copies are deleted (`discard_files`) only after the send succeeds; uploads are cached by path so a retried send does not upload twice. `NSCameraUsageDescription` and `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` were added to `Info.ios.plist`. Dormouse styles moved from `desktop.css` to `dormouse.css`, imported by `Dormouse.tsx`, so the iPhone bundle renders the mascot.

Verification: app and native typechecks, all app Vitest tests (new `native-home.test.tsx` covers launch-in-new-mode, start with chosen project/agent/model, attachment ids passed through, failure restore and the 20-file limit), `cargo check` (desktop and `aarch64-apple-ios`), and the mobile browser regressions (composer, header, read, send, swipe, project-groups, pairing, conversation-activity, mascot-header) passed. The fixture simulator build (`ios:sim:fixture`) was exercised on iPhone 17: home in light and dark, project and agent action sheets, Photo Library selection into two chips, send into a new conversation with the agent pills. Take Photo, Files, the real upload and relay path, and a physical device were not exercised. No accounts, pairing or live-provider turns were used.

### Background sub-agents (Claude Code), 2026-11-04

Claude Code answers `result` for the main agent while `Agent` calls started with `run_in_background` keep running, then wakes the main agent with a task notification. Freddai previously treated the first `result` as terminal, marked the thread Completed and terminated the process, so those sub-agents were lost ("didn't finish before the previous session ended").

- `crates/core/src/background_tasks.rs` tracks agent tasks from `system` `task_started`/`task_notification`/`task_updated` frames, the `Agent` launch result (`agentId`) and `<task-notification>` text. A `result` with a running task keeps the process, stdin and stream open and the thread `running` (sidebar, header, mascot and iPhone derive from `status`). The thread becomes Completed only after every task reported back and the main agent's follow-up `result` arrived. If no follow-up arrives within 90 s of the last notification, the thread completes and `background_tasks.wake_missing` is logged.
- Each task is stored on the session (`tasks`: id, description, kind, status `running|completed|failed|stopped`, summary), sent to the iPhone, and shown as a compact row under the transcript, outside the collapsed tool-activity group.
- If the process exits (or the turn errors) with tasks pending, they are marked `failed` (`stopped` when the user stopped/archived the thread), `background_tasks.lost` is logged, and the thread error names them. On host restart, running tasks become `stopped`.
- Split Claude `assistant` frames (one content block per frame, shared `message.id`) are keyed by message-level block index, matching streamed text, so the final reply is no longer rendered twice and tool_use blocks no longer overwrite text.
- Resuming an interrupted thread (Continue, Resume all, queued or typed message) prepends to the Claude prompt a notice listing sub-agents stopped by interruption (`interrupted` flag; description, type, agentId) and asking the main agent to resume them (SendMessage, else relaunch); the stored message keeps the user's text and the notice is sent once. The tracker is seeded with the thread's earlier tasks, and a `SendMessage` resume (`Resuming agent`/`resumedAgentId` result) or a relaunch with the same agent id flips the row back to `running`, so the hold-the-turn logic applies again. The SendMessage-resume result shape is observed in real transcripts; whether Claude also emits a `task_started` frame for it is not (both are handled).
- Codex: not changed; its app-server has no equivalent background-task wake-up that Freddai consumes. Messages sent while the thread is held join the queue and run after it completes (Stop cancels).
- Verification: Rust unit tests and fake-CLI integration tests (stays running, completes after notification and reply, exit with pending tasks fails loudly), vitest row rendering. Not built, installed or exercised with a live provider.

Removed connections are explicitly labeled. The title grows to fill the remaining space before status/actions and truncates only at that boundary. Inline rename continues using that width.

## Android hybrid companion (November 4, 2026)

Android shares the mobile React entry, thread models, native-shell event contract and Rust companion transport with iOS. It does not compile the desktop agent runtime. The Kotlin Tauri plugin embeds the existing WebView between native navigation and a multiline composer, with a native thread drawer (search, project/status filters, new/select/older actions), connection control, thread actions, selection dialogs, Android Back handling and system/keyboard insets. Conversations, approval forms, pairing and agent-choice pills remain shared web content. Android document-provider attachments are copied into the app cache, passed as paths to Rust, bounded to 25 MB each and removed after successful sends. Failed sends restore text and file chips through the existing bridge.

Pairing reuses the existing six-digit request and explicit Mac approval, pinned local TLS and encrypted relay protocol. Android encrypts credentials using an Android Keystore AES-GCM key and stores ciphertext atomically in the no-backup directory. Credential reads/writes are Rust-only plugin calls, absent from WebView permissions. Android now enrolls each user’s Mac using public setup supplied at runtime; no developer Mac endpoint/CA is embedded. See the direct-download beta section below.

`pnpm android:build` initializes generated Android scaffolding when needed and builds an arm64 debug APK. `pnpm android:install` selects one authorized adb device (or `FREDDAI_ANDROID_DEVICE`), installs and opens it. SDK/JDK/NDK and provisioning requirements are documented in `docs/android.md`. Debug signing is for development installation; Play Store signing/publication is separate. Android tablet persistent-sidebars and direct camera capture are not yet implemented.

## Home hub (desktop, 2026-11-04)

Home is the default landing view of the desktop app, reachable from the House button in the title bar or Cmd/Ctrl+1 (the thread-list and composer focus shortcuts moved to Cmd+2 and Cmd+3). It is a dense workbench pane, not a dashboard: the thread browser stays mounted underneath and returns when a thread, the New thread draft or a git pane is opened.

- Top: the animated Dormouse reflects the aggregate state (idle, something working, something needs you) via the shared `mascotState`, with a text summary.
- Projects: dashboard tiles share the sidebar’s saved project order and persistence. Each tile has a pointer drag handle (drop on the left/right half to insert before/after) and Alt+Up/Down keyboard reordering; moves update both surfaces. One tile per configured project in saved project order, including projects with no threads or no activity today. Thread activity and midnight never reorder or remove these tiles. Clicking a project with no live threads opens its inline prompt. Tiles have no entrance/movement animation and show colored icons with numeric counts for working (blue), done today (green), and needs you (amber); failures appear in red only when non-zero. Hover titles and accessible labels explain each count. Below the counts, each tile shows up to three recent live threads using the shared thread recency ordering; archived/deleted threads are excluded. Each row shows a status icon and truncated title and opens that conversation through the existing selection path. Each tile has a "+" that opens an inline prompt with a hold-to-talk mic (existing local dictation); submitting launches a thread through `launchThread` in DesktopApp, the same path as the New thread composer, with the project's resolved default account, saved worktree preference and the current default permission mode. Launching from a tile passes `select: false`, so the new thread is never selected or opened and Home stays on screen (the selection effect in DesktopApp is what used to flip `home` off); the tile's counters update live from the snapshot, shows a fading "Started · <title>" line, and closes its input so another project can be recorded into. The New thread composer keeps opening the thread. Once + or the mic is pressed, each tile shows an agent chip (`provider · account · model · effort`, e.g. `Claude Code · Main · default model · default effort`) produced by `resolveHomeLaunch` in `packages/app/src/desktop/home-launch.ts`, the single resolution used by both the chip and `createHomeThread`. With no resolvable account the chip is an error and Start (and the mic) refuse to launch. Add project reuses the existing add-project flow.
- Needs you: one card per thread with a pending request in any project, with the existing `Permission` card to allow, decline or answer in place and an Open thread link. Cards leave when the pending request clears in the live snapshot (the shared presence animation uses the motion tokens and respects reduced motion). Run state mirrors mobile `nativeRunState`.
- Bottom: an empty `home-summaries` slot is reserved for daily summaries, wired separately.
- All data comes from the existing snapshot state; no new polling.

Verification: see implementation report; native install and live-provider checks are not part of this entry.

## Daily summaries (2026-11-04)

An AI-written recap of what got done across all projects for Today, Yesterday and the day before (local calendar days), shown by `packages/app/src/desktop/DaySummaries.tsx` (no required props; optional `client` and `now` for tests).

- **Never automatic.** Opening the view only reads the cache (`day_summary`). A provider call happens only when the user clicks Generate or Refresh (`summarize_day`), because it spends their agent subscription. A cached day is returned untouched unless `force` is set; Refresh sends `force`.
- **Collection** (`crates/core/src/day_summary.rs`): threads whose last activity (`updated`) or last user message falls between local midnight and the next local midnight (DST days are 23 or 25 hours); deleted threads are skipped. Messages carry no timestamps, so a thread belongs to the days of those two markers. The prompt holds project, title, status, the first and latest user prompts and the final assistant message per thread, capped at 24,000 characters in total; newest threads win and omitted ones are counted in the prompt.
- **Format.** The summary markdown starts with `## Overall` (2-4 bullets of the day's headline outcomes across all projects, plus one `Open:` bullet for the most important unfinished items or things needing the user), followed by one `## <project>` section per project (bullets of what was done, then `Open:` bullets). The UI emphasizes the first `## Overall` heading. Summaries cached before this change keep the old project-only format until Refresh.
- **Side request, no visible thread.** Codex uses the same ephemeral, read-only, never-approve side thread on its own app-server process as thread-title generation. Claude uses a tool-free `claude -p --no-session-persistence --tools ""` call. The account with the most threads that day among Codex and Claude accounts is used; no other provider is substituted, and with none the request fails with `no_supported_provider`.
- **Host flow.** `summarize_day` starts a detached job (the host socket times out in 40 s) and the UI polls `day_summary`, which returns `{date, summary, running, error, threads}`.
- **Cache.** `<app data>/day-summaries/YYYY-MM-DD.json` holds markdown, `generated_at`, provider, account, model (`default`, the provider's configured model) and thread count.
- **Loud failures.** Errors are structured (`code`, `reason`, `at`), shown in the section, and logged as `day_summary.requested`, `.completed` and `.failed` in native.log. A failure writes no cache.
- **Verification.** Rust unit tests cover local-midnight and DST bucketing, input capping, provider choice, cache round trip and corruption, and the fake-agent side requests (`crates/core/tests/fixtures/summary_agent.py`); Vitest covers cached render, generate click, error display and empty days. No live provider turn was run, and the app has not been rebuilt or installed for this change.

## Voice dictation (2026-11-04)

- **Filler removal (2026-11-08).** Settings > Voice has a “Remove filler words” switch, on by default for new and existing settings files. Persisted as `removeFillerWords`; disabling preserves the original trimmed Whisper transcript. Shared Mac transcription cleanup covers desktop and companion recordings locally, without a provider call. Removes standalone hesitation sounds (um, uh, erm, er, uhm, ah, hmm and elongated variants), with adjacent punctuation cleanup. Meaning-bearing words such as “like”, “well” and “you know” remain; this is conservative hesitation cleanup, not rewriting or semantic editing. No translated filler dictionary is applied.
- **Filler removal delivery verification (2026-11-08).** Source: shared transcription cleanup and persisted Voice switch implemented. Tests: 555 app tests passed, 12 voice Rust tests passed (real-engine test ignored), workspace typechecks and diff whitespace check passed. Build: `pnpm native:install` succeeded. Signature: existing Developer ID used; strict/deep installed verification passed. Install: `/Applications/Freddai.app`, executable SHA-256 matches signed build `b4dc296eb9a1e893f631ad87f0bda5eab2207c5093af850208006733c6490f10`. Launch: approved launch returned existing GUI PID 61078, still mapping the backup bundle; window observation failed with ScreenCaptureKit error -3811. Manual quit/reopen is required; new GUI, task recovery, microphone and live-provider verification were not performed. No account or agent turns were used as fixtures.


Local, push-to-talk speech-to-text into the composer using Whisper (whisper.cpp). Nothing is sent to a cloud service; audio never leaves the Mac.

- **Settings > Voice.** Off by default. Model list: Whisper Turbo Q5 (recommended), Turbo Q8, Small, Base. Models are downloaded on request (resumable, size and sha256 verified, pinned revision) to `<app data>/voice/models/<id>/`; settings (enabled, active model, shortcut, language) are in `<app data>/voice/settings.json`. Turning dictation on requires an installed model.
- **Hold-to-talk key.** Default Right Option; configurable. Active only while the app is focused (no global/background capture). Hold to record, release to transcribe and insert at the cursor.
- **Composer mic button.** Press-and-hold in both the new-thread and existing-thread composers; same recorder and engine as the key.
- **iPhone/iPad.** The mic button is shown only when Mac voice is ready (enabled and model installed). The device records m4a (AAC), uploads it through the existing companion upload chunks, then sends the composer op `transcribe`; transcription runs on the Mac and the text is appended to any typed text (one space) and sent immediately through the same native composer Send path (attachments included, no extra tap). An empty transcript is not sent and shows "Didn't catch anything"; a transcription failure is not sent, keeps typed text, shows the error and logs `console.warn`. Desktop dictation is unchanged. The `voice` companion op reports readiness.
- **Permissions.** macOS `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` plus the `com.apple.security.device.audio-input` entitlement; iOS `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`.
- **Loud failures.** Not-ready, missing model, download and decode errors are returned with the reason and shown to the user; there is no silent fallback.
- **Verification (2026-11-04).** Real-engine `#[ignore]` test downloads whisper-base through the module download path (sha256 verified), then transcribes `say`-generated WAV and AAC m4a (both contain "dictation"). Mac build/sign/install and iPad install done; live microphone hold-to-talk in the GUI and on-device iPhone recording are not yet verified.

## Voice call mode (macOS, phase 1) (2026-11-09)

A hands-free conversation mode on the Mac desktop app, available in normal thread composers and in Commander-in-Chief. It is explicit and user-started (Phone button in the composer tools row); it is not a wake word and never starts on its own. Audio is processed locally (Whisper per segment, system `say` for read-out); nothing is sent to a cloud service. Requires Voice dictation to be enabled with an installed model (same readiness check; a not-ready state is shown as an error, not hidden).

- **Listening.** While a call is active the microphone stays open. An energy VAD segmenter (`voice/call.rs`, 20 ms windows, 100 ms hangover, 200 ms tail kept) cuts speech into segments; after about 1 s of silence (`silence_ms`, default ~1000) the segment is transcribed by Whisper and sent as a normal user message. Segments shorter than `min_speech_ms` are dropped.
- **Barge-in on the agent.** If the user speaks while the agent is working, the turn is interrupted (Stop) and everything said is bundled into one message after a 700 ms grace period. Mode `Interrupt | Queue`: Queue sends the message without stopping the running turn.
- **Speak replies (optional).** When on, each call message carries a `<voice-call>` instruction asking the agent to end with a short `<spoken>…</spoken>` line. That line is read with macOS `say` (selectable voice via `voice_list_voices`) while the mic is paused. Esc or the Stop speaking button ends read-out and resumes listening.
- **Transcript presentation** (`packages/app/src/conversation/voiceCallMarkup.ts`). The `<voice-call>` block is stripped from user messages, which show a mic icon; `<spoken>` is stripped from assistant messages and its text is rendered as a lead paragraph. Any renderer that shows these messages must strip both tags.
- **UI.** `VoiceCallBar` (controller `packages/app/src/desktop/voiceCall.ts`, `useVoiceCall`) shows state (Listening, Hearing you, Transcribing, Speaking, Error), the mode, Speak replies and End call.
- **Contract.** Tauri commands (macOS only): `voice_call_start(silence_ms?, min_speech_ms?)`, `voice_call_stop`, `voice_call_set_listening(listening)`, `voice_speak(text, voice?, rate?)`, `voice_stop_speaking`, `voice_list_voices`. Events: `voice-call://state`, `voice-call://speech-start`, `voice-call://segment` (transcript), `voice-tts://start`, `voice-tts://end` (`{interrupted}`). Failures are returned/emitted with the reason and shown in the bar; there is no silent fallback.
- **Known limitations.** The mic is paused during read-out, so there is no barge-in over the agent's voice until echo cancellation exists. Microphone-permission denial is detected after about 2 s, not immediately. The energy VAD can misfire in noisy rooms (false starts, or never reaching the silence threshold).
- **Phase 2 plan (not built).** iOS, iPadOS and Android companion call screen: on-device VAD segmentation uploads each clip to the Mac's Whisper through the existing companion transcribe path (`/v1/composer` begin/chunk/transcribe), on-device system TTS for read-out, background audio so a call survives screen lock, and a faster live link than the current polling/relay. Companion native renderers (iOS, Android, mobile web) must also strip `<voice-call>` and `<spoken>` tags. Always-listening and wake word remain out of scope.

## Agent thinking and todo list in the transcript

- **Thinking.** Claude `thinking_delta` stream events and final `thinking` blocks, Codex `reasoning` items (`item/reasoning/summaryTextDelta`, `textDelta`, `summaryPartAdded`, and the completed item's summary, else content) and ACP `agent_thought_chunk` updates are stored as transcript messages with role `reasoning` (`crates/core/src/reasoning.rs`, `claude_stream.rs::accept_thinking`, `runtime.rs::reasoning`). Claude streamed and final blocks share the `<messageId>-<blockIndex>` key, so final text replaces streamed text. The earlier drop of thinking was only "not assistant text"; there was no recorded privacy or persistence constraint. Because messages are persisted and sent in snapshots, each reasoning block is capped at 20,000 characters with a visible "(thinking truncated)" marker. Claude blocks with empty `thinking` (signature-only/redacted) never erase streamed text, and empty reasoning renders nothing.
- **Rendering** (`packages/app/src/conversation/ReasoningBlock.tsx`, shared by Mac and iPhone): collapsed `Thinking…` block that streams live while the thread is active and the block is the last transcript item, then `Thought for Ns`. The duration is measured in the UI from when this client first saw the block live, so a thread opened after the fact shows plain `Thought`.
- **Todo list.** Claude `TodoWrite` tool calls are kept as-is. Codex `turn/plan/updated` and ACP `plan` updates are converted by the host into the same `TodoWrite` tool_use shape (statuses `pending` / `in_progress` / `completed`; `activeForm` falls back to the step text) under the key `<turn>-plan`, so each update replaces the previous one within a turn. Codex plan *items* (free text) are not parsed. The `TodoWrite` row in tool activity shows a checklist (`TodoList.tsx`) instead of raw JSON, and `TodoPanel` pins the latest list above the composer (Mac and iPhone), collapsible, showing the active item, hidden when every item is done and the agent is idle.
- **Verification.** Source/tests only: Rust unit tests for thinking stream parsing, Codex reasoning, Codex/ACP plan normalisation and the size cap (`cargo test -p freddai-core --lib`), vitest for rendering (`ThreadTranscript.test.tsx`), `pnpm typecheck`. Not verified: build/sign/install of the app, launch, and any live provider turn (Claude/Codex/ACP real thinking or plan event shapes were taken from protocol knowledge, not captured from live runs). ACP `agent_thought_chunk` uses one reasoning block per turn.

## Integrated terminal (desktop)

- **Backend** (`apps/native/src-tauri/src/terminal.rs`, desktop only, cfg-guarded away from iOS/Android; `portable-pty`). Commands `terminal_open(cwd, cols, rows) -> id`, `terminal_write`, `terminal_resize`, `terminal_close`. Spawns `$SHELL -l` in the given folder; if `$SHELL` is unset it logs a warning and uses `/bin/zsh`. A missing folder or failed spawn returns an error that the panel prints in red inside the terminal. Output streams as `terminal-output {id,data}` events (UTF-8 safe across reads); shell exit emits `terminal-exit {id,code}`. Closing a tab and app exit (`RunEvent::Exit`) kill the shells.
- **Frontend** (`packages/app/src/desktop/TerminalPanel.tsx`, `terminal-sessions.ts`, `terminal-panel-state.ts`; `@xterm/xterm` + `@xterm/addon-fit`). Bottom panel in the thread pane, toggled with Ctrl+` or the header terminal button. Working folder is the thread's worktree if it has one, else the project root. Tabs with "+" and close; closing the last tab hides the panel. Colours come from the app's current CSS theme tokens and follow theme changes. Height is draggable (or Arrow keys on the handle) and remembered in localStorage along with open/closed. In split view the panel shows in the focused pane.
- **Persistence.** Sessions are keyed by folder and survive thread switching and hiding the panel. They are NOT restored after an app restart (shells are killed on exit).
- **`!command` in the composer.** A reply starting with `!` (not `!!`, no attachments) runs the rest in the thread's terminal instead of being sent to the agent. `!!text` is sent to the agent as written.
- **Verification (2026-11-04).** Source and tests only: Rust tests `terminal::tests` (real PTY runs `echo`, reports output and exit code 3; missing-folder error; UTF-8 split) pass; vitest `terminal-panel-state.test.ts` (toggle, height clamp/persist, Ctrl+` shortcut) passes; `pnpm typecheck` passes. Not verified: visual rendering in the running app, install/launch, iOS build (code is cfg-guarded and the dependency is desktop-only).

## Composer slash commands and @file mentions (desktop, 2026-11-04)

- The host no longer rejects prompts starting with `/` (launch, queue, edit-queued, steer). Text is sent to the agent as typed.
- `list_slash_commands {session|project, account}` (crates/core/src/commands.rs) returns `{name, description, argumentHint, source}` for the thread's/draft's provider:
  - Claude Code: built-ins that run headlessly (`compact`, `init`, `review`, `security-review`; interactive-only commands such as `/clear` are not listed), `~/.claude/commands/**/*.md` (or the account's config home), `<project>/.claude/commands`, skills (`skills/*/SKILL.md`, user and project) and installed plugin commands/skills as `plugin:name`; frontmatter `description`/`argument-hint` parsed. The `slash_commands` list in Claude's `system` init event is merged in per thread.
  - Codex: `~/.codex/prompts/*.md` as `prompts:<name>`. Codex app-server has no slash protocol, so the host expands `/prompts:name args` itself (`$ARGUMENTS`, `$1..$9`, `$KEY=value`, `$$`) before sending; an unknown `/prompts:x` fails loudly. No other Codex commands are listed.
  - ACP agents (Gemini, Grok, ...): only what the agent sent in `available_commands_update` (in memory, per thread and per provider). A brand-new draft sees nothing until that provider has run once in this host process.
- `list_project_files {session|project}`: `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard` in a git repo, else a bounded walk (skips dot-folders, node_modules, target, ...); capped at 50k, cached 30 s per project.
- Desktop composers (new-thread and reply) use `SuggestTextarea` (packages/app/src/desktop/ComposerSuggest.tsx, fuse.js): `/` at the start opens commands, `@` at a word start opens files and inserts `@relative/path`. Arrows move, Enter/Tab select, Esc closes; Enter still sends when nothing matches (pasted paths). Load failures show an inline alert. Mobile composer is unchanged.
- Verification: source/unit only. `cargo test -p freddai-core` (7 new command tests, queue test for slash prompts) and vitest `ComposerSuggest.test.tsx` pass. Not verified: a live Claude/Codex/ACP turn with a slash command, the real `slash_commands` init payload, app build/install/launch (orchestrator installs).

## Agent config settings (2026-11-04)

Settings > Agent config views and edits agent configuration for Claude Code and Codex, per account profile (`config_home`, or `<app-data>/accounts/<id>` for separate logins) and optionally per project. Backend: `crates/core/src/agent_config.rs`, exposed as the single Tauri command `agent_config` (`apps/native/src-tauri/src/desktop.rs`); UI: `packages/app/src/desktop/AgentConfigSettings.tsx`.

- **MCP servers**: Claude user scope = `mcpServers` in the profile `.claude.json` (`~/.claude.json` for the default `~/.claude` profile), project scope = `<repo>/.mcp.json`; Codex = `[mcp_servers.*]` in `<CODEX_HOME>/config.toml`. Types stdio/http/sse (Codex: stdio/http). Enable/disable: Codex `enabled`, Claude project servers via `disabledMcpjsonServers` in `.claude/settings.local.json`; Claude user-scope servers cannot be disabled (remove instead). Values that look like secrets are masked in lists and password-typed in forms.
- **Hooks** (Claude): `hooks` in profile `settings.json`, project `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json`; list by event, add/edit/delete command hooks (non-command hook types are listed, deletable, not editable).
- **Plugins** (Claude): reads `plugins/installed_plugins.json`, `known_marketplaces.json`; the enable toggle writes `enabledPlugins` in the profile `settings.json`. Install/uninstall is not run by Freddai; the exact CLI command is shown to copy.
- **Memory**: profile `CLAUDE.md` (Claude) / `AGENTS.md` (Codex), project `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`, in a monospace editor. File ids are a fixed set, never arbitrary paths.
- **Safety**: JSON edits are read-modify-write via `serde_json` with `preserve_order` (unknown keys and key order kept; whitespace is re-pretty-printed); TOML via `toml_edit` (comments and layout kept). Every write is atomic (temp + rename, permissions kept) and first copies the old file to `<app-data>/agent-config-backups/<timestamp>__<path>`. Unparseable files are never overwritten; validation errors carry field paths (e.g. `server.url`). Changes apply to new agent sessions only (stated in the UI). Running agents, and Claude itself rewriting `.claude.json`, are not coordinated: edits made between a list and a save of the same file are not locked.
- **Verification**: source/tests only. `cargo test -p freddai-core` (7 `agent_config` tests: round-trip with unknown keys, backups, TOML comments preserved, hooks CRUD, plugins, memory, validation) and `cargo check -p freddai-native` pass; vitest `AgentConfigSettings.test.tsx` (masking, edit/save payload, error display) passes; `pnpm typecheck` passes. Not built/installed/launched; not exercised against real agent sessions or real user config files. Note: enabling `serde_json/preserve_order` in `freddai-core` changes JSON object key order workspace-wide (no test regressions in core).

## Git write actions and "Save & push" (November 4, 2026)

- Backend (`apps/native/src-tauri/src/git_actions.rs`, Tauri commands in `desktop.rs`): `git_status` (`status --porcelain=v2 -b`), `git_diff_file` (staged / unstaged / untracked via `diff --no-index`; capped at 400 KB with a visible truncation note), `git_stage`, `git_unstage` (works in unborn repos), `git_discard` (restore / `rm -f` for newly added / `clean -f` for untracked), `git_commit` (optional `add -A` first), `git_push` (sets upstream on the first push, preferring `origin`; never forces; refuses detached HEAD and no-remote with a clear message), `git_pull` (`--ff-only`), `git_branches`, `git_switch_branch` (create or switch; names starting with `-` rejected), `git_pr_status` / `git_create_pr` (`gh`; when `gh` is missing or signed out, the reason is shown on the disabled Create PR control), `git_ship_preview`. All use explicit argument vectors in the project directory with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`; on failure the CLI's stderr is returned and rendered verbatim.
- Git pane (`GitActions.tsx`, wired into `GitHistoryPanel.tsx`): Pull / Push / branch switch / new branch / Create PR row; Uncommitted changes opens by default when dirty, with Stage all / Unstage all, a commit box, and per-file Diff (unified, add/del/hunk coloring), Stage/Unstage and Discard (inline confirmation). The pane acts on the path it shows (the project folder).
- Thread list "Save & push" (`ShipButton.tsx`): a compact pill under each project heading, above its threads, with changed-file and unpushed-commit badges; hidden when the tree is clean and nothing is ahead; shows "Push" when only commits are ahead. Click opens an inline confirm panel with the file list and an editable commit message, then Commit all (`add -A`) and push. Never force-pushes. A rejected push shows stderr and offers "Pull, then retry push" (ff-only pull, then push). The button always acts on the project root repo, not a worktree. Git status failures other than "not a git repository" are shown in the row, not hidden.
- Message generation (`crates/core/src/commit_message.rs`, host commands `commit_message_start` / `commit_message_state`): a tool-free one-off side request reusing `day_summary`'s `side_codex` / `side_claude` runners with the project's default agent (`Project.account`) over `git diff HEAD --stat` plus the diff truncated to 16,000 characters (the prompt states the truncation). Only Codex and Claude can write messages; a project without an agent, or with a Gemini/Grok agent, gets an explicit error. Generation runs on a detached host thread and the UI polls. On any failure the error is shown and the field stays empty and editable; no message is invented. Failures are logged as `commit_message.failed`.
- Refresh: status is read once on mount, debounced 1.5 s after any change in the project's thread statuses (turn finished), every 60 s while the window is visible, and after every action.
- Verification (source/tests only): 9 Rust tests with temp repos and a local bare remote (stage/commit/push with upstream, rejected push stderr, ff-only pull, discard variants, unborn-repo unstage, branches, verbatim errors, preview), 3 core tests for the prompt/cleanup/missing agent, and 9 vitest tests (visibility, generate/edit/commit/push, generation failure, rejected push -> pull -> retry, push-only, status error, discard confirmation, stderr). Typecheck passes except unrelated `ComposerSuggest.test.tsx` matcher errors from another change. Not built, signed, installed or launched; the live side request to a real agent, real `gh` PR creation and visual layout were not exercised. The running host must be restarted for `commit_message_*` commands to exist (older hosts report "Unknown host command").

## Keep Mac awake and wake from iPhone (2026-11-04)

Problem: the phone is useless while the Mac sleeps. Honest physical limits: an app cannot reach a fully asleep Mac over the internet or cellular; the relay only works while the Mac is awake. Wake-on-LAN (WoL) works only from the same local network, and many Macs on Wi-Fi wake only through an always-on Apple TV/HomePod Bonjour Sleep Proxy rather than a raw magic packet. The primary defence is therefore to prevent sleep.

Layer 1, prevent sleep (Mac). `apps/native/src-tauri/src/awake.rs`; the monitor runs in the long-lived `--host` process (started from `main.rs`), polls every 15 s and holds `caffeinate -i -w <host pid>` (a PreventUserIdleSystemSleep assertion; the display may still sleep; macOS drops it if the host dies). Settings in `<root>/awake/settings.json`, UI in Settings > Background (`AwakeSettings.tsx`): "Keep Mac awake while agents are working" (default on; holds while any thread is starting/running/waiting, a queue has unpaused messages, or an enabled schedule is due within 2 minutes) and "Keep Mac awake while a phone is paired" (default off, warns about battery; same system-sleep-only assertion). Acquire/release/reason changes and acquire failures are logged to the host's stderr (`awake: ...`); failures are shown in Settings. The host publishes `<root>/awake/state.json`; Settings shows "not reported" if it is older than 60 s. Closing the lid or choosing Sleep still sleeps the Mac.

Layer 2, Wake-on-LAN. The Mac exposes `/v1/wake` to paired devices (local and relay): per interface the hardware MAC from `networksetup -listallhardwareports` (not Wi-Fi's rotating private address), IPv4, broadcast address, plus `pmset -g` `womp`. The phone saves this with its Keychain credential (refreshed at most hourly after a successful snapshot). When the Mac is unreachable the connection banner shows Wake Mac: it sends 102-byte magic packets to UDP 9 at the directed broadcast, 255.255.255.255 and the last-known IP (`wake.rs`), then polls the Mac for 30 s and shows awake or a failure message. Any send error or a missing saved MAC is shown, never swallowed. Settings shows whether "Wake for network access" is on, with instructions (System Settings > Battery > Options) and the limits above.

Layer 3, scheduled wake: not implemented. `pmset repeat wake` / `pmset schedule wake` require administrator privileges; a non-admin app cannot set them. Schedules therefore run only while the Mac is awake (Layer 1 holds it awake shortly before a due schedule only if it is already awake).

Unverified: iOS may require the `com.apple.developer.networking.multicast` entitlement (and Local Network permission, already described by `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` in `Info.ios.plist`) before UDP broadcast succeeds; no entitlement was added, so on-device broadcast delivery is untested and failures surface as an error. Actual waking of a sleeping Mac, `womp` behaviour on this hardware and the assertion on a real idle timeout were not exercised. Verified: Rust unit tests for magic-packet layout, MAC/ifconfig/networksetup/pmset parsing, destinations, the assertion state machine (active acquires once, idle releases, failures recorded, paired opt-in) and activity detection; vitest for the wake-and-poll flow; the iOS target type-checks (`cargo check --target aarch64-apple-ios`). No app build, install or on-device launch was performed.

### Import sessions

Users can bring Claude Code and Codex sessions that were started outside Freddai (terminal, IDE, other apps) into Freddai as threads. This supersedes the earlier "external sessions are not imported" statement.

**Discovery (read-only).** Sources: Claude `<config dir>/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<sessionId>.jsonl` and Codex `<CODEX_HOME>/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`. Directories scanned: the config home of every Freddai Claude/Codex account (the shared default `~/.claude` / `~/.codex` is covered when an account connects it) plus the default homes (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`CODEX_HOME` or `~/.claude`/`~/.codex`). A session found in a home that no Freddai account uses is listed but cannot be imported until that sign-in is connected in Accounts; the reason is shown. Source files are never modified.

Per session: provider, session id, working folder, first real user prompt (title; injected context, command echoes and caveats are skipped), last activity (file mtime), message count, git branch. Sub-agent files (Claude `agent-*` and sidechain-only files, Codex `source.subagent` rollouts) are skipped, as are sessions Freddai already owns (matched on provider session id, including archived and deleted threads). Listing is lazy: all files are `stat`ed, the newest 600 per home have only a bounded header read (<= 400 lines / 2 MB, per-line cap 2 MB), and message counts are computed only for files <= 2 MB (otherwise shown blank).

**Import.** The dialog (Settings > Import sessions, title-bar menu, thread context menu, Settings > Projects) groups sessions by folder, newest first, with search (title, folder, branch), multi-select per session or folder, and an "Only for existing projects" filter. Importing a session whose folder has no project asks for explicit confirmation before creating the project. The host locates the file by session id inside the chosen account's CLI home (the client never sends paths), creates a `done` thread bound to the existing provider session id and the account whose home holds it, so the next message uses the normal exact-resume path (`--resume` / `thread/resume`). Imported threads are marked read. Errors (missing folder, missing file, wrong account, no confirmation) are returned per session and shown in the dialog; nothing falls back silently. Host methods: `import_candidates`, `import_sessions` (max 200 per call).

**Transcript fidelity.** Text messages (user/assistant) and tool calls are imported; tool calls are stored as their (string-truncated, 1500 chars per field) JSON record, like live tool activity. Tool results, thinking/reasoning, sidechain/sub-agent messages and attachments/images are not imported. Only the most recent 2000 messages are kept (long sessions) and at most 64 MB of a file is read; the dialog reports truncated sessions. Token usage and cost are not reconstructed.

**Verified / not verified.** Verified: Rust tests (`cargo test -p freddai-core --test import`) with fixtures for both providers (metadata, sub-agent skipping, transcript conversion, de-duplication, project-creation confirmation, error paths) and vitest for dialog selection logic. Not verified: against live provider data from every CLI version (formats are undocumented and change; unknown line types are ignored), actual resume of an imported session in a live provider turn, and the dialog in the running app. Known limitation: Claude keys sessions by the encoded working folder, so a folder reached through a symlink (project path canonicalised differently from the recorded cwd) may not resume.

## Office (v2: fixed floor plan with a story)

A pixel-art virtual office showing live agent activity as a small story. It is a self-contained, props-driven module; the old prototype `packages/app/src/Office.tsx` (mock `Thread` model, unused) is untouched. It does not use Pixel Agents or the Rive Dormouse; sprites are inline-SVG pixel mice with no external assets or new dependencies. v2 replaces the v1 layout that re-sorted and resized rooms whenever a thread changed.

- Module: `packages/app/src/office/` (`index.ts` exports `Office`, `officeModel`). Props only, no host calls: `{ sessions, projects, schedules?, queuedThreadIds?, now?, variant: "mini" | "full", onOpenThread(id), onExpand?(), onClose?() }`. Home passes all `db.sessions` (unfiltered) and `db.projects`; `queuedThreadIds` is optional and not wired on Home because the Home view has no queue data.
- Fixed floor plan: every project gets one room, in the order of the `projects` array (the host's creation order; it is not re-sorted). Rows differ by at most one project and each row fills the building width; spare width expands individual rooms and the spacing between their four desks (2x2). One or two projects also fill the available room band. Room height and desk artwork stay fixed. `floorPlan(projects, cols)` in `officeLayout.ts` is a pure function of the project list and a column count; thread states never enter it, so the map cannot reflow when work starts, waits or finishes (unit test plus a component test compare the floors before and after state changes). Only a viewport change (the column count that scales the map largest into its box, ties keep fewer columns) or a project being added, removed, renamed or recoloured changes the plan. A project with more than four desk-bound agents shows "+N" on its scoreboard; the room never resizes and the extra agents are not drawn.
- Shared spaces along the bottom: a compact lobby with the front door, a small reception counter, two queued-task benches, and a project-colored reserve mouse for every project; a coffee lounge with coffee machine, tables and cups; and a "You" desk with an in-tray. Corridors link every room door to these spaces; all walkways are axis-aligned and pass walls only through doorways (tested).
- One character per thread (`officeModel`, pure): running = seated at its desk with the typing animation and scrolling screen; waiting (pending decision) = in the coffee lounge holding a cup with a "?" bubble and its numbered tag, its desk stays reserved with a paused screen, and it walks back when the thread resumes; `starting` threads (and settled threads in `queuedThreadIds`) sit on lobby benches; failed = standing beside its desk with a red "!"; done/review = walks to the You desk, drops a folder in the in-tray and leaves through the front door; idle threads have no character. A newly started task walks from its project reserve spot to a free desk (or a queue bench while starting); the reserve replenishes after the departing mouse clears the spot. Thread characters wear their project color. Pending confirmation routes to coffee even when the reported status is failed. Desk, lounge and bench slots are remembered per thread so nobody swaps places.
- In-tray: shows a folder (project colour) for each finished thread within the 6-hour linger window, newest first, up to eight plus "+N"; clicking or pressing Enter on a folder opens the thread.
- Scoreboard on each room's wall, e.g. "50 msgs · 5 threads · 2 working": `projectTotals(sessions, projects)` counts user-role messages and threads over all non-archived, non-deleted sessions of the project (not only the lingering ones). Orphan threads whose project is not in `projects` are not drawn.
- Walking: characters follow corridor waypoints at a constant 90 logical px/s with a two-frame walking sprite (`requestAnimationFrame`, pure stepping in `officePath.ts`). A thread that leaves the model stays rendered until its exit walk finishes. Existing characters are placed at their destination on first mount (and for data arriving within 1.5 s of mount); walk-ins are not replayed, and a plan change (resize, project added) snaps everyone into place. `prefers-reduced-motion` teleports characters and removes animation. Run state reuses `homeRunState`; it does not imply progress beyond what the thread status reports.
- Clock shrine: permanently placed beside the lounge. Enabled `schedules` (`{ id, name, project, next_due, enabled }`) gather up to four admiring mice around a raised clock, with next-run and total/overflow labels; Home passes `db.schedules`. Empty schedules leave the shrine unoccupied without changing geometry.
- Accessibility: every character and folder is a button labelled "project · title · state"; state is also a glyph and legend text, never colour alone; hover/focus shows the same label as a tooltip. The legend counts Working, Needs you, Failed, Starting and Finished.
- Variants: `full` is the whole map scaled to the window (fixed overlay, inset 0, z-index 40, below Radix dialogs) with a close button, Escape, focus trap and focus restored on close; opening a character from full closes the overlay and calls `onOpenThread`. `mini` is the same fixed map scaled to the Home card width with the stage height capped at 280px; clicking its background or the expand button opens `full`.
- Verification (source/tests only, no live agent runs): vitest for `src/office` and HomeView; Vite fixture `packages/app/tests/fixtures/office.{html,tsx}` (6 projects, 12 threads, an Advance button) driven by `packages/app/tests/office.browser.mjs`, which asserts the plan does not move while characters walk, mid-walk motion, the mini height cap, folder click-through, reduced motion and no console errors, and saves screenshots (full 1440x900, mid-walk, mini). Not yet: layout editing, richer hover cards, schedule-shrine click-through, queue data on Home.

### Companion thread activity avatar (2026-11-04)

Conversation screens in the shared iPhone/iPad, Android and web companion render the locally bundled Rive dormouse in a persistent activity row above the composer, outside the transcript scroller. This complements native navigation without introducing a second animation runtime. The row names starting, working, waiting for input, completed, interrupted, failed, ready and a paused message queue. Pending input takes precedence over working; terminal failure/interruption takes precedence over stale pending input. Pausing the message queue does not imply that an active turn has stopped. Disconnection/connection recovery replaces cached activity with a neutral avatar and an explicit unavailable/connecting label. Loading an absent thread is distinct from idle. Existing mascot Reduce Motion, animation pause, runtime fallback and animation-slot limits apply; the polite live status remains readable when animation is disabled. No accounts or provider turns are needed for fixture verification.

### Companion send feedback parity (2026-11-04)

Companion optimistic messages show “Sending…” only until the host accepts them. Acceptance leaves the normal user bubble without a Mac-specific queue label; actual FIFO messages remain visible in the existing queue panel with pause/resume controls. Starting/running/waiting threads and optimistic threads awaiting their first snapshot never show a handoff instruction. An unavailable, inactive thread without pending input or outgoing messages instead explains that it cannot take replies and offers starting a new thread. Queue pause copy no longer suggests switching devices. Execution, request deduplication, snapshot reconciliation, provider streaming and approval handling are unchanged.

### Stale companion server replacement (2026-11-04)

The long-lived `--companion-server` process records `companion/server.identity` (pid plus build identity: package version, resolved binary path, size and mtime; the desktop caches binaries under a content-hash name, so it changes on every install). On launch the desktop's `ensure()` compares it with the identity of the binary it would spawn. If the lock is held by a different or unrecorded (pre-identity) build, it sends SIGTERM (holders found via `lsof` when no record exists), waits up to 5 s for `listener.lock` to free, then spawns the new server. The server is a stateless HTTP front end over `host.sock`; agent runtimes live in the separate `--host` process, so replacement never interrupts agent work (an in-flight phone request may be dropped and is retried by the phone). Events: `companion_server.started`, `companion_server.stale_replacing`, `companion_server.stale_replaced`, `companion_server.stale_replace_failed` (stderr). The iPhone companion now shows “Dictation unavailable: <error>” (and `console.warn`) when the voice status check fails, keeping the mic hidden. Verified: Rust identity-decision tests and a vitest for the failure surfacing; the replacement itself takes effect on the next desktop launch and is not yet verified live.

### Android direct-download beta (2026-11-04)

Android now enrolls the user’s own Mac at runtime rather than embedding the developer’s endpoint/CA. The pairing form accepts public JSON setup (HTTPS origin plus CA certificate), six-digit code and phone name. The app pins that CA with no built-in roots or redirects, retains the previous pairing on failed enrollment, and persists approved setup with credentials through Android Keystore. iOS legacy compile-time setup remains supported. Mac Pair phone explicitly provisions a fresh identity only when absent, starts the listener, and exposes public setup while a pairing code is active; existing identities remain intact. Older public Mac builds can use the documented local provisioning helper and existing Pair iPhone approval.

`pnpm android:release` builds a non-fixture ARM64 release, signs with a persistent private release keystore outside source, verifies signature/alignment/non-debuggability, and emits the APK and SHA-256 checksum. Android betas use a separate GitHub prerelease and `ANDROID_DOWNLOAD_URL` on the landing page, preserving desktop stable-release discovery. The page labels it an APK beta and links setup/update instructions. No Play listing or developer account creation is performed.

## Companion idle-message start transition — November 4, 2026

The companion reply route persists the request in the host FIFO, then immediately invokes the existing queue dispatcher instead of waiting for its two-second scheduler interval. Running turns, queue pauses, request-id deduplication, ordering, and persisted launch errors retain their existing behavior.

The shared iOS/Android/web companion marks a reply sent to an idle, empty, unpaused thread as starting immediately, driving both the native/header state and activity avatar. Its optimistic bubble remains visible through stale snapshots and its own temporary dispatch queue entry is omitted from Up next. A host user-message echo ends the overlay. Real queued follow-ups remain in Up next and cease appearing as optimistic chat bubbles once the authoritative queue owns them; deleting/clearing a queued message cannot resurrect the optimistic bubble. Host pauses, queue errors, approval requests and terminal failures remain visible, and transport errors restore the draft through the existing path.

Regression: the idle-reply test failed before the fix because Starting… was absent; it now exercises idle → accepted → persisted FIFO → running/echo without the Completed/Up next flash. The running-reply regression verifies queue ownership removes the pending chat article. An offline host-dispatch test verifies an immediate launch attempt (missing project, no provider execution), retained failure, pause behavior, FIFO and duplicate request handling.

### Companion interruption recovery (2026-11-04)

The shared companion allows 60 seconds from the first failed snapshot before displaying the outage alert; retries do not reset the deadline, success clears it, and authentication failures remain immediate. Interrupted/empty bodies, response timeouts and HTTP 408/429/5xx responses are transient network failures rather than app-version mismatches. All outage kinds expose diagnostic details and copying.

Native snapshot reads now try the configured encrypted relay when the direct response cannot be read/decoded. A direct response only marks the local route healthy after decoding succeeds. This recovery is restricted to read-only snapshots; uncertain mutations are never replayed by this path. Existing native transport/error logs include fallback and recovered timing. A bounded local history of the last 100 failure/recovery events survives UI reloads and is included in Copy details (timestamp, classification, attempt reason, count, duration only; no payloads or credentials). Exact physical-network cause is not established by the screenshot; these changes address reproduced alert behavior and confirmed transport recovery gaps.
