## Native computer use milestone 1 (November 4, 2026)

Freddai bundles a signed `Freddai Computer.app` MCP stdio helper on macOS 14+. New Codex and Claude turns receive its `freddai_computer` server through process arguments, preserving user MCP configuration and credentials. Cached background hosts resolve the helper inside their own signed bundle. ACP providers and unbundled development binaries do not receive it in this milestone.

The six tools cover permission status/setup, app launch by bundle identifier, visible-window discovery, observation, and one foreground action. Observation returns the selected window's ScreenCaptureKit PNG at native scale, pixel dimensions, a bounded accessibility tree, and a one-use 30-second token. Actions support click, Unicode text, a bounded shortcut vocabulary, vertical scroll, and AXPress. A recent token, unchanged window geometry/owner and unchanged pixels are required before focus is raised. Focus identity and Escape are checked before input; coordinate actions refuse an overlaid target. Each dispatched action returns fresh evidence. Failed post-action captures explicitly prohibit blind retries. The model must compare the evidence with its intended result; a dispatched action does not establish task success.

A nonblocking per-user advisory file lock serializes Freddai computer actions across helper processes. It does not prevent human input or unrelated automation. Foreground use moves focus/pointer; background use, drag, screen crops, OCR, arbitrary accessibility value editing, and ACP integration remain future work. Animated windows can invalidate the conservative pixel check and require re-observation. Screen contents are untrusted task data. Existing provider approval settings apply; macOS Screen Recording and Accessibility grants remain user-controlled, with an explicit setup tool and actionable missing-permission errors.

Implementation references: Apple's ScreenCaptureKit `SCScreenshotManager` and AXUIElement APIs; OpenAI's computer-use integration recipes (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/tools-computer-use-integration): preserve screenshot resolution, return image content rather than base64 text, and inspect the result after short action sequences. This is a shared execution surface, not a claim of model-quality parity with the Codex desktop app.


### Companion snapshot scale and initial connection (2026-11-04)

Diagnosis: actual Mac request logs showed 39–43 MB snapshots; phone diagnostics showed repeated network failures roughly 48–54 seconds apart. Every initial/periodic snapshot serialized all unarchived transcripts. Relay ciphertext is hex-encoded, so these responses also exceeded its 16 MiB envelope limit; extending grace and falling back could not fix this. A synthetic 124-thread regression failed at 44,690,633 bytes before the fix.

New native clients explicitly request scoped snapshots: project/thread metadata on Home, plus the complete messages/tasks of the selected conversation when open. Thread selection immediately refreshes with that ID. Metadata carries messagesLoaded=false; unloaded transcripts show a conversation loading state and are neither marked read nor sent replies until hydrated. All thread summaries remain available, and legacy clients without scoped=true retain full snapshots. No transcript is truncated or deleted. The same synthetic Home projection is now 46,541 bytes, and the selected-history regression verifies every original message survives. Snapshot requests have a 20-second overall deadline including relay fallback; mutation delivery behavior is unchanged. Very large individual transcripts still need pagination in a future iteration; this change removes the all-conversation download from every refresh.

Initial native Home presents a dedicated connecting scene with the existing dormouse, cloud and string, explanatory text and no loading project dropdown. Ready controls appear on first successful snapshot. The animation respects reduced motion and the existing mascot pause control; an established outage uses a static waiting scene. Device-local diagnostics now also record the first successful connection of a session; server logs separately report response preparation and successful/failed delivery, and rejected relay responses include byte counts and HTTP status without identifiers or payloads.

Coordinate input was correctly refused because a macOS Screenshot overlay covered the desktop; successful coordinate click, typing, shortcuts and scroll remain unverified. No live-provider turns, credentials, account creation or sign-in were used.

### Lightweight companion startup metadata (2026-11-04)

Startup keeps project/agent configuration and resolved default-agent choices plus a small searchable thread title index. The existing per-project view shows live/needs-attention threads and five recent finished threads, with older titles available via search and Show older. A hard five-thread synchronization cap is intentionally not introduced: it would complicate search and could hide unread/approval work for little benefit now that transcript bodies are fetched only on selection. Local phone display preferences continue loading locally; private desktop settings/credentials are not copied to the phone.

Unopened native scoped threads now contain only ID, project, title, status, timestamps/read cursor, queue count, empty message list, messagesLoaded=false, canReply=false, null error and an empty pending object when attention is required. The pending object is a presence marker, never an actionable approval payload. Usage, model/options, approval details, errors, task bodies and queued prompts are supplied only for the selected conversation. Full selected and legacy snapshots retain all existing details; the existing UI loading gate prevents actions against summary-only rows. This is a Mac-server optimization and does not require another iOS binary installation.

## Background accessibility and visible agent cursor (November 4, 2026)

Computer use defaults to `mode=background`. In this mode Freddai never calls app activation, window raising, or global keyboard/mouse event posting. Background `press` performs AXPress on an observed element; coordinate `click` resolves to a pressable accessibility element inside the observed window; `set_value` explicitly replaces a writable, non-secure text field's value. `hover` only displays the visual agent pointer (it does not synthesize app hover events). Existing foreground actions remain available through explicit `mode=foreground`, with instructions requiring user authorization and forbidding automatic fallback. Keyboard shortcuts, arbitrary typing/scrolling, non-accessible controls, and actual tooltip hover are not supported in background mode. App launch requests `activates=false`.

A transparent nonactivating NSPanel draws a lavender pointer, app/action label and click halo for 1.2 seconds. It ignores mouse events, cannot become key/main, and never changes the system pointer. Coordinates convert from Quartz's top-left origin to AppKit's primary-screen bottom-left origin. The MCP stdin reader now waits off the main actor so overlay hiding/rendering can continue between requests. The cursor is a separate window and does not pollute target-window screenshots. Observation exposes pixel-space accessibility bounds and whether values are settable.

Background actions refuse to target the current foreground app, preserving the person's work even in another window of that app. Actions retain the observation token, geometry/owner, pixel-freshness and shared-lock checks. If an app activates itself in response to an accessibility action, the helper reports that the action may have completed and blocks further background actions for that PID for the helper session; it does not steal focus back. App-specific behavior prevents an unconditional guarantee for all apps. Process-targeted CGEvent mouse injection was experimentally ignored by the native fixture, so it is not shipped as a claimed background capability.

Verification workflow: compile `scripts/tests/computer-use-fixture.swift` and launch a target plus a `--witness` instance; compile `scripts/tests/computer-use-focus-state.swift` to `/tmp/freddai-focus-state`; run `scripts/tests/computer-use-background.py <helper>`. The test asserts the actual changed counter/field, unchanged foreground PID and pointer, rejects unsupported background keys, and captures the separate cursor window. `--allow-human-motion` explicitly skips only the pointer-equality assertion if the person moves their mouse during the test. Foreground smoke tests specify `mode=foreground` to preserve the original coverage. Apple API references: CGEvent.postToPid, NSWindow.StyleMask.nonactivatingPanel, NSWindow.ignoresMouseEvents and AXUIElement.

Background milestone verification: the two Rust MCP wiring tests, workspace typechecks, dual-architecture Swift build, and MCP protocol/permission checks passed. Native fixture background click and set_value changed the target while the foreground PID and physical pointer coordinates stayed unchanged; unsupported background Return was refused without fallback. This passed both before packaging and against the final signed installed helper. Some earlier runs detected human pointer movement and failed the strict pointer assertion; the final installed-helper run passed that assertion even with the optional human-motion flag present (no skip was taken). Hover overlay was captured independently and visually checked (`output/computer-use/agent-cursor.png`), without capturing the person's desktop in the final artifact.

### Mid-turn steering across providers (November 4, 2026)

Command/Ctrl-Enter in a running conversation and the queued message’s Steer action work for every integrated provider. Ordinary Send continues to queue. Codex uses its existing `turn/steer`. Claude Code sends its streaming control `interrupt`, waits for both the control acknowledgement and the interrupted turn’s result (either order), then writes the update on the same process/session. When Claude is holding for background agents after a result, the interruption acknowledgement is sufficient. Gemini, Antigravity, Grok, OpenCode, and Cursor use ACP v1 `session/cancel`, wait for the outstanding prompt response, then submit a new `session/prompt` with a fresh request ID in the same session. Interruption does not spawn a replacement provider process.

Validate attachments before interrupting. Cancel pending permission requests and discard obsolete permission cards; ACP permissions receive the `cancelled` outcome. Continue recording output received during cancellation, and separate the follow-up response’s transcript keys from the preceding response. Preserve the submitted update and attachment metadata in history, remove it from the queue only after writing the follow-up, and deduplicate acknowledged request IDs. A write confirms submission to the CLI, not model execution. Rejected or timed-out interruption leaves the queued message intact; direct submission errors restore the composer draft. A 25-second handoff deadline prevents sending an update later after reporting interruption failure. Never claim that all background tools or sub-agents survive a provider’s own interrupt; Freddai retains the process and continues tracking the lifecycle events the provider emits.

Protocol references: [Claude SDK interrupt control](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-python/blob/main/src/claude_agent_sdk/_internal/query.py), [ACP v1 cancellation and subsequent prompts](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/prompt-turn#cancellation).

Verification uses an isolated Python protocol peer in temporary workspaces, without credentials or live provider calls. Coverage includes all six non-Codex providers, both Claude acknowledgement/result orders, pending permission cancellation, attachment validation and delivery, same-session continuation, separate responses, rejected interruption, timeout retention, and acknowledged retry deduplication. Existing Codex steering and background-agent tests remain in the core suite.

### Active turn elapsed time

The macOS transcript activity row shows discreet elapsed time for the current dispatched turn, updated once per second and persisted as `turn_started_at`. It includes tool execution and waiting for input; queue acceptance, steering, streaming updates and switching panes do not reset it. New turns reset it. Older sessions without a dispatch timestamp omit the timer. While active, available provider context usage is shown as explicitly labeled context tokens (last reported, potentially stale), never presented as generated tokens for this round. Terminal states remove these metrics.

### Companion completed-thread emphasis

iOS, Android, and web companion thread rows use 65% content opacity when the thread is done, read through its latest update, has no pending user decision, and has no queued work. All other rows retain full opacity. The rule also applies to the dashboard project thread list; rows remain selectable.


## Recurring task management — November 5, 2026

The shared schedule editor supports creation, editing, agent/account assignment, project-policy routing, pause/enable, explicit Run now and confirmed deletion. Deletion removes future dispatches, preserving existing sessions and running agents. Next-run labels use the stored timezone. Daily, weekday and Monday presets are provided; the When field accepts explicit phrases such as “every weekday at 9 AM”, with a visible cron/timezone preview. Ambiguous language is rejected. Advanced five-field cron remains available.

Desktop conversation actions include “Schedule recurring task…” prefilled with the thread's project, account, title and first user instruction; the task remains editable before saving. This is a form attached to the thread, not conversational tool execution. Arbitrary natural-language create/update/delete messages sent to a provider are not yet wired to Freddai schedules and must not be presented as verified scheduling.

Paired companion apps expose the same editor and host-owned schedule snapshot through two explicit allowlisted commands (`save_schedule`, `schedule_action`), reusing host validation. Disconnection disables mutations; cached schedules are informational. Companion client source must be packaged into a phone build to expose the new controls. No new account, login or live provider turn is required for management tests.

### Companion recording feedback and spacing (2026-11-05)

iPhone/iPad conversation push-to-talk prepares its medium start haptic when the hold is recognized and fires only after recording starts successfully. The audio session explicitly permits haptics during recording so microphone activation does not suppress that feedback. The microphone has a 16pt gap from the up-arrow Send button (previously 6pt); the attachment control remains on the opposite side. Physical-device vibration verification remains required.

### Native companion dashboard interactions (2026-11-05)

The iOS/iPadOS dashboard project cards are UIKit views and Android cards are platform views. Native controls own project navigation, New thread, the global coding-agent picker, model/effort pickers, and microphone gestures. The existing web surface above the native list retains the Office and daily summaries; its height is capped at 260 points/dp and shrinks in landscape. Browser companions retain the shared web cards. The native bridge transports a privacy-limited dashboard model and explicit actions, reusing existing composer selection keys and authenticated transcription transport; no accounts or credential setup is introduced.

Microphones use press-and-hold: native recording starts after permission while the press is still active, with start haptics and elapsed recording feedback. Release transcribes into the selected project/agent's draft, without submitting a provider turn. Moving outside the control, touch cancellation, navigation, backgrounding, disconnection and recording interruption discard captured audio. Releasing during a permission/readiness request never starts a delayed recording. Native captures are mono 16kHz AAC, capped at two minutes; sub-300ms captures are discarded. Android also enforces a 9MiB capture limit. Snapshot refreshes do not replace an actively held native control. Native accessibility activation provides a start/finish alternative; browser pointer/keyboard holds use the same release/cancel semantics.

The destination is captured when recording starts. If the user navigates while transcription is pending, its result is saved under that project/agent draft key and restored by that card's next New thread action; it never overwrites the newly selected composer's text. In the normal dashboard flow, the transcript is appended to the native composer for review, preserving existing typed text. Errors restore idle controls and remain visible through the existing error surface.

Native dashboard follow-up: tablet cards preserve responsive two/three-column grids; large accessibility text uses one column and stacked task buttons. The Office's expand/close callbacks temporarily give its web view the full native content area, then restore the cards; expanding also cancels an active recording.

### macOS project schedule workspace (November 2026)

Each project heading has a clock button that reveals scheduled task rows above its normal threads, including paused schedules and a New scheduled task action scoped to that project. A schedule row opens its latest available run conversation with a configuration panel on the right. Before the first run, that area explains where the conversation will appear. The configuration supports name, prompt, recurrence/time, IANA timezone, account routing, worktree, enable/pause, explicit Run now, confirmed deletion, and run-history navigation. Deletion keeps existing conversations. Home, ordinary threads, Git history, and new-thread navigation leave this workspace. Configuration drafts survive snapshot polling while the panel is open; switching schedules discards unsaved configuration edits.

The first implementation uses the existing detached macOS host, not system crontab entries: UI commands (`save_schedule`, `schedule_action`) go through Tauri to the owner-only host socket and persisted schedule records. Its two-second loop claims durable occurrence IDs before dispatch and reconciles session history after restart. Five-field cron/timezone rules, overlap skipping, one-minute missed-run tolerance with no catch-up, and account/permission checks remain unchanged. Every execution creates a separate conversation. Closing the GUI does not stop the host; sleeping/logging out is not a guarantee of execution. No new MCP, file watcher, Git trigger, webhook, or companion interface is introduced by this change. Recurring times and explicit Run now are the supported triggers; a future MCP should call these same host commands rather than introduce another scheduler.

Validation uses injected UI transports and existing isolated scheduler tests; no real account, credentials, or live provider turn is created for testing. Native build/sign/install and launch verification are reported separately at delivery.


Desktop composer model-picker dismissal, November 5, 2026: the shared floating details menu closes on outside pointer clicks, outside accessibility-generated clicks, and Escape anywhere in the document. Inside interactions keep the panel open. Escape returns focus to the summary when focus was inside the panel and consumes the key so an enclosing composer/dialog stays open; composer text and model/effort selections are preserved. Model-picker dismissal synchronizes React's expanded state immediately rather than waiting for the browser's asynchronous toggle event. Regression checks exercise the actual AgentControls component without accounts, credentials, or provider requests.

Native observation showed restored composer draft text and existing running threads; background host PID 60768 and agent watchers remained running across GUI restart. This verifies GUI reconnection to existing work, not a macOS reboot or provider resume. ## Browser computer-use reliability (November 5, 2026)

Confirmed Safari regression: the depth-first 160-element accessibility walk exhausted its budget on Start Page content before reaching the toolbar. The read-only `scripts/tests/computer-use-browser.py <helper>` regression failed against the installed helper (address bar absent among 160 elements) and passed with breadth-first traversal (smart search field at element 21). Observation now walks breadth-first with cycle deduplication, a 320-element/24-level budget, a bounded 2048-entry queue, and explicit `accessibility_truncated` metadata. Existing one-use observation tokens, pixel/geometry/owner checks, focus safeguards and background-only defaults remain.

`computer_open_url` opens an absolute HTTP(S) URL in an explicitly selected installed browser, including Safari and Arc, through NSWorkspace URL handling with `activates=false`. It does not require address-bar accessibility, AppleScript browser automation, a browser extension, debugging ports, typing, or pointer takeover. It validates the selected app as an HTTPS handler, rejects credentials/custom schemes, uses the same cross-process desktop lock as actions, invalidates the old observation, reports dispatch separately from page verification, and stops subsequent background interaction if the browser activates itself. Browser preferences determine tab/window placement; this is not exact-tab navigation. Tool instructions direct agents to list windows and observe the result rather than assuming navigation succeeded. Foreground key vocabulary includes l/t/w/r/brackets for browser shortcuts, with the existing explicit-authorization requirement.

Source references: Apple's NSWorkspace open(_:withApplicationAt:configuration:) and OpenConfiguration.activates documentation (https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace). New protocol regressions exercise URL rejection, explicit browser selection and coordination-lock refusal without navigating. Direct signed-helper smoke tests loaded and observed Example Domain in both Safari and Arc with `focus_changed=false`; no account, credential, sign-in or provider turns were used. Full app delivery verification follows below.

### Thread windows

On macOS, the thread list context menu and conversation header actions offer **Open in New Window**. The action opens a thread-only secondary native window. It contains the thread header, transcript, approvals, queue and composer, with no home, navigation, thread list, account footer, app settings, or workspace creation/split shortcuts. Close window closes that secondary native window. Repeating the action focuses the existing window for that thread. Windows share the existing host and conversation state (messages, queue, approvals and management actions), so opening or closing a window does not launch or stop an agent. Reply drafts and staged attachments remain local to each window; opening a window leaves the original composer intact. Thread windows do not overwrite the main workspace split preference or pane layout.

Thread-window verification (November 5, 2026): source and spec updated. Tests: app TypeScript checks passed; all 290 frontend tests passed; the native regression test passed for exact special-character ID routing, visible window configuration and preserved native minimum width. Build: final production build completed through `pnpm native:install`. Signature: existing Developer ID signing and strict installed verification passed (team BSH2UEDLU5). Install: `/Applications/Freddai.app` replaced; installed and built executable SHA-256 hashes matched. Launch: previous GUI quit normally via Apple Events, then the installed app was relaunched with `computer_launch`; PID 9802 mapped `/Applications/Freddai.app/Contents/MacOS/freddai-native`. Native observation showed existing conversations and four working threads after relaunch; existing host/watchers remained alive. Opening/focusing a thread window remains unverified in the live GUI: background `computer_action` refused because the target app was currently in use; no foreground fallback was attempted. Live-provider verification: no new provider turn or account change was used. Existing chunk-size and unused-function build advisories remain.

Further browser interaction probes found that exact whole-window pixel equality rejected Safari AXPress on an otherwise unchanged button, while Arc initially exposed chrome but no web area. Observation now performs the supported AXEnhancedUserInterface/AXManualAccessibility assistive-technology handshake (unsupported attributes are ignored), allowing Arc web content to appear. References: https://www.chromium.org/developers/design-documents/accessibility/ and https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/tutorial/accessibility/ . Semantic press/set_value now validate the same live AX target's window membership, window state, role/title/description/identifier/value/enabled/selected/document/URL/actions/bounds against the observation; unrelated pixels can change. Coordinate and keyboard actions retain exact pixel freshness. Tokens, geometry/owner, locking and focus checks still apply. Background set_value verifies the actual field value after dispatch and reports mismatches explicitly.

The local browser fixture deliberately animates during actions and changes a button after observation to test both accepted and refused paths. Safari button presses succeeded despite animation; stale target changes and consumed-token replay must still be rejected. Safari's background HTML text edit returned AX success without changing the field, and Arc's background button press returned AX success without triggering the page; these are observed limitations, not passed interaction coverage. Arc inspection mode explicitly checks page controls without claiming interaction. Foreground control remains available only with explicit takeover authorization; no automatic fallback was added.

### Routine editor UI refinement

The macOS routine workspace uses the regular chat surface and composer before its first run, with “This routine hasn’t been run yet.” The composer shares the Task prompt with the configuration panel: before creation it saves the routine; once saved, sending saves current configuration and explicitly requests Run now. After creating a routine, its refreshed snapshot selects it automatically; the first available run conversation opens automatically when no historical run is selected. Existing run conversations retain their normal reply composer. Save errors preserve the draft and never dispatch a run. Neither opening nor saving a routine starts a provider turn.

Configuration keeps name, project, agent/account, timezone, task prompt, separate worktree, and enabled. Routing is no longer exposed: existing routing is preserved, while explicitly choosing an account pins it. Replace natural-language When and raw cron fields with a shadcn Repeat picker (every day, weekdays, every week, every month), native time input, and weekday/day-of-month selection as appropriate. Unsupported existing custom recurrences remain intact unless replaced with a supported repeat option. Days 29–31 explain that only months containing the day run. The backend cron contract is unchanged.

Separate worktree and Enabled use shadcn Switch controls aligned to their labels. Remove the overlap/permissions footnote from the editor. The configuration close icon is right aligned. MCP and companion changes remain outside this UI refinement.

### Chat list markers (2026-11-05)

Shared transcript styling explicitly restores disc bullets and decimal numbering after the global Tailwind reset, in both desktop and compact layouts. Nested lists retain their indentation and visible markers. Offline browser coverage renders real Markdown through ThreadTranscript and checks computed list styles and list-item display at desktop and mobile widths.

### Multiple schedules per routine

Each macOS routine has an editable list of schedule entries. Add schedule appends a row; each row offers independent weekday chips and a time, plus existing daily/weekday/monthly presets. Monday/Wednesday/Friday at 09:00 and Monday/Tuesday/Thursday at 15:30 are separate entries on the same routine. Remove deletes one entry; at least one entry and one weekday per weekly entry must remain. Existing single, monthly, and custom recurrences are preserved. All entries share the routine’s timezone, prompt, account, enabled state, worktree choice, and run history.

The persisted `cron` field now holds one to 32 newline-separated five-field expressions. Single-expression data needs no migration. The host validates every entry and computes the earliest next occurrence across the entire list for save, resume, and scheduler advancement. Coincident entries coalesce into one occurrence ID and one run; existing overlap, missed-run, and repeated local-time rules apply to the routine as a whole. Older hosts reject multi-entry input rather than silently retaining only the first entry; the UI explains that the background host must finish its update before saving multiple entries. No live accounts or provider turns are used for validation.

### Composer account picker deduplication (2026-11-05)

The new-thread Composer lists each configured account once. Its selected value is the effective account ID, so the shared Select checkmark identifies the current account even when it is resolved automatically from project/global preferences. ### Coding agent display names

Every connection identity uses `Coding agent · User name` (for example, `Codex · Personal`, `Claude · Work`, or `Gemini · Personal`). The same shared formatter supplies desktop usage/footer cards, thread headers and tooltips, composer/account selectors, settings, Home, schedules, and companion agent pickers, including native iOS/Android picker models. Editable connection-name fields retain the exact user name; CLI setup instructions may use the official CLI product name. Missing connections retain their explicit unavailable labels.

### Routine end date

A routine may have an optional `end_date` (YYYY-MM-DD) applying to all its schedule entries. The macOS End by date input includes Next week, Next month, and No end date controls. Shortcuts use the routine’s timezone and calendar date; next month clamps to the last day when needed. The end date is inclusive in that timezone, including daylight-saving transitions. Legacy records without the field remain unlimited.

The host validates and persists the end date, excludes future occurrences beyond it, and disables expired routines with `next_due=0`. The final eligible occurrence still runs even when there is no subsequent occurrence. Wake/restart checks and pre-dispatch validation prevent automatic launches after expiry. Existing active runs and history are preserved. The UI shows Ended; extending/clearing the date and enabling the routine allows future runs. Explicit Run now remains available and does not re-enable expired routines.

Host hello advertises `schedule_end_dates`. The native save command checks this capability whenever an end date is supplied, refusing older hosts that would otherwise discard the unknown field. The user is told to let active work finish for the host update, then save again. No accounts, credentials, or live provider turns are used as test fixtures.

### Connection editing availability

Rename remains available while agents run or a background-host update is pending; it changes only connection display metadata. Remove connection always opens its confirmation in the native app. The confirmation explains that active work and pending sign-in for that connection will be stopped, while CLI credentials, history, and pinned thread/schedule bindings are retained. On confirmation, removal first uses the existing host command; if active work prevents it, the UI reads the current host snapshot, cancels that connection’s sign-in, requests normal stops for its active threads only, and retries removal for up to ten seconds while supervisors finish. Backend removal guards remain authoritative. Failures are shown without deleting the connection or switching accounts. Browser previews and in-progress mutations retain their existing disabled controls.

### Remaining-capacity circles

Account quota displays share a remaining-capacity ring: full at 100% left and empty at 0% left. The footer selects the most limiting known window; Accounts & usage displays a circle for every reported window instead of consumed-capacity bars. Percentages, tooltips, and accessibility values all describe capacity left. Low remaining capacity (10% or below) is amber; stale footer values remain marked last known. Unknown or non-finite usage never becomes a numeric zero. Conversation context circles on desktop and companion web views also represent context capacity left, with an explicit “Context …% left” label; detailed token counts retain their original meaning. The separate sample-profile preview already represents remaining capacity and retains that convention.

### Routine stopping conditions: date or run count

The macOS Stops selector offers No limit, On a specific date, or After a number of runs. Date and count are mutually exclusive; existing end-date routines retain their date. `max_runs` is an optional positive integer (absent/null means unlimited). The count is a lifetime total across every schedule entry, including prior runs and explicit Run now launches. An occurrence with a bound session counts once, even if it later fails, is interrupted, or its conversation is deleted. Skipped/missed/blocked occurrences without a session do not count. Uncertain crash-time dispatches conservatively reserve a slot until reconciled, preventing an accidental extra launch.

The host owns history and restores it from storage before evaluating edits; client-supplied or stale run history cannot reset a cap. Save, resume, scheduler recovery, pre-dispatch, and post-launch checks enforce the limit. The routine disables with next_due=0 when the limit is reached; the last running conversation is allowed to finish. Manual Run now is blocked at a run limit (unlike an expired date). Increase or clear the limit, and enable the routine for further automatic runs. Progress updates from snapshots without replacing unsaved configuration drafts.

Host hello advertises `schedule_run_limits`. The native save transport refuses a run limit if the current host lacks this capability, instead of allowing older serde code to ignore it. Source tests use isolated data and injected transports, without accounts, credentials, or live provider turns.

### Routine notifications and conversation destination

The macOS routine editor offers “Start each run in a new chat” (on by default). Turning it off reveals “Runs in”, a picker of established, available conversations in the selected project. Choosing a thread binds its existing account and working folder, disables the account picker, and hides Separate worktree. The host validates that binding again at dispatch; unavailable/deleted/archived threads block the run rather than creating a replacement. Busy threads skip the occurrence. New-chat routines retain their existing behavior. Opening a saved routine with no run history can show its selected destination conversation.

Notifications offers Important updates only, Every completed run and issues, or Off. New routines default to Important; existing routines without a preference remain Off. Important includes actionable findings, meaningful changes, failures, blocked/uncertain launches, and requests for approval or input. The host appends a notification instruction to the agent prompt (without changing the visible task): a successful check without important findings must end with exactly “No important updates.” Quiet successes remain in chat without notification. Other nonempty final replies notify; importance is agent judgment, not a deterministic classifier. All mode also reports quiet completions. Both enabled modes notify attention states; skipped/missed occurrences stay in history.

Each occurrence keeps its own final state and notification records even when several occurrences reuse one conversation. Completed outcomes are frozen before later turns replace thread state. Notification IDs deduplicate repeated observation of the same run/state. The detached macOS host polls a persisted notification outbox and submits notifications using the app identity even when the GUI is closed. Successful delivery acknowledges the record; failures retry. A crash between system delivery and acknowledgement may repeat a notification. macOS notification settings still govern presentation. Turning notifications Off cancels pending records; editing a routine does not replay old completed runs.

Host hello advertises schedule_delivery. The native transport refuses these settings on an older active host so it cannot silently discard them. Focused tests use synthetic unconnected sessions and injected transports; no accounts, credentials, live turns, or real notification delivery are used as fixtures.

## Local MCP bridge for agent harnesses (2026-11-05)

The native executable accepts `--mcp [workspace-root [project-id [thread-id]]]` before GUI initialization and serves newline-delimited MCP JSON-RPC on stdin/stdout. The default root is the user's standard macOS Freddai workspace. No TCP service, credentials or second scheduler is introduced. `freddai_context` reads host readiness even when an older host is still busy; other tools dispatch through the owner-only host socket's `mcp_tool` allowlist. The host advertises `mcp_bridge: 1` in hello. An old host returns an actionable update-needed tool error rather than silently dropping commands.

Nine tools expose context, project/agent lists, paginated thread summaries/messages, paginated schedule configuration/recent history, schedule create/update/action, and explicit new-task launch. Account lists contain only ID/name/provider. Unknown tools/fields, invalid types/ranges and unauthorized host-command names are rejected. No login, account mutation, permission response, arbitrary host invoke or permission-bypass parameter exists. New routines default to paused; updates preserve unspecified settings and host-owned run history. Saves return the actual persisted schedule ID through `save_schedule_returning`, while the existing UI save API remains compatible. New-task requests require a stable retry key and use Ask permission mode. These are local trusted-client tools, not a project sandbox; context IDs provide defaults. Task and schedule mutations still require user intent, per tool instructions and provider approval policy.

One shared server list is injected session-locally: Codex CLI TOML overrides, Claude's merged `--mcp-config`, and ACP `session/new`/`session/load` for Gemini, Antigravity, Grok, OpenCode and Cursor. Both the Freddai bridge and bundled computer-use helper are supplied; existing user MCP configuration and sign-ins are preserved. Auth probes continue to create tool-free ACP sessions. Existing turns retain their tool catalog; the GUI does not force-upgrade a busy host. After active work settles, the app upgrades the host and subsequent new/resumed turns obtain the tools. Protocol/provider wiring is tested offline; per-provider live discovery is not implied.

Testing instructions and external-client JSON configuration: [MCP bridge](mcp-bridge.md). Protocol references: [MCP lifecycle](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle), [MCP tools](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/server/tools), and [ACP session setup](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/session-setup).

### Experimental conversation status display

Settings → Thread display includes an off-by-default, device-local “Ignore unread status (experimental)” switch. With it enabled, finished replies display as Done immediately and participate in recent/older bucketing without requiring a visit or Mark as read. Status filters use Working, Needs you, and Done wording; Mark as read is hidden. Approvals, failures, and interruptions retain their actionable status; viewed failures still count toward attention. The completed-thread hiding setting explains the immediate move to recent/older threads. This is a presentation preference only: it does not bulk-mark replies as read or alter host/phone read cursors. Normal viewing still records read history, and disabling the experiment restores the usual indicators from that history. Existing behavior remains the default.

### Project thread pins

Threads can be pinned or unpinned from their sidebar context menu or conversation actions. Pins persist on the desktop device by thread identity and follow project moves. Within each project, pinned threads appear first (newest first among pins) and remain visible when read threads are hidden, without consuming the recent-finished allowance. Flat view also places pins first. Search, status filters, project collapse, archive and deletion visibility still apply. Pinning is available during running work and pending host upgrades; it does not stop the thread, pause its queue, or change its recency. There is no global starred section.

Desktop pins are stored as a local presentation preference, shared between app windows, and can be toggled while work is running or a host upgrade is pending. Explicit local pin/unpin choices override legacy host pin metadata. Thread action menus include icons.

Companion extension: the Mac publishes `ignoreUnread` with its shared thread-list presentation settings. The companion follows it on each snapshot in web rows, dashboard thread fading, filters, recent/older grouping, and the iOS/Android native drawer unread flags and status options. Older settings files default to false. Phone viewing continues to record actual read history independently of the presentation projection. Turning the Mac toggle off restores normal unread behavior on the companion on its next snapshot.

Desktop reply drafts: unsent text and staged attachment chips are retained by thread in memory for the app session, including when switching threads, visiting Home/Settings, or closing a pane. Returning to the thread restores its composer. Sending clears that thread’s composer; existing failure recovery restores rejected input. Normal quit/relaunch is outside this temporary retention contract; the existing updater text-draft handoff remains supported.

### Computer-use input protection (November 5, 2026)

Accessible press/click, explicit value edits and visual hover use the background path even if an agent requests foreground mode. No automatic fallback occurs. Foreground-only input is refused while keyboard/pointer activity occurred in the preceding two seconds or a mouse button is held. An idle focused editable control does not block explicitly authorized foreground control: composer focus persists after sending approval and is not evidence of ongoing typing. This activity check does not detect microphone use. Background actions still refuse to edit the person's current app.

For unavoidable explicitly authorized foreground input, the helper snapshots the frontmost app, focused window/control and pointer without reading draft contents. After dispatch (and on errors), it attempts to restore focus before verification capture. It does not restore if the user has switched to another app; the pointer is restored only if it remains at the injected position. This is best effort, not a guarantee against simultaneous input or a way to resume interrupted dictation. Apps that self-activate during background actions/navigation are blocked for that helper session and previous focus restoration is attempted. Existing one-use observations and interprocess action locking remain.

### Android companion parity (2026-11-05)

Android now advertises the same native dashboard capability as iOS: a single native scroll view contains the 240dp virtual workspace, independently expandable cached daily summaries, project cards and Schedules. Generate/Refresh remains an explicit action through the shared summary client. The existing WebView is reparented for expanded Office, conversation and pairing screens rather than recreated. Project titles share their row with compact new-thread and microphone controls; the coding-agent selector sits beside Your projects. Tablet grids retain the responsive one/two/three-column rules and large text falls back to one column.

Android tablets with a smallest window dimension of at least 600dp show a persistent 320dp thread sidebar in landscape when the window is at least 900dp wide. Compact and portrait windows retain the overlay drawer. Selecting a thread keeps the persistent sidebar visible. Reparenting is deferred until native layout traversal completes; rotation must preserve the composer and must not mutate DrawerLayout children during its layout pass.

The Android native composer now records hold/release dictation, with accessibility start/finish activation, elapsed feedback, haptics, two-minute/9MiB limits and sub-300ms rejection. Releasing before permission completion, moving away, cancellation, navigation, backgrounding and microphone errors discard the capture. Completed recordings reuse authenticated Mac transcription; the shared composer path appends text and honors the existing submit flag, including staged attachments. The dashboard microphone continues to create a draft without submitting. No provider or account setup is added.

Attachments offer Photos, Files and Camera (when a system camera is available). Camera capture delegates to Android's camera app using the existing app FileProvider; cancelled/failed captures are discarded. All sources retain the 20-file, 25MiB/file and 100MiB/message limits. Image chips use sampled thumbnails, removable accessible controls and off-main-thread decoding. Native drawer rows use a fixed indicator slot, reduced-motion-aware running pulse, unread emphasis and single-line titles; project headers have compact adjacent add actions. Conversation headers show project and running/needs-you/failed status. Restored attachment chips ignore missing or unmanaged files. Existing Android Keystore pairing storage and runtime Mac enrollment are unchanged.

Verification is recorded below after builds and emulator acceptance complete. Physical Android pairing, relay, camera hardware, real microphone transcription and live-provider turns require separate verification and are not inferred from offline checks.

### Windows desktop runtime — November 5, 2026

Windows replaces the non-Unix host rejection with owner-restricted local named pipes, preserving the existing JSON RPC, workspace lock, persistence, queue and schedule behavior. Native startup caches a Windows executable, logs host errors under app data, and reconnects after GUI exit/reopen. Per-user login startup uses the Windows Run registry key. Agent/metadata/login child processes use kill-on-close Job Objects; agent trees are assigned while suspended before they can launch descendants. CLI discovery supports `.exe`/`.cmd`/`.bat`/`.com`, Windows PATH and USERPROFILE; interactive Claude/Gemini login opens PowerShell with literal arguments and isolated account environment. The integrated terminal uses PowerShell/ConPTY. File listing keeps forward-slash project-relative paths on all platforms.

Mac-only voice and keep-awake controls are gated on Windows. Phone hosting and computer-use helpers retain explicit platform limits. No credentials, connected accounts or live provider turns are used as fixtures. The Windows workflow validates the host, core protocol behavior, process-tree cleanup, NSIS installation, WebView2 workspace rendering and GUI restart independently from signing. The installer remains unsigned and is not automatically published to the website.

Initial regression evidence: `scripts/tests/windows-host.ps1` failed on the previous Windows build with “Background host currently requires macOS or Linux”; it passes with named-pipe transport. Full Windows core validation passed after correcting path separators and Unix-only fixture assumptions. Final installation/UI and Mac delivery results are recorded below after completion.

### Recurring-task permissions and complete history (2026-11-05)

Recurring-task configuration exposes Full access using the existing LaunchOptions permission_mode (bypass/ask), supported by Codex and Claude. New schedules for supported agents default to full access. A scheduled turn in an existing thread applies its explicit permission mode while preserving the thread's model, effort and working-folder controls; absent modes retain legacy behavior. Changing the setting affects the next turn, not an already pending approval. Existing user routines were explicitly updated through the owning host's save_schedule API at the user's request; no provider turns were launched.

Each task card always shows started-run and distinct-thread counts, including tasks without a run cap. The expandable history displays every stored occurrence, newest first, with status, date, available thread title and Open thread. The former 30-row display truncation is removed. Repeated runs in one chat count as multiple runs and one thread; unsuccessful attempts without a thread do not count as started runs.

## Desktop 0.1.1 distribution

Version 0.1.1 packages the accumulated workspace changes through the existing signed and notarized universal macOS/Linux release workflow. The existing updater signing key is configured in Actions; published installer links remain driven by stable GitHub release metadata. Local delivery uses `pnpm native:install`. Release, download, native launch and live-provider verification are tracked separately in `docs/releases.md`.

Queued follow-up handoff (2026-11-05): the shared host dispatches eligible FIFO messages immediately after the previous provider process is cleaned up and its running lease is removed. This works independently of the companion connection, foreground screen and two-second schedule timer. Successful completed threads retain their enabled queue across idle host upgrades/restarts; explicit pauses and dispatch errors remain paused, and interrupted, failed, missing, archived or deleted threads require resume. Idle upgrade takes the FIFO lock so shutdown cannot race an already-selected queue dispatch. This host behavior serves desktop, iPhone/iPad and Android without a companion UI change. It does not automatically resume an interrupted provider turn or unpause previously paused user queues.

Regression reproduction: `cargo test -p freddai-core completed_queue_survives_idle_host_upgrade -- --nocapture` failed on the former unconditional restart pause, then passed after preserving completed-thread queue intent. Offline fixtures use no account, credentials or provider turns and verify attempted dispatch via the expected missing-project error, retained FIFO identity, manual pauses, failures and interrupted-state recovery. Physical companion and live-provider execution remain unverified.

Queue handoff delivery verification: Source/spec updated; the restart regression was observed failing before the fix. Tests: all 156 core unit tests, two host integration checks (two helper tests intentionally ignored), seven import integration checks and all 391 frontend tests passed. Build: `pnpm native:install` completed frontend TypeScript/production and Rust release builds. Signature: existing Developer ID team BSH2UEDLU5; strict/deep installed verification passed. Install: `/Applications/Freddai.app` matches built executable SHA-256 `2e47e672533287fa7c264c0f6793410619cbecbda825ec99ce9e8afd680c53a9`. Launch: normal AppleScript quit and approved computer_launch relaunched GUI PID 80346; lsof maps the installed executable. The background host retained the older cached build while five existing threads were active, confirmed through read-only host hello/snapshot. The host-side fix therefore remains pending the normal idle upgrade; no forced host restart, provider turn, account mutation or live queue submission was used for validation. GUI restart did not terminate the host; interrupted-task or reboot recovery was not induced. No companion reinstall is required for this host-only change; physical-device queue execution remains unverified.

### November 5, 2026 — Preserve navigation during thread creation

On both companion and desktop, acknowledging a new-thread send may open the resulting conversation only if no newer navigation has been requested since Send. Choosing another thread, Home, a fresh composer, Settings, a schedule, or a git pane takes precedence over a delayed acknowledgment, including navigation away and back. The created thread and its pending message remain tracked normally. If the user stays on the originating composer, successful creation still opens the conversation. Desktop send completion must also preserve a newer Settings/dialog selection rather than closing it. Regression coverage drives the actual app send handlers with a deferred host acknowledgment; no live account, credentials, or provider turns are used.

Navigation-fix verification: source changes are limited to the two app send paths and a shared navigation-revision hook; pre-existing runtime/video work was preserved. The companion regression failed before the guard (`Transcribed new task` replaced `Old thread`); disabling the desktop guard reproduced three failures for thread/Home/Settings navigation. With the guards restored, all 397 tests across 61 files and all workspace typechecks passed; `git diff --check` passed. `pnpm native:install` rebuilt the release app, signed it with Denis Duvauchelle’s existing Developer ID (BSH2UEDLU5), strictly verified its signature, and installed `/Applications/Freddai.app`. A normal GUI quit and native-control launch produced PID 90215 mapping the installed executable; the loaded UI and thread navigation were observed. Background host PID 33761 remained running and the four previously active threads retained their statuses (three running, one waiting); no interrupted-task or reboot recovery was induced. The physical iOS archive was rebuilt and strictly signature-verified with the existing Apple Development identity, installed on the connected DiPhone, and launched; device process PID 1404 maps the newly installed bundle container `1A2BA819-E720-43DF-B7A0-EF975FEBE961`. Live-provider verification: no account/sign-in changes or live message/voice turns were made; the delayed acknowledgment race was verified in isolated app-component tests, not by sending work through the user's providers.

Release certificate compatibility: first-time Mac companion setup uses a short certificate common name and retains the complete Mac `.local` hostname in the TLS subject alternative name. This permits long valid local hostnames without exceeding X.509 common-name limits; existing identities remain unchanged. The regression exercises actual certificate generation and checks the full hostname in the resulting certificate.

## Published desktop 0.1.1 acceptance

The published v0.1.1 release uses native source `fc19bb9`. It includes the signed and notarized universal Mac DMG, signed Mac/Linux updater artifacts, Linux AppImage/Debian packages and a deliberately published unsigned Windows beta installer. Empty queue automatic-pause correction (2026-11-05): failed/interrupted turn cleanup and host startup must not create a pause on an empty FIFO. Otherwise a later phone submission inherits an automatic pause even after a subsequent successful turn. Existing explicit pause/stop intent and safety pauses for actual queued messages remain unchanged. Regression `empty_queue_does_not_gain_an_automatic_pause` failed with `empty completion: failed` before the correction; it checks completion, restart, and subsequent dispatch without any accounts or provider processes. The observed Lucy thread was already done with an empty, enabled queue when inspected; its exact historical pause cause cannot be reconstructed from that snapshot. Read-only host hello confirmed an older cached host remained active, so previous installation alone had not delivered the earlier handoff correction to the serving host.


### Generated image and PDF previews

Chat on macOS and the native iOS/Android companion offers in-chat preview buttons for Markdown image links and file links to PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP and PDF outputs, including absolute, project-relative, `file://` and `sandbox:` paths. Previews load on demand from the Mac; local filesystem links are never passed to Finder on a phone. The paired companion uses the existing encrypted transport in bounded 192 KiB chunks. Reads require an existing, nonarchived thread and the exact file reference in its transcript, accept regular files up to 25 MiB, and validate supported content signatures. Missing files, unsupported content and disconnected Macs produce visible errors. External HTTP(S) links retain existing browser behavior. Both companion binary and Mac listener must be updated to use this feature. PDF rendering uses the platform webview's PDF support; no live-provider turns are needed to load a preview.

**Local file links and richer previews (2026-11-06).** Every non-web link that looks like a local path (relative, absolute, `file://`, `sandbox:`) renders as a button, never dead text. The reader also previews `.html/.htm` (UTF-8 required; shown in an `<iframe srcdoc>` with `sandbox=""`, so no scripts, same-origin or navigation), `.md`, `.txt`, `.log`, `.csv`, `.json` (UTF-8 required, 5 MiB maximum; Markdown rendered, others in a scrollable block) and `.svg` (shown via `<img>`). Image/PDF extensions still require matching content signatures. Other file types return metadata only (`previewable:false`). Every response carries the resolved absolute `path`; the dialog shows it with Copy path and, in the Mac app only, Open in default app and Reveal in Finder (`opener:allow-open-path` scoped to `$HOME`, `/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, `/private/var/folders`; plus `opener:allow-reveal-item-in-dir`). The thread-reference, working-directory resolution, size and read-only chunk rules are unchanged. Companion shows Copy path only.

### Embedded browser sessions — November 6, 2026

The macOS desktop workspace opens clicked HTTP(S) conversation links in a right-hand native browser pane. The Browser button beside the terminal control in each thread header opens/closes that thread’s pane; the address bar supports explicit navigation and Back, Forward, Reload, Close and Open externally. Modifier-clicks retain external opening. Generated-file previews and mobile links retain their existing behavior. A new link navigates the active session; switching sessions or reopening the pane retains an existing session's current page. Browsing never launches an agent turn.

Browser visibility, last address and selected profile are remembered separately for each thread while the workspace is running. Switching threads hides the previous native view and restores the destination thread’s browser only if it was open. Split panes each own their browser. Named profile preferences and native website stores are scoped by thread; existing global stores are left intact. Personal is the default browser session for each thread. New session creates a named profile (for example Admin, User or Work); the session selector switches between independent, persistent WebKit website data stores. Profile IDs/names are saved in app preferences, and each profile's stable store ID is separate from Freddai's own webview. Users sign in directly on the site once per profile. This is independent of provider subscriptions and account setup. No credentials are read, copied or inserted by Freddai. Isolation requires macOS 14+; older systems receive an explicit error and can open links externally. These profiles are not automatically shared with external browsers or agent browser tools. Automatic social posting and agent control of these stores are not implemented by this change.

Browser webviews receive no remote IPC capabilities, reject non-HTTP(S) navigation, and deny popup windows. Popup-dependent authentication, downloads and custom-scheme authentication handoffs are not supported in this initial version. Application dialogs hide the child webview while open; geometry follows the browser surface as the workspace changes size. Closing the pane hides the webview without clearing its store. No session deletion/clear-data control is supplied yet.

Source verification: existing user changes preserved. All 403 app tests and workspace typechecks passed before the final navigation refinement. A real native WebKit harness verified two independent stores using a non-authentication preference cookie and verified that the same profile retained its store after webview recreation. It used no connected account, credential or provider turn. Final checks, build, signature, installation and launch results follow below.

### Explicit conversation link actions — November 6, 2026

HTTP(S) links now display an Open in side panel icon button whenever the desktop browser context is available, plus an Open in default browser icon button. Both buttons have tooltips and destination-specific accessible names. Clicking the link itself retains side-panel opening on desktop, with modifier-click retaining external opening. Mobile and standalone transcripts offer external opening without an unavailable side-panel control. Generated local PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP images and PDFs continue to open in the existing on-demand file preview (25 MB maximum); this does not add arbitrary-format previews or detect plain-text file mentions. These file previews use the connected Mac's existing referenced-file reader.

Memory usage, November 6, 2026: transcript text uses copy-on-write shared buffers so runtime/session snapshots and the debounced persister do not duplicate unchanged tool output. Editing a message detaches its buffer; workspace JSON and host wire formats remain unchanged, preserving full history. Workspace persistence and Unix host replies stream through buffered writers instead of allocating a second full serialized workspace/reply. Host RPC unwraps replies by ownership rather than cloning their result. Collapsed tool-activity groups mount no calls; individual closed calls mount no output or scroll areas, releasing those children on close while keeping full output available on expansion. Threads without background-task launch IDs skip tool-JSON parsing for task placement. The synthetic `scripts/tests/snapshot-memory.py <binary>` benchmark uses an isolated account-free workspace and six full snapshot requests; it never starts a provider turn.

Projects settings ordering: Settings → Projects displays the same saved order as the grouped sidebar and Home. Each project row has a pointer drag handle, insertion marker, and Alt+Up/Down keyboard reordering. Drops persist through the existing local project-order preference; editing a project remains a separate button. New projects remain included.

Lean desktop memory, November 6, 2026: every desktop window sends its currently visible thread IDs with snapshot requests. Home/new-thread views retain only user/assistant/notice history and lightweight thread metadata; tools/reasoning are hydrated for visible split panes or standalone thread windows. Scope changes invalidate the snapshot cursor, fetch immediately and discard in-flight responses for a previous scope. Human conversation text remains available for titles, outgoing acknowledgements, forks and schedule creation. Large tool messages (>4 KiB) cross the desktop boundary as metadata plus a byte count and content hash; full raw output stays intact in workspace storage and is read on disclosure in UTF-8-safe pages of at most 64 KiB. Only one page is retained, released on close/navigation; stale content hashes fail visibly instead of combining different output versions. Todo/sub-agent metadata remains available in compact snapshots. Older hosts retain full-session protocol compatibility and are compacted in the native bridge before reaching WebKit. New hosts compact before serialization. Generated-file preview reads use the selected conversation directly instead of transferring the full workspace for each file chunk, and closing a preview clears its retained data URL.

Reference research: Theo's T3 Code, commit `6577c6b6185167c0e502fa01f9e8e8f2dee6ff25`, separates thread shells/details (`apps/web/src/state/threads.ts`) and bounds caches by entry count and bytes (`apps/web/src/lib/lruCache.ts`, `components/chat/MarkdownCodeBlock.tsx`: 500 entries / 50 MiB). These are architectural references; no T3 code was copied. Freddai uses its existing React/Tauri/Rust structure and keeps deferred tool output uncached after closing. Remaining larger opportunities are disk-backed historical tool bodies in the host, scoped companion-to-host reads, and viewport windowing for unusually long human-message transcripts; these require separate behavior/scroll/storage validation.

Compatibility transport follow-up: Unix desktop snapshot RPC decodes the host JSON stream message-by-message, compacting large tool bodies before retaining the next message and then removing hidden tool/reasoning records per thread. This avoids both a full wire-line string and a full unprojected JSON tree even while an older host remains active. Snapshot decoding stays bounded by the existing maximum response size and socket timeout; malformed/truncated replies and host errors remain visible. Non-desktop RPC and persisted history retain their existing full formats. Tests compare streamed and eager projection, verify field-order independence, preserve host errors and reject truncated JSON.

Switching threads, projects, Home, settings, or closing a pane does not clear drafts. Successful send clears only the submitted context; failed sends retain it. Drafts are local to this installation, not synced to a provider. Attachment persistence remains separate from text persistence.

Reordering preserves workspace IDs, colors, membership and the selected workspace. Editing and reordering share the existing disabled state.

- A project may belong to multiple workspaces. Settings provides draggable project chips and an Add project button for each workspace. Membership removal and workspace deletion preserve projects, threads, and project order.

All includes every project; a workspace scopes the project picker and grouped/flat thread lists, including empty projects. Changing workspace resets the project filter and older-page expansion. The selector uses the same styling as the project/status filters, without a left bar, and is hidden when no workspaces are configured.

- If the running host predates workspace support, editing is disabled until its pending update completes; Settings explains the update banner's Restart now option. This prevents older hosts from silently dropping workspace changes while active threads delay their update.

### Titlebar thread statistics

Exactly two thread statistics appear beside the top-level New thread button: Today (non-deleted threads created or active on the current local calendar day, counted once per thread using their latest update) and Lifetime (all retained threads, including archived and soft-deleted threads). Both follow the workspace and project selection, independent of search/status filters. There is no separate filtered thread count. Tooltips explain the counts. Both counts remain visible at narrow widths.

### In-app color selection

Workspace, project, and account colors use shared in-app swatches and an editable six-digit hex field. Swatches apply on selection; valid custom hex applies on blur or Enter; invalid drafts revert on blur. Enter never submits the surrounding creation form. These controls do not use HTML color inputs or open the native macOS color panel. This avoids the WebKit `WKColorPopoverMac` → AppKit `NSColorPanel` crash path captured in incident 741C0900-8847-4E50-B68F-C0FF49881BA7 (November 6, 2026). The precise native appearance-observer defect is not established by that stack alone. Existing stored colors and save paths are retained.

## Revision: large conversations over the Internet relay (2026-11-07)

Confirmed failure: the installed companion listener repeatedly uploaded approximately 61 MB encrypted responses and received HTTP 413 from the existing Railway relay, whose response limit is 16 MiB. A synthetic selected-conversation regression through host snapshot projection reproduced a 62,401,181-byte envelope. Relay health and a small synthetic encrypted round trip passed; missing hosting was not the cause.

Mobile encrypted requests now advertise `acceptEncoding: "deflate-v1"`. Only an authenticated request can opt into response compression. The Mac serializes the response, compresses it with raw DEFLATE when beneficial, prefixes a version marker, and then encrypts the complete payload using the existing AES-GCM device/direction/request binding and fresh nonce. The relay receives the unchanged opaque nonce/ciphertext schema and needs no deployment or increased limits. Desktop/mobile decoders authenticate before decoding and bound decompression to 64 MiB. Legacy peers keep uncompressed JSON. Oversized legacy/incompressible responses produce a small authenticated error instructing the user to check the conversation before retrying an action, rather than retrying a guaranteed 413. A mutation may already have executed; no command is automatically replayed.

This preserves complete selected conversation content and the existing local/Internet fallback, device pairing, credentials, revocation, expiry and replay protections. Both the Mac listener and phone need this update to negotiate compression. Source tests cover full synthetic large-history round trips, optional explicitly enabled public-relay round trips using synthetic keys only, legacy responses, capability spoofing, authenticated context, and bounded decompression. Delivery verification follows below.

Verification: all 34 targeted native companion tests passed, as did the Node relay test and `git diff --check`. The explicit synthetic public-Railway test preserved a 31,200,506-byte plaintext response in a 421,417-byte encrypted wire envelope; the pre-fix regression failed at 62,401,181 wire bytes. No real device credentials or provider turns were used as fixtures. Build: `pnpm native:install` completed frontend/TypeScript and native production compilation; the universal iOS debug archive also built. Signature: existing Developer ID macOS signing and strict/deep installed verification passed; strict/deep iOS archive verification passed. Install: macOS installed at `/Applications/Freddai.app`; built/installed executable SHA-256 both `d957ed1d3aec9d332336c7fdf9acc3ea11f6377cc1b5f3e0e0529ead16b8f376`. iOS installed on DiPhone and 2022 iPad mini. Launch: normal Mac Quit and approved native launch produced GUI PID 12804, mapped to the installed binary; standalone listener restarted normally as PID 15203. DiPhone launched PID 2195 from the new installed container `BC8B7DEF-5B69-4C12-8B4F-188329180084`. iPad launch was denied because the device was locked; unlock it and open Freddai. Native desktop screenshot inspection failed with ScreenCaptureKit -3811; process/window evidence verifies launch, not visual behavior. Existing agent watchers 66638, 88810 and 92129 remained alive; no recovery event was induced. Live-provider verification: not performed. Actual phone-over-cellular verification remains pending the user's test; local phone snapshots resumed successfully after launch. Railway deployment/billing and paired-device registry were not changed.

## Recent threads view (desktop sidebar and phone companion)

A segmented "Projects | Recent" toggle sits above the thread search. Projects (default) is the existing grouped list. Recent is one flat list of threads from all projects, newest first by the same activity key as `compareThreadRecency` (`last_user_message_at`, falling back to `updated`), excluding archived and deleted threads. It still honours the search, workspace, project and status filters. Each row is the normal thread row (status dot, unread/needs-you indicators) plus a truncating project name in the project's colour. Show more adds a page; Show less returns to the first page and appears only when expanded; Show more is hidden when nothing remains. Empty state: "No recent threads". Clicking a row opens the thread exactly as in the project view. The paging position is not persisted; the chosen view is (desktop `localStorage` key `freddai.threadNavView`, phone `freddai.companion.threadNavView`).

Page sizes are parameters of the shared pure helper `selectRecentThreads(threads, pages, paging)` in `packages/app/src/recentThreads.ts`: the desktop sidebar uses `RECENT_PAGING` (5, then +10 per click); the phone companion's Recent view uses `COMPANION_RECENT_PAGING` (25, then +25 per click). The toggle and footer are in `packages/app/src/RecentThreadsControls.tsx`.

Companion Projects view, bottom "Recent" section: below the project list, always visible without switching modes, the companion shows a "Recent" section with the 5 most recent threads (same ordering, exclusions and filters; same row style: title, project name tag, status dot). Show more expands it inline by 10 (then Show less returns to 5). The section is hidden when there are no threads. It pages independently of the Recent view; both reset to the first page when search, project/status filter or workspace changes. It applies to the web companion list and the native iOS and Android drawers. Desktop is unchanged.

Native shell drawer (iOS `ThreadDrawerViewController`, Android `NativeShellPlugin`): the same toggle (UISegmentedControl / two-button row) sits under the title. JS owns the choice (`freddai.companion.threadNavView`) and both lists: `buildThreadsModel` adds `view`, `recent {threads, remaining, expanded}` (Recent view, 25/+25) and `recentSection` (bottom section, 5/+10), rows carrying `projectLabel`/`projectColor`, all from `selectRecentThreads`, so Swift/Kotlin never re-implement ordering or paging. Native emits `setView {view}` and `recentPage {action: more|less, section?: "bottom"}` events; the toggle flips optimistically until JS echoes the view back. Tapping any Recent row fires the same `selectThread` event as the project view. Android was compiled but not run on an emulator.

Verification: unit tests for the helper (page sizes per caller), desktop and companion component tests (25/+25 Recent view, bottom section present/paging/hidden, paging reset), native payload/event wiring tests, `pnpm -r typecheck`, and the iOS simulator UI test `testDrawerProjectsRecentToggle` (fixture client, Projects view scrolled to the bottom section and the 25-row Recent view in light and dark; `VITE_COMPANION_FIXTURE_MANY=1` adds 30 extra fixture threads for the screenshots).


### Mobile project reordering — November 7, 2026

In the Projects drawer, touch and hold a project heading on iOS or its drag handle on Android, then drop above or below another project to move the whole group. The web fallback provides a touch/pointer handle and Alt+Up/Down keyboard moves. Thread ordering and collapsed groups remain intact. Reordering requires a connected Mac that advertises support; Recent and ungrouped lists do not expose project moves.

The paired transport accepts only a project ID, target project ID and before/after position. The Mac validates both IDs and merges the move into its complete order, preserving projects hidden by search, workspace or status filters. Order is saved atomically in a separate companion presentation file, leaving other sidebar settings untouched. Companion snapshots prefer this order; the desktop imports it on launch and every two seconds and publishes explicit desktop moves back to the same file. Failed mobile saves retain the previous order and show the error. No offline reorder queue is provided.
