### Companion microphone continuity (2026-11-07)

The native companion retains the last confirmed Mac dictation readiness across connection interruptions and failed readiness refreshes. An explicit disabled/not-ready response still hides the microphone; initial unknown readiness remains hidden. Loading, running and stopping do not remove the microphone. Transcripts completing while a thread is busy, an action/upload is pending, the conversation is loading, or the connection is unavailable append to the composer without submitting, allowing the next message to be drafted. Idle, ready conversations and New thread retain release-to-send behavior. Mac transcription still requires connectivity; audio is not queued offline.

Verification: app TypeScript and 43 native-home/native-shell tests passed, including a regression that failed before the fix for a lost readiness refresh and verifies draft-only transcription while running. Native build/install/launch and phone delivery are recorded separately below.

Delivery verification: `pnpm native:install` completed macOS release build, existing Developer ID signing (BSH2UEDLU5), strict verification and `/Applications/Freddai.app` installation. Built/installed executable SHA-256 matched `ffe4b63e624c680f1dc41c8c5a5ce22b8a7effa396e7066fb2176c82d30b2b3f`. Normal Apple Events quit and approved computer_launch started PID 3362, whose mapped executable is the installed bundle. Host PID 19587 and existing watchers 66638/90182/94903 survived. Runtime logs show the relaunched GUI reading host snapshots and phone snapshot/composer requests returning 200. Desktop visual verification is incomplete: computer observation returned a blank window and could not uniquely match accessibility identity; UI recovery is not claimed. The final iOS debug archive passed strict signature verification, installed on paired DiPhone (00008140-00144DE80A7B001C), and devicectl confirmed launch. No real microphone capture, live transcription or provider turn was used as a fixture; on-phone microphone behavior remains unverified. No reboot recovery claim.

### Companion connection interruption and durable outgoing queue

Companion new-thread and reply sends are saved on the device before submission. A clock-marked queued row appears in the thread drawer and a device outbox lists pending messages with a cancel action. Cached, previously confirmed project/thread readiness permits enqueueing while disconnected; initial unknown targets cannot be invented. The composer remains available while delivery is pending. Recognized network failures retry in FIFO order at five-second intervals while the app is running and connectivity is healthy enough to attempt delivery. Foreground and online events trigger immediate connection checks. Suspended mobile apps resume delivery when reopened; continuous execution while iOS/Android suspends the process is not promised.

The outbox survives webview/app restarts and retains stable request IDs through lost acknowledgments, using the host's existing new-thread/reply deduplication. Native attachment paths and send options are retained until acknowledgment; partially uploaded files may upload again after process restart. Native attachment bytes currently live in the native temporary/cache directory and can be purged by the OS; unavailable attachments retain the message with a visible error. Validation/authentication failures stay in the outbox with an explicit Retry or Cancel action rather than retrying indefinitely or overwriting the next draft. A blocked FIFO head must be retried or cancelled to continue delivery. Cancellation is disabled during an in-flight delivery, since an already received request cannot be recalled. A completed pairing starts a new outbox namespace so an old Mac's messages cannot be submitted to the new pairing; the prior namespace remains stored. Queue storage failure prevents acceptance and preserves the draft.

Relay response expiry describes an unanswered request, without asserting that the Mac is offline or the phone was revoked. A relay request failure alone cannot identify whether the interruption occurred on cellular, at the relay, or between the relay and Mac.


### Companion composer recording: swipe up to send (2026-11-07)

Supersedes release-to-send for the native conversation/New thread composer on iOS, iPadOS and Android. Hold the microphone to record; elapsed m:ss appears above “Swipe up to send ↑” with a live audio waveform beside the timer. The microphone follows the upward drag. A release at least 64pt/dp above the initial hold and within 96pt/dp horizontally sends through the existing transcription/submission path. The hint changes to “Release to send ↑” with a threshold haptic. Dragging back down restores cancellation. Release without that upward gesture, OS gesture cancellation, backgrounding, hidden composer and recording limits discard audio without transcription or submission. Permission completion cannot revive a released gesture. Existing typed drafts, attachments and busy-thread draft-only transcription remain intact. Accessibility offers explicit send and cancel alternatives. Dashboard dictation retains its existing draft-only interaction.

### Visible turn controls (macOS, iOS, Android)

The conversation activity row beside the agent’s working/starting/waiting status exposes an accessible Stop control. Stopping interrupts the current turn and pauses its message queue. Stop uses a discreet borderless, square-icon-only ghost button aligned to the far right of the activity row, with a 44px touch target, accessible Stop label and tooltip. An interrupted resumable thread exposes a prominent primary Resume button, ordered avatar → Resume → Interrupted in that same row on macOS, iOS, and Android. Resume continues the existing provider conversation with “Continue where you left off.” while preserving composer drafts and leaving queued follow-ups paused. Controls are disabled during another action or a disconnected companion state, and Resume is unavailable for missing accounts or non-resumable conversations. Mobile uses the paired Mac’s `resume_thread` composer operation; it does not enqueue the continuation behind a paused queue.

Verification (2026-11-07, durable companion outbox): Source and spec updated without removing concurrent workspace edits. The original regression failed when an interrupted send had no persisted queue; it passes after the change. Checks: all 486 tests across 76 app suites passed, followed by all 39 native-home tests including the additional timed automatic-retry test; workspace typechecks and diff whitespace checks passed. Build: `pnpm native:install` completed and the iOS development archive built. Signature: strict Mac and iOS bundle verification passed; the Mac uses Developer ID Application Denis Duvauchelle (BSH2UEDLU5). Install: `/Applications/Freddai.app` and DiPhone received updated bundles. Launch: native launch returned Mac PID 32264, whose mapped executable is `/Applications/Freddai.app/Contents/MacOS/freddai-native`; its SHA-256 matches the built executable (76948d9e…). DiPhone PID 2606 maps the newly installed 1AA30FCC… application container. Recovery: the host bridge remained ready and this existing thread continued responding after normal GUI Quit/relaunch, without terminating agent processes. Mac screenshot inspection failed with ScreenCaptureKit -3811, so visual verification was not completed. Live-provider verification: no new agent/provider turn or account/sign-in was used as a fixture. The original live 5G outage was not reproduced; retry, restart persistence, attachment retention, and pairing isolation were exercised with controlled test clients. Android uses the shared companion implementation but was not built or installed in this verification.

Swipe-recording delivery verification: Source/spec updated while preserving concurrent workspace changes. Tests: 490 app tests and app TypeScript pass on the final rerun; eight iOS native tests pass, including upward release thresholds, return-to-cancel, sideways rejection and rendered feedback fitting 320/393/744pt composers. Earlier shared-suite/typecheck failures during concurrent edits cleared on rerun. Build: production-configured iOS development archive, Android debug APK, and final `pnpm native:install` completed. Signature: iOS strict/deep verification, Android APK v2 verification and macOS Developer ID BSH2UEDLU5 strict bundle verification passed. Install: final Mac app at `/Applications/Freddai.app`; iPhone at `D088C4E1-6315-4D71-959A-099A5A815A51/Freddai.app`; iPad at `255D6315-4509-42AC-AA2B-187C1958A03E/Freddai.app`. No Android device was connected; APK remains available for installation. Launch: iPhone PID 2613 maps the newly installed bundle; iPad launch was denied because it is locked, so unlock it and open Freddai. Final normal Mac quit/native launch produced PID 46688 mapping `/Applications/Freddai.app/Contents/MacOS/freddai-native`; built and installed SHA-256 match `6411bcb80af8fe0e384a28fa4b30df8e40a309540743f8b5f05ae92f4d5bab45`. Existing host PID 19587 and watcher PIDs 9396/66638 survived; no agent force termination. Mac window exists, but screen capture failed with ScreenCaptureKit -3811, so visual reconnect/recovery was not verified. Live-provider/hardware verification: real microphone capture, transcription, send/cancel on physical devices and provider submissions were not exercised. No accounts, credentials or live turns were used as fixtures.

## Commander-in-Chief (planned, 2026-11-07, not implemented)

Status: spec only. No code exists yet. Open decisions are listed at the end.

### Concept
A global "chief of staff" agent, named **Commander-in-Chief** by default (renamable). It is a built-in default project created for every user, with its own folder. It knows about every project, recent thread and schedule. It hands tasks to other projects, creates recurring tasks, keeps a to-do list and notes, and checks that delegated work was actually completed. It does not do the work itself: its tools are limited to delegating, tracking, note-taking and reading.

### Current state (from code mapping)
- The `freddai` MCP server (`crates/core/src/mcp.rs`, injected for every agent by `crates/core/src/agent_mcp.rs`) already provides list/read projects and threads, `freddai_start_task` (always launched in Ask mode) and schedule tools. Commander reuses these.
- Missing pieces:
  - creating a default project (`Runtime::project()` requires the folder to already exist);
  - thread origin (`Session` has no parent/started-by field);
  - a way to react when a thread finishes (`finish_turn` has no hooks);
  - per-project tool restrictions (only `PermissionMode` exists);
  - a project-level model setting with a "latest" option;
  - an app-level avatar.
- Today every agent can read all threads and start tasks in any project (`docs/mcp-bridge.md`, "trusted local-client interface"). Decision needed: should only Commander have the cross-project tools?

### Backend plan
1. **Default project:** on first launch, create a folder (default `~/Freddai/Commander`, configurable), register it as a project marked `kind: "commander"`, and add a `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` describing its role. It is pinned and cannot be deleted, only reset.
2. **Provider/model:** add a project-level account and model setting. A "Latest" option resolves to the newest model the provider currently reports, at launch time.
3. **Origin:** add `origin: { kind: "commander" | "user" | "schedule", thread?: id }` to `Session`. `freddai_start_task` records it.
4. **Completion wake-up:** when a thread that Commander started finishes or fails, the linked to-do moves to `needs_review` and a "review this result" message is queued in Commander's thread. Commander does a light review (see Decisions) and posts a short message in its chat. Any follow-up it suggests goes through an approval card. Every wake-up is logged.
5. **Tool restriction:** Commander launches with file-editing and shell tools disabled for each provider (for example Claude `--disallowedTools`, plus the matching settings for Codex and ACP). It only gets the MCP tools and read-only file access to its own folder.

### To-dos and notes (app-owned, stored in Commander's folder)
- `todos.json`: each to-do has `id`, `title`, `project`, an `owner` (`me` | `commander` | `thread:<id>`), a `status` (`open` | `in_progress` | `needs_review` | `done` | `dropped`), `due`, `check_back_at`, the linked thread and a history.
  - The runtime mediates all writes (atomic replace), so the UI and the agent never overwrite each other.
- `notes/*.md`: plain markdown notes that the user and other agents can open and search.
- New MCP tools, only for Commander: `freddai_todo_list/add/update` and `freddai_note_list/read/write`.
- Connections (email and others) come later and are shown as "coming soon".

### Commander chat UI
- A chat-first layout, unlike normal project threads:
  - messages scroll behind a header that only fades the page's own background (one continuous surface, no tinted panel or hard edge);
  - **desktop column (2026-11-08):** the welcome-back card, transcript, proposal cards, queue and composer share one centered column (max 800px, 24px side margins); proposal cards flow inline after the latest messages instead of being pinned above the composer, and a finished proposal shows a human result ("Started" + Open thread), never raw JSON; host-injected notices ("Light notice / Due notice / Heartbeat (... not a user message)") remain hidden background inputs (`packages/app/src/conversation/systemNotice.ts`, detected by that first-line prefix);
  - a large round **avatar** with its name sits top centre;
  - **call** and **tools** buttons sit at the top right.
- Replies are prose. Tool activity is collapsed into short summaries, for example "Started 2 tasks, checked 1".
- Tapping the avatar opens the tools panel (a sheet on mobile, a dropdown on desktop):
  - **To-dos**, grouped by project, with owner chips and status;
  - **Notes**;
  - **Schedules**;
  - **Connections** (later).
- **Companion (iPhone) header, implemented 2026-11-07:** every conversation screen (Commander and regular threads) uses one compact translucent floating header (`packages/app/src/mobile/ThreadHeader.tsx`), only safe area + ~28pt tall. It is fixed to the top edge of the web view, so keyboard, scrolling and strips above the transcript never move it. The native bar draws the hamburger (left) and actions (right) on the same row; the web layer draws a centred 56pt avatar on that row (the animated mascot is allowed to spill out of its circle, like the "Working..." activity avatar) with a small name pill under it (truncated title, no status text). The transcript is padded by the header's height so the first message is visible at load and scrolls under the blur afterwards. Queued-message strips start below the header.
- **Commander sheet:** there is no tab bar and no pinned approval card. Tapping the Commander avatar opens a bottom sheet with medium and large detents and a grabber (drag or tap to switch). Tabs: To-dos, Notes, Waiting, Sub-threads. Pending approvals (Yes / No / Edit) live in Waiting; the avatar shows a count badge while something needs the user, and the sheet opens on Waiting in that case. On regular companion threads (iPhone, iPad, Android and shared web UI), tapping the header avatar opens Commander’s current conversation through the same navigation as the dashboard; if no Commander conversation exists it opens a new Commander draft. The navigation button is labelled with the configured Commander name and does not advertise a tools dialog. Reduced motion disables the sheet animation; reduced transparency makes the header opaque. Limitation: the sheet is rendered by the web layer, so it sits above the native composer rather than covering it.

### Dashboard presence
On the desktop Home/Dashboard and the companion Dashboard, Commander appears **above the project list**, as if overseeing it. It is built from three circles:
- one **large avatar circle** in the centre, with an idle "ready" animation (breathing or blinking; it switches to a working state while Commander has a turn running; reduced-motion users get a static image). Tapping it opens the Commander chat;
- a **microphone circle** overlapping the avatar's bottom-left edge. It starts voice capture straight to Commander, using the existing local dictation (`packages/app/src/desktop/voice.ts`);
- a **plus circle** overlapping the avatar's bottom-right edge. It opens a quick task or message composer addressed to Commander.

Each control has an accessible label and a 44pt minimum touch target, and they all work from the keyboard.

### Calling
- **Phase 1:** a hands-free call mode. Speech is transcribed locally with the existing Whisper dictation, sent as a Commander turn, and the reply is read aloud with the system voice (AVSpeechSynthesizer on macOS/iOS, Android TTS). Expect a few seconds of delay per turn, so it feels like walkie-talkie rather than a live call. The Mac desktop part is built (see Voice call mode (macOS, phase 1), 2026-11-09, using `say`); companion thread call controls now use the same controller (see Companion voice calls, 2026-11-09); the richer dedicated call stage remains Phase 2.
- **Phase 2 (later):** a live voice conversation through a realtime voice provider that can use Commander's tools. This is a separate model and is billed per minute.

### Dashboard command center (wake word, Phase 2)
- **Scope:** the Home/Dashboard view on Mac, iPhone, iPad, Android and mobile web. When the user opens the Commander call from the dashboard, the mic stays live in wake-word mode. The existing VAD segments the audio and Whisper transcribes it locally. A transcript is dropped and never leaves the machine unless it begins with the wake word "Freddie" (near-spellings accepted: Freddy, Freddi, Freddie's, "Hey Freddie"). The Commander avatar shows a listening state.
- **Utterance:** after the wake word the user names a project and gives an instruction, e.g. "Freddie, in Recursive, add a retry to the uploader." Speech ends at a pause of about 1.5 to 2 s (the existing VAD segment end). The wake-word prefix is stripped before sending.
- **Routing:** in this phase project resolution is local name matching in the UI (no LLM round-trip); the longest matching project name wins. Exact names take priority; if none matches, the project named `freddai` also accepts the spoken spellings Freddy, Freddie and Freddi. If no project matches, Commander speaks a short "which project?" and no card is shown. While that question is open, the next utterance answers it without a wake word; after two unrecognised answers the command is dropped and listening returns to wake-word only. The launch calls the host launch directly with the card's agent, model and difficulty, which is why it never enters the Commander proposal hold. Objective-based fuzzy routing through Commander is a later fallback.
- **Review card:** once the project is resolved, a review card appears on the dashboard next to the Commander avatar. It shows the project, the instruction text (editable), and the three standard selectors from thread creation: agent (account), model, and difficulty (effort level). Defaults come from the project's defaults, then the global defaults. The session is always a new thread in that project; continuing an existing thread is out of scope for this phase. Any selector can be changed with a click.
- **Commit:** saying "go" (also "send it", "do it") or clicking Send launches a new thread in the project folder immediately. Voice-originated dashboard launches bypass the Commander proposal/approval hold: the spoken "go" or the Send click is the approval. Outward actions (email, calendar, sharing) are not covered by this bypass and keep their existing holds. Commander speaks a one-line acknowledgement naming the project.
- **Correction:** while the card is open, speech that is not "go" or "cancel" is treated as a correction and replaces or amends the instruction text on the card (no wake word needed). It does not start a new command.
- **Dismissal:** saying "cancel" (also "never mind", "scrap that") or clicking the X on the card dismisses it without launching. There is no automatic timeout.
- **After launch or dismissal:** the card clears and the mic returns to wake-word-only listening until the next "Freddie". Closing the call ends listening.
- **Consent:** wake-word mode is active only while the user has explicitly opened the dashboard call; it never starts at app launch. The listening indicator is always visible while active.
- **Failures are loud:** if transcription or launch fails, the card shows the error and Commander speaks it. Nothing is silently dropped or silently retried.
- **Companion review:** dashboard call transcripts use the same local wake-word/project reducer as desktop. The card shows editable text, the real assistants advertised for the project, and model/effort controls; its scrollable height follows the visible viewport when a mobile keyboard opens. Send or spoken “go” starts the reviewed request through the paired Mac; no turn is dispatched before confirmation. Failed sends retain the card and reuse the request ID on retry. While a native dashboard call is active, the web content occupies the full content area so the card is not clipped into the native dashboard workspace. Ending the call restores the dashboard.
- **Out of scope this phase:** continuing an existing thread, wake word outside the dashboard, custom wake words.
- **Open questions:**
  - Should difficulty default per project?
  - Should multiple queued commands be allowed while a card is open?

### Decisions (2026-11-07)
1. **Avatar:** the existing Freddai mascot (the Dormouse used across the app). Not a new character and not the user's photo.
2. **Light review:** when a delegated thread finishes, Commander does a quick completeness check, not a deep audit. Did the thread do what was asked, did it finish or fail, are there open questions? It then posts a short message in its own voice in the Commander chat, for example "Done with the pricing page for Recursive, have a look here →", with a link to the thread. If something is missing it says so plainly and suggests a follow-up. The follow-up still needs the user's approval (see 3).
3. **No autonomous starts by default; opt-in budgeted autonomy:** in the default `propose` mode Commander never starts a task, enables a schedule or sends anything without explicit user approval. Settings (`autonomy_settings.json` in the Commander folder; Tauri `commander_settings_get` / `commander_settings_save`) can switch `autonomy.mode` to `budgeted`: a Commander `freddai_start_task` then runs without approval only if the target project is in `allowedProjectIds`, running Commander-origin threads are below `maxConcurrent` (default 2) and today's (local date) auto-allowed starts are below `dailyStartCap` (default 5). Otherwise it is held as today. Every auto-allowed start (`decision: auto_allowed`) and every budget-denied hold (`budget_denied`) is written to `actions.log` with the reason. Schedules stay approval-gated in every mode.
   - **Proactive wakes:** heartbeat and due/check-back inputs remain persisted background work, logged as `commander.wake.*` events and in `actions.log`. Heartbeats respect the configured interval, active window and change detection, and review Commander-delegated or explicitly tracked work. Unrelated completions and failures do not notify Commander. Delegated or explicitly tracked completions retain the light-review wake. Heartbeat/due wakes defer while paused, busy or already holding a pending wake. All internal wake inputs are hidden from the shared conversation and queue UI and excluded from queued-message badges; only user messages and meaningful assistant replies occupy the conversation. Legacy unrelated completion batches and passive queue cards are retired automatically.
   - It proposes actions as **approval cards** in the chat: a short question, what it would do, which project, and **Yes / No** buttons, plus an optional "Edit" before approving. Example: "Want me to take care of these 3 emails? Yes / No".
   - Approving runs the action. The card then shows the result and links to the started thread. Declining is recorded so Commander doesn't ask again.
   - Cards also appear in the avatar tools panel under "Waiting on you" and on mobile.
   - Implementation: the propose-then-approve flow is enforced in the runtime. MCP actions from the Commander project create a pending proposal instead of executing, and only the UI can approve. A prompt instruction alone is not enough.
4. **Cross-project tools for all agents:** dropped. Not a user-experience concern; leave as is.

### Project objectives
Commander must know each project's core objective and intent in order to route and guide work.
- Add `objective` (a short paragraph: what the project is, who it's for, its current goal) to `Project`. It is editable in Settings → Projects and in the project header.
- `freddai_list_projects` and `freddai_context` return it.
- When a project has no objective, Commander drafts one from the project's README, CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md and recent threads. It shows the draft as an approval card; nothing is saved without approval.
- Routing example: the user writes "for Recursive, can we add X?" Commander identifies the project (by name, or by objective when ambiguous; if still unclear, it asks), restates the task with a brief description in the context of the project's objective, and shows it as an approval card. On Yes, it starts the thread and adds a to-do linked to it. On completion, it does the light review and posts its short message.

### Patterns ported from Tamias Duo (2026-11-07)
Source: `/Users/denisduvauchelle/Documents/code/tamias-duo` (`apps/brain/convex/`). We port the concepts and data shapes, not the Convex agent loop: Freddai runs CLI agents with MCP tools. Keep Commander's tool count small (about 10 MCP tools).

1. **Action gate** (ref `harness/gate.ts`, `gateRules.ts`, `harness/approvals.ts`).
   - Every Commander MCP tool is labelled `internal` (to-dos, notes, reads: runs immediately) or `outward` (start a task, enable a schedule, anything that sends: held for approval).
   - A held call is saved as a pending proposal with its exact arguments and a one-line summary ("Before I {summary}, I need your OK."). On Yes, the runtime replays that exact call; on No, it records the decline.
   - Every call goes into a `commander/actions.log` file (tool, arguments summary, decision, result). Failures come back to the agent as `{ok:false, error}` and are shown, never swallowed.
   - Standing "always allow" rules are out of scope; the only approval-free path is the budgeted mode above.
2. **Completion evidence** (ref `lib/taskOutcome.ts`). Each to-do gets:
   - `verification[]`: rows of {requirement, status `met|missing|unclear`, evidence (a quote or link from the thread)};
   - `completion_basis`: `verified` (every requirement met) or `user_confirmed` (stores the user's exact words);
   - `completion_note`.

   Commander's light review fills in `verification[]` by reading the finished thread. The short in-chat message reports the result; if any requirement is `missing` or `unclear`, the to-do stays open and a follow-up approval card is offered.
3. **Attention is always actionable.**
   - A global **Needs you / Running / Done** status indicator for Commander (in the dashboard avatar cluster and the chat header).
   - Every "needs you" item (approval card, review result, question) always shows a visible action button. None is only reachable by scrolling the transcript.
   - **Fold-in:** results of delegated work that finish while Commander is replying appear after that reply, never in the middle of it.
4. **"While you were away" + Fresh Start** (ref `w/chat/AwayBlock.tsx`, `c/freshStart.ts`).
   - When the user returns after a gap (default 10 min), the chat collapses the old transcript into a peek and shows a greeting plus at most 3 one-tap cards: what finished, what is waiting on the user, and what is next.
   - Tapping a card opens it; dismissals are remembered.
   - The habit scoring by time of day is deferred.
5. **Memory and personality** (ref `harness/memory.ts`, `memoryRules.ts`, `lib/assistantDocuments.ts`).
   - `commander/memory.json` stores entries of {kind `preference|fact|person|project`, text, confidence, pinned, status `active|stale|forgotten`, superseded_by, source thread, optional project}.
   - After each Commander turn, a cheap extraction pass proposes new memories. It keeps only those with confidence of at least 0.85, then merges or supersedes duplicates.
   - The most relevant memories are injected into each turn up to a fixed budget (about 1,500 characters). Project-scoped memories are injected only when relevant. Memories go stale if unconfirmed for 30 days.
   - Tools: `freddai_memory_remember/forget/search`. Memories are viewable and editable in the avatar tools panel.
   - **Soul:** `commander/SOUL.md` holds personality, tone and style (it drives the short in-character messages). Commander can propose edits only through an approval card; each change is kept as a revision with a reason and can be restored.

### Later phases (from Tamias Duo, not yet scheduled)
6. **Goals:** title, success criteria, milestones, linked to-dos, a review interval, and a periodic goal-review prompt.
7. **Contacts/CRM:** people with an AI "where things stand" summary and an activity feed. **Calendar:** wait for connections.
8. **Voice call model:** the first sentence is spoken right away as an acknowledgement; tool steps and cards show on a call stage; the result is spoken at the end. Hanging up leaves a summary card in the chat and runs memory extraction over the call.
9. **Proactive offers:** "Yes, do it / Not now / Never for this", with at most 2 visible, 3 per day, 30-day suppression, and an offer type stops after 3 declines.
10. **Mascot states:** idle, thinking, listening, wave, happy and sleepy, with a still pose for reduced motion, driven by Commander's state.

### Still open
- Commander's name is renamable; is "Commander-in-Chief" the default? To decide later.

### Implementation status (backend)
Real as of 2026-11-07 (source and unit tests only; not yet built, installed or run against a live provider):
- `Project` gained `kind`, `objective` and `model` (all serde-defaulted); `Session` gained `origin`. `Runtime::ensure_commander()` runs when the host starts: it creates `~/Freddai/Commander` (override with `FREDDAI_COMMANDER_DIR`), seeds `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `SOUL.md` and `notes/` without overwriting edits, registers one "Commander-in-Chief" project first in the list, and refuses deletion (`Runtime::assert_project_deletable`; there is no project-delete command in the app yet).
- `crates/core/src/commander.rs` stores `todos.json`, `memory.json`, `proposals.json` (atomic replace), `actions.log` (JSONL) and `notes/*.md`. Host commands: `commander_state`, `commander_todos`, `commander_todo_save`, `commander_notes`, `commander_note_read`, `commander_note_write`, `commander_proposals`, `commander_proposal_respond`, `commander_memory`, `commander_memory_save`, `project_objective_set`, `commander_settings_set`, with matching Tauri commands in `desktop.rs`.
- **Soul, identity and core memory** (`crates/core/src/commander_profile.rs`): the Commander folder also holds `SOUL.md`, `USER.md` (seeded with a template), `identity.json` (`{name}`) and `revisions/<soul|user>/<ISO>.md` plus `revisions/index.jsonl` (`{file, at, by, reason}`; a revision on every change, atomic writes). The default soul is the new short text; an existing SOUL.md that exactly equals the previously seeded default is replaced (the old text is kept as a revision), anything else is left alone. `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` are now generated (header comment, role instructions, Identity, Soul, About the user, Remembered from `memory.json` pinned-first within about 1,500 characters, Projects with objectives). They are written on `ensure_commander` and in `before_launch`, which runs before every Commander turn (new launch, resume and queued continue all pass through the same spawn point); unchanged content is not rewritten, and a render or write failure logs `commander.prompt_write_failed` and fails the turn instead of using stale instructions. Hand edits to CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md are overwritten. Host and Tauri commands: `commander_profile`, `commander_profile_save {name?, soul?, user?, reason?}` (a name change renames the Commander project, keeping `kind=commander`) and `commander_revision_restore {file, at}` (creates a new revision with reason "Restored <at>"); all three are allowed over the companion. MCP tools (Commander only): `freddai_user_profile_update` (internal, logged) and `freddai_soul_propose` (held in the action gate, applied on approval with `by = commander`). Verified by unit tests only; no live provider turn.
- MCP: ten Commander-only tools (`freddai_todo_*`, `freddai_note_*`, `freddai_memory_*`, `freddai_propose`) listed and callable only for the Commander project. `freddai_list_projects` and `freddai_context` return `objective` for every caller.
- Action gate: from the Commander project, `freddai_start_task`, enabling or editing an enabled `freddai_save_schedule`, and `freddai_schedule_action` resume/run/delete create a pending proposal and return `{ok:true, held:true, proposal_id}`. Approval replays the exact call (optionally with an edited prompt), records the thread, links or auto-creates the to-do (owner `thread`, `in_progress`) and sets `origin` to `commander`. Failures become status `failed` with the error. Every call is logged.
- Completion wake-up: when a Commander-origin thread ends `done` or `failed`, its to-do becomes `needs_review` and a light-review message is queued into the latest Commander thread (or the to-do simply waits under Needs you if none exists). Threads inside the Commander project never wake it.
- Tool restriction: Claude Commander threads get `--disallowedTools Edit,Write,MultiEdit,NotebookEdit,Bash` and `--allowedTools mcp__freddai`; Codex starts with a read-only sandbox; Commander never runs with bypass permissions. ACP harnesses have no restriction mechanism yet and rely on `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` (TODO in `runtime.rs`).
- Project model: `model` may be a model ID or `"latest"`. "Latest" is resolved at launch from Codex model discovery; other providers have no catalog, so the launch logs `commander.model_latest_fallback`, uses the provider default and exposes `model_notice` in `commander_state`.
- Not done: the automatic post-turn memory extraction pass, memory injection into prompts, soul revisions, "while you were away", and any UI.

### Companion Commander (iOS first, 2026-11-07, source implemented, not yet installed or device-verified)
- **API:** the phone proxies `commander_state`, `commander_proposals`, `commander_proposal_respond`, `commander_todos`, `commander_todo_save`, `commander_notes`, `commander_note_read` and `commander_note_write` through `/v1/composer` (allow-listed in `companion_operations.rs` and `companion_server.rs`). Memory and settings stay desktop-only. `commander_state` has the folder `path` removed. Project entries in the companion snapshot gain `kind` and `objective`.
- **Dashboard:** native iOS draws the cluster in `DashboardView.swift` (mascot avatar with idle breathing, still under Reduce Motion; mic circle bottom-left holding the existing dictation; plus circle bottom-right; Needs you / Running / Done pill). Mic opens Commander with the transcript as a draft (never auto-sent); avatar, pill and plus open the Commander screen. The browser/fixture dashboard uses the web cluster with a quick composer that replies to Commander's latest thread or starts one. The commander project is not repeated in the project cards.
- **Screen:** the normal conversation of the commander project, wrapped in a translucent blurred header (large centred mascot, name, status), pending approval cards pinned above the composer (Yes / No / Edit, inline result or loud error, link to the started thread) and a bottom tab bar Chat, To-dos, Notes, Waiting. To-dos are grouped by project with owner chips, status, verification checklist and Mark done / Needs more work / Drop. Waiting shows approvals and to-dos needing review. iOS uses a `commander` shell mode: transparent navigation bar, web content under the status bar, title hidden.
- **Drawer:** the commander project is listed first with the mascot and opens the screen (iOS and Android). The native iOS drawer clips the Commander avatar to a circle; reused ordinary project rows retain their folder shape. Verified by the iOS layout suite (10 tests passing, including circular clipping and folder reuse); signed companion build installed and launched on DiPhone on 2026-11-07. Physical-device visual confirmation remains pending. Android has no transparent-bar variant, so it uses the plain conversation chrome, and its dashboard has no cluster yet.
- **Gaps:** if Commander has no thread yet the app opens the New thread screen for the commander project; "While you were away" cards, memory, schedules and calling are desktop-only for now.

### Compact user messages

Shared Mac and phone transcripts show user messages as plain-text previews capped at five visual lines (including wrapped lines). Overflow exposes an accessible Show more button; expansion restores complete Markdown and Show less collapses it again. Attachments and delivery status remain outside the preview. This applies to pending outgoing messages too; original message content sent to providers is unchanged.


### Companion recording capsule and rising send target (2026-11-07)

Refines the native iOS/iPadOS and Android conversation/New thread recording presentation. A neutral rounded 44pt/dp capsule grows left from the held microphone over approximately 280–300ms. Its only content is elapsed m:ss and a scrolling live waveform beside the original microphone; red is confined to that microphone. A separate circular up-arrow target rises 64pt/dp directly above the held control. Reaching the existing upward-release threshold enlarges and fills the arrow target, with the existing threshold haptic; dragging back restores its neutral state. The held microphone remains stationary, preserving the gesture origin and making the target legible. No visible instruction text or red waveform/background remains. iOS uses the shared damped spring; Android uses a short eased reveal and eased target transition. Reduced-motion settings remove spatial entry animation. Existing release-to-cancel, upward-release-to-send, permission ownership, accessibility send/cancel alternatives and draft safety are preserved. Dashboard microphones retain their separate draft interaction. Visual inspiration: older Apple Messages voice recording/swipe-up interaction, adapted to Freddai rather than copying current Messages behavior.

### Commander single conversation and background threads (2026-11-07)

Commander has one user-facing conversation. The host adopts the latest existing non-delegated Commander conversation once and persists its ID in `conversation.json` in the Commander folder. Later updates to other threads do not replace it; an archived or deleted main conversation allows adoption of another live foreground conversation. Completion wake-ups return to this same conversation. Existing conversations are preserved.

Projects, flat thread lists and Recent on desktop and companion exclude all Commander-project threads and threads with `origin.kind = commander`, including selected, pinned, running and unread ones. The Commander project has one open action rather than an expandable list or a new-task action. The avatar tools/sheet adds **Sub-threads**, listing other Commander conversations and Commander-delegated work across projects with title, project and current status. Opening a sub-thread uses the normal conversation controls. Its execution, approval gates, read history and storage are unchanged. Ordinary user-created project threads remain in navigation.

The companion snapshot includes only the origin kind and caller thread alongside existing thread metadata. No account or credential fields are added. Calendar/schedules remain in their existing tools; this change adds no calendar feature.

### Companion sustained recording (2026-11-07)

Native companion recording allows up to ten minutes (600 seconds), with no silence-based stop. While recording, the native recorder keeps the screen awake so normal auto-lock cannot cancel a hands-free dictation. The previous screen idle policy is restored after iOS capture ends; Android releases its recording keep-screen-on flag. At the composer duration cap, audio follows the normal finish/transcribe/send path rather than being discarded. Explicit cancel, leaving the screen or app, and OS interruptions remain separate stop conditions. The 9 MiB Android upload guard remains; 10 minutes of 32 kbps AAC is about 2.4 MB.

### Desktop file-drop routing
Conversation and new-thread file drops subscribe to Tauri drag events with an AnyLabel target scoped to the current window label. This accepts both webview-routed drops and parent-window drops after an embedded browser is created, without consuming another conversation window’s drops. Both routes use the attachment picker’s staging and draft ownership checks; navigation folder drops retain project import behavior.

### Companion connection with large workspaces (2026-11-07)

The companion listener must bound data at the local host RPC boundary, before preparing its network response. Scoped phone snapshots and metadata needed for companion actions use a full-index `snapshot_since` request with revision zero and `openThreads` limited to the selected conversation (empty for the home screen and actions). They must not request the entire workspace through legacy `snapshot`: accumulated tool output can exceed the 512 MiB IPC limit even when the eventual phone index is small. Existing unscoped clients retain the legacy response. The listener restores the selected conversation's deferred tool bodies with bounded, hash-checked `tool_text` reads, preserving the phone's existing complete-history behavior. Generated-file reads forward to the existing host operation, where validation and transcript access remain local. Saved transcripts, paired credentials and provider execution are unchanged.

Verification: a synthetic host-boundary regression reproduced the oversized-reply connection failure before the fix. Native tests cover bounded initial sync, multi-page Unicode tool-output restoration, encrypted relay snapshots and existing actions. No provider turns or connected credentials are used as test fixtures. Build/install and observed device reconnection are recorded separately in the delivery report.

### Companion phantom backlog prevention (2026-11-07)
An online message being submitted is shown as Sending in its conversation, without a device-queue banner or queued drawer row. Device backlog indicators include only messages waiting for connection, retrying or blocked after a delivery failure, or waiting behind the active FIFO head. Real Mac dispatch queues remain visible. Messages remain durable until the Mac accepts them, with stable request IDs for retry; acceptance removes them from durable device storage immediately. Optimistic accepted transcript echoes remain in memory only until the host snapshot catches up. On startup, legacy acknowledged outbox entries are removed from the current pairing namespace without resending them; genuinely unacknowledged messages and other pairing namespaces are preserved. No age-based deletion of unsent work is permitted.

### Companion transcription FIFO

The native composer remains recordable while prior recordings transcribe or their chat messages are queued. Completed captures enter a serial transcription FIFO and retain the chat/project, agent controls, typed text and attachment paths captured at submission. Native text/files are handed off and cleared immediately, leaving the composer free for the next recording. Successful transcripts become independent messages in the existing persisted outgoing FIFO; busy chats queue replies through the host. Navigation cannot route a late transcript into the current unrelated composer. Consecutive recordings from the same New chat destination share the first created thread, including when its creation acknowledgement is pending. Transcription failures/empty audio report errors without blocking subsequent recordings. Pending audio transcription jobs are session-local; completed outgoing text messages use the durable outbox. Dashboard dictation keeps its draft-only behavior.

The companion reply endpoint permits queueing during an active first provider turn even before its resumable provider session ID is available; completed non-resumable conversations remain rejected, and pending approval restrictions remain unchanged. Native Android title updates and the automatic New chat-to-conversation transition preserve an active second recording; explicit navigation still cancels capture.

### Remote attachment/dictation relay queue correction (2026-11-07)

Both mobile attachment uploads and dictation recordings use `/v1/composer` begin/chunk requests (24 KiB per chunk) followed by finish/transcribe. The relay retained completed replies for 120 seconds and incorrectly counted them against its 32-request pending-work limit. A local protocol regression and the deployed HTTPS synthetic encrypted-upload check both reproduced HTTP 503 after 32 completed requests, before a 1 MiB recording could finish uploading. This establishes a shared remote-upload failure; it does not establish that every reported transcription issue has this cause.

The relay now counts only unanswered requests against the 32 pending slots, retaining completed ciphertext replies for safe retries. Total records per mailbox remain separately bounded at 4096, alongside existing TTL, global memory, body-size, authentication and rate limits. No mobile protocol, encryption, account, provider or transcription-model changes are needed. Regression checks cover sequential completed uploads, idempotent retry/no redispatch, authentication/isolation and rejection of a 33rd unanswered request. The HTTPS smoke check now also exercises more than 32 encrypted 24 KiB chunks using ephemeral synthetic material without contacting a Mac or provider.

Delivery verification: local relay tests passed (3/3); public HTTPS regression failed before deployment at remote upload step 31 (after the smoke check's initial completed request). ### Companion product usage

Companion thread navigation includes a labeled Usage panel with two-column cards. Each card shows the product (Codex/Claude) and account name, a normal-size percentage explicitly labeled “left”, a thin neutral bar showing remaining capacity, and a separate “Resets in …” caption. The two-column footer has no card backgrounds or large numbers; four products occupy 132pt instead of 220pt (40% less height), keeping thread navigation visually primary. Low capacity below 20% uses amber alongside the numeric label. Unavailable readings stay explicit and never show an invented percentage. Details opens all quota windows with their reset dates and stale-reading explanations. Readings older than five minutes are labeled last known. The iOS footer shows at most two rows at once and scrolls additional products within the footer so the thread list remains usable. iOS pins these below the thread list; Android and web include them at the end of navigation. Read-only quota checks run on the Mac in the background, at most every two minutes per account, without delaying snapshots or creating model turns. Only account display metadata and quota readings cross the companion boundary. Older hosts omit these rows; unavailable readings never imply zero usage.

Completion preserves the capsule while it slides upward by 22 points/dp and fades; Reduce Motion avoids upward travel.

### Project office voice orders (2026-11-08)

Desktop Home and companion dashboards (iOS, iPadOS, Android and browser, including native-card dashboards) expose project rooms as keyboard-accessible order targets in both the inline and expanded Office. Select a room to expand the Office with its project name and hold microphone ready; native companions expand their WebView through the existing Office expansion bridge. Release transcribes locally on the paired Mac and starts a new task in that project; this explicitly submitting Office action is separate from existing companion card dictation, which continues to open a draft. Desktop uses the existing voice controller/Home launch resolver. Companions use their existing readiness check, WebView/browser MediaRecorder capture and authenticated chunked transcription/start transport, retaining the dashboard's global assistant fallback and model/effort selection. Voice must be enabled with a ready Mac model; unsupported or denied capture reports an error. WebM/Opus captures are decoded on the companion and converted to mono 16kHz PCM WAV before uploading, because the Mac decoder accepts AAC/M4A and PCM WAV. No live transcript is claimed: Whisper returns text after release.

Listening illuminates the selected room and adds three decorative scurrying mice. Reduced motion retains the static cue. New real task snapshots drive the existing lobby-to-desk character movement; listening never fabricates running sessions. Move away or cancel a pointer hold to discard capture. Visibility loss/unmount discards companion capture, releasing before readiness cannot start a delayed recorder, and a late transcript cannot dispatch through an unmounted order control. Failed submissions retain their transcript with a Retry action; companion retries reuse the same request id. Existing typed drafts are untouched. Room switches during transcription cancel that control's delivery rather than routing its transcript to a different project.

### Companion scheduled thread indicators
Companion thread navigation shows a trailing schedule icon and announces “Scheduled task” for scheduled runs in project groups and both Recent lists. The Mac snapshot supplies a summary-safe `scheduled` boolean from the same schedule ID or scheduled launch option used by desktop; the iOS, Android and web companions preserve status indicators and title truncation. Older snapshots without the field show no schedule icon.

### Codex MCP elicitation

Codex `mcpServer/elicitation/request` interactions enter the existing per-thread pending approval queue instead of being rejected as unsupported. Desktop and companion web views display the server name and message, typed primitive form fields (or a JSON response for complex schemas), and explicit allow/decline actions. URL requests expose an HTTP(S) link for the user to open. Responses preserve the request ID and use MCP `action`/`content`; unknown interaction methods still fail closed. This does not automatically approve requests or replay previously declined tasks.

Companion pin appearance (2026-11-08): iOS thread navigation uses the existing SF Symbols library (`pin.fill`) in a trailing slot to the right of the title, before the schedule icon. The web companion uses Lucide `Pin` in the same position. Titles contain no pin emoji; status indicators remain on the left and pinned accessibility announcements are retained. Reused unpinned iOS rows hide and collapse the pin slot.

Companion device-outbox auto-retry (2026-11-08): the queue retries on its own with backoff 2s, 4s, 8s, 16s then 30s while any message is unsent, and immediately (backoff reset) on `online`/network-connection change, page focus, return to foreground and host reconnect. Retries reuse the message's stable request ID; the Mac dedupes `start` and `reply` by that ID, so retries cannot duplicate. An attempt that gets no result in 120s is abandoned by a watchdog and retried, so a dead socket after a 5G/Wi-Fi switch cannot wedge the queue. The queued bubble and banner show the last failure and the next attempt ("Queued · last attempt failed: <reason> · retrying in 8s"), and the banner has a "Retry now" button beside "Cancel message". Each attempt logs a structured `[companion-outbox]` event (id, attempt, connectivity, result, error, ms). Known limit: attachment upload restarts from zero when any chunk fails, so large images over the relay can still take several attempts.

## Thread link cards and sub-agent cards (2026-11-08)

Shared by the Mac and the phone companion (one React transcript). A `ThreadLinksContext` (`conversation/ThreadLinksContext.tsx`) gives the transcript `openThread(id)` and a live `threadInfo(id)` (title, project name, status running/waiting/done/failed/interrupted). Each app provides it around its thread transcript from its polled data (Mac `db.sessions`/`db.projects`, companion `data.threads`/`data.projects`), so cards update as polling refreshes. Opening a thread uses the app's normal selection path (Mac: `setSelected`, which also leaves the Commander view; companion: select + conversation screen). Outside a provider, links and cards are inert.

- Thread links: assistant markdown `[Fix login](freddai://thread/<id>)` (or `<freddai://thread/<id>>`) renders as an inline chip with the link text and a status dot; tapping opens the thread. Without a host it is plain text.
- Sub-agent cards: every successful `freddai_start_task` call renders a visible "Sub-agent" card (title, project, status chip with a spinner while running, "Needs approval", "Done", "Failed") below its collapsed "Tool activity" group; the raw call stays inside the group. Parsed from all provider shapes (`conversation/startTask.ts`): Claude `tool_use` paired with `tool_result` by id, Codex `mcpToolCall`, ACP `tool_call_update`. A start that returns an error shows a failed card with the message; a start held for approval shows no card (the approval card handles it); a call with no result yet, or output elided for size, shows nothing.
- Cards show "Thread" until the thread appears in the live list; the host may later add `title`/`project`/`status` to the start result and the title is used as a hint.

### macOS window teardown crash — 2026-11-09

Removed the live WKWebView Objective-C runtime subclass used to override `allowsWritingToolsAffordance`. AppKit/WebKit must retain their original runtime class and observation metadata throughout layout and window destruction. Configuration-level Writing Tools disabling and the guarded selection-update workaround remain; the floating Siri affordance may reappear on systems that ignore these opt-outs. Do not reintroduce live `isa` replacement or class-identity spoofing to suppress it.

Evidence: `cargo test -p freddai-native --test thread_windows` reproduced SIGSEGV at address zero with the reported `_NSKeyValueRetainedObservationInfoForObject` → scroll-view separator observer removal → WebKit window detach → NSWindow dealloc stack. The browser harness independently reproduced SIGTRAP during NSView frame updates. After removing class replacement, all three native integration harnesses passed: ten thread-window close/reopen cycles (including route/reuse checks), browser cookie isolation/persistence and resizing, and Writing Tools configuration/copy plus textarea editing/selection. No accounts or live provider turns are test fixtures. This supersedes earlier claims that class-identity spoofing safely preserves AppKit observation behavior.

Built and installed executable SHA-256 matched: `1acc8e84e06f191716614dce13ad81abfca6ca9cf2d15539e8a9a3faded53fbc`. Normal Apple-event quit plus native launch loaded the installed executable as PID 92555; accessibility showed the existing sessions (1 working, 2 needing attention). Pressing the installed window's close control removed the window and exited the GUI without a new production crash report; native relaunch succeeded as PID 94075. Background host PID 85131 and existing agent processes survived both GUI restarts. Live-provider verification was limited to reconnection to existing work; no new turns, accounts or sign-ins were initiated. Full workspace tests were not run for this targeted native correction.

## Companion voice calls (2026-11-09)

New threads and existing companion conversations, including Commander, have a compact **Start call** action in the thread header on iOS and the web companion, and in the thread actions on Android. The desktop call action stays inside the composer toolbar. Idle call controls reserve no vertical space above the composer. Starting checks the Mac's voice model readiness and requests microphone access. The same call controller as macOS bundles utterances, offers Interrupt/Queue, mute, optional Speak replies, Stop speaking, and End call. Spoken input uses the companion's persistent authenticated outbox without clearing typed drafts or attachments. Voice must be enabled and ready on the paired Mac; no accounts or agent turns are created for readiness checks.

iPhone and iPad use native AVAudioRecorder with metering to split speech after 800 ms of silence, with a 20-second segment cap. Silence-only recordings are discarded. Recordings follow the existing companion upload/transcription path to local Whisper on the paired Mac; they do not use a cloud speech provider. AVSpeechSynthesizer reads optional replies on the companion itself, pausing capture to prevent feedback. The screen stays awake during native capture. The native dictation mic is disabled while a call owns audio. Browser/Android web capture uses MediaRecorder plus AudioContext metering and browser speech synthesis, converting audio to a Mac-compatible recording when necessary.

Calls require the companion to remain in the foreground. Leaving the thread, losing the Mac connection, backgrounding the app, or an OS audio interruption stops capture. Background/interruption errors require ending and restarting the call. Hangup cancels pending sends and ignores transcription results that arrive afterward. It does not stop an already-running agent task. Device delivery and live microphone/provider checks are reported separately from automated transport/controller tests.

### Companion workspace settings

The iOS, iPadOS, Android, and web companion dashboard exposes Settings near the top. Settings opens workspace management followed by immediately visible project name/color/sort controls. Workspaces support touch/pointer drag reordering and Move up/down controls. Dragging a project from the project catalog adds membership; dragging a member handle to another workspace moves that membership, preserving other memberships. Dropping in No workspace removes all memberships. Each member also has a Move to menu for accessible touch and keyboard operation. All edits persist through the paired Mac; disconnected companions cannot edit.

### Commander background notifications

Commander receives completion follow-ups only for threads it launched or threads explicitly linked to its to-do list. Unrelated completions neither create cards nor wake Commander; unrelated failures also do not trigger heartbeat attention. Legacy pending completion batches and queued passive cards are retired automatically without deleting source threads or results.

Internal heartbeat, due and delegated-completion inputs run in the background and occupy no transcript or queue space on macOS, iPhone, iPad, Android or web. Historical internal notices are hidden. User messages and meaningful assistant replies remain visible, including reviews of Commander-delegated work. Native shells use the same shared transcript and queue renderers.

A normal native quit/relaunch mapped the installed executable; this implementation thread continued across the GUI restart. ### Satellite workspace aspect ratio

Companion dashboards on iPhone, iPad, Android, and web size the office overview to its full available width while preserving map proportions. The card grows vertically without a viewport-height cap. Embedded web content reports its measured card height (including controls) to the native dashboard, which resizes its workspace slot after layout or project changes. Expanded office zoom/pan behavior is unchanged.


### Companion thread sheets (November 2026)
Companion thread presentation is determined by its entry point on iPhone, iPad, Android, and browser companions. Selecting a thread from navigation (including the left sidebar, drawer, Recent, and search) replaces the main view, even when a sheet was already open. Only an existing thread selected from a dashboard project card or a thread card/link inside a conversation opens a sheet. New-thread actions, Commander navigation, schedules, and queue navigation use the main view. Dismissing a sheet returns to the dashboard or conversation that opened it, preserving drafts and ongoing work; linked threads within a sheet reuse that presentation. Both native shells require an explicit threadSheet flag, defaulting to false. Desktop sidebar navigation continues to replace its main pane.

Dashboard voice controls: native iOS/iPadOS and Android use a compact 224-point/dp recording capsule at the bottom trailing edge. Send sits above its right end and Cancel at its left edge. Upward drags grow and move Send toward the thumb while activating its accent color; leftward drags grow and move Cancel toward the thumb in red. Direction changes reset the other cue. Reduced-motion settings suppress following; release thresholds and recording behavior remain unchanged. Browser dashboard dictation and desktop voice controls retain their existing distinct flows.

### Organization settings discoverability (2026-11-10)

The iPhone/iPad thread sidebar and Android sidebar include a labeled Settings button above search. iOS also exposes a Settings gear on Dashboard and New thread navigation. These open the same organization dialog without changing the selected thread or drafts. Project name/color and ordering controls are immediately visible below workspace management, without an expandable section. Browser companions retain their Settings entry; macOS retains its existing organization settings.


### Calls from new threads (2026-11-10)

New thread exposes Start call on iPhone/iPad's native navigation bar, Android's navigation bar, the web companion header, and the macOS new-thread composer. A ready project/agent and connected Mac are required on companions. Starting the call alone does not create a thread or launch an agent. The first spoken message creates the thread using the chosen project, agent, model, effort and permissions; later speech continues in that thread. The call survives the transition from the empty composer to the created conversation. Typed drafts and attachments are preserved. Navigating away ends the call.

### Dashboard model and difficulty selection

Dashboard task defaults appear once: Provider, then Model, then Difficulty. On iPad/tablet and desktop widths they form a compact group to the right of the Projects heading on the same baseline. On phone widths they move below the heading as a compact horizontal group and wrap if needed. They never appear on individual project cards. Shared web controls use the existing shadcn Select components; native controls match their small outlined appearance with trailing chevrons and accessible touch targets. Native accessibility text sizes may use extra space as needed. The selected model/difficulty applies across projects using the selected provider through existing launch controls, including new-thread and dictation paths. Providers without effort support keep their supported default.

### Dashboard recording controls at the pressed microphone

Native iOS/iPadOS and Android project/Commander dashboard recording controls appear just above the microphone that started capture, rather than at the dashboard's bottom-right corner. The waveform, elapsed time, Cancel, destination and upward Send cue move together and stay within the viewport. The layout follows the source control across scrolling/resizing and adapts to phone/tablet widths. Existing hold/release, tap-to-record, slide-to-send/cancel, accessibility actions and task routing remain intact. Desktop and browser microphones already render their recording feedback at their source controls.

### Companion busy-send queue placement (2026-11-10)

Companion sends classify against the latest local thread status before network delivery. A send during starting/running/waiting, behind another pending message, or into an existing/paused queue appears immediately in the queue above the composer, without first appearing as a transcript bubble. This applies to the shared companion runtime used by iPhone, iPad, Android and web companions, including native composer events. The local row persists across acknowledgment until a host snapshot owns it or echoes the dispatched user message; request IDs prevent duplicate rows during handoff. Queue edit/delete/steer controls remain disabled on device-owned rows until the host owns them. Existing outbox retry and recovery controls remain available. Idle replies retain immediate transcript feedback. The existing host persistent FIFO remains authoritative and handles the actual dispatch decision under its queue lock; there is no added pre-send network round trip.

### Dashboard Commander call (2026-11-10)

The dashboard offers **Call Commander** on desktop and companions; iOS/iPadOS and Android native dashboard headers expose the same entry point. A compact, expandable call island leaves the virtual office visible and persists when navigating to projects or threads. It provides microphone mute, End call, pending transcription, errors, and optional spoken replies. The foreground call uses existing local desktop capture or companion transcription and speech transports; background/lock-screen calls remain unsupported.

Spoken requests are serialized into the existing Commander conversation, creating it on the first request if necessary, without selecting it or changing the user's draft. Existing conversations receive FIFO messages; talking about another project does not interrupt Commander or a coding worker. Commander resolves actual projects through its existing tools, asks when the destination is ambiguous, and delegates under the configured autonomy/approval policy. Pending proposals stay pending until approved; the office follows real thread states and its existing walking/desk animation. This call does not override permission, budget, or project scope settings.

During calls, desktop snapshots include the Commander transcript; companions separately poll that transcript so browsing another conversation cannot divert spoken replies. Disconnect ends a companion call. Starting a thread call is unavailable while the dashboard call owns the microphone. No live provider turns or connected accounts are used as test fixtures.

Thread sheets use the native grabber and swipe-down dismissal without an additional down-chevron toolbar button.

## Google connected accounts: Gmail, Calendar and Drive (2026-11-10)

Settings → Connected accounts lets you sign in one or more Google accounts so agents can read and act on Gmail, Calendar and Drive, and so Google events can start agent work. These are Google accounts for tools and triggers, not coding-agent provider accounts; they are separate from the agent accounts used to run threads.

### Sign-in model and its limits

Freddai ships no Google credentials. You create your own Google Cloud OAuth client of type “Desktop app” and enter its client ID and secret in Settings. Sign-in uses a loopback redirect to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` with PKCE (S256) on the computer running the Freddai host; the consent page opens in the browser and an unfinished flow expires after 5 minutes. Requested scopes are `gmail.modify`, `calendar` and `drive` (each can be left unticked per service) plus `openid`, `email` and `profile`. Several accounts can be connected, each with its own granted services. Disconnect revokes the refresh token at Google (best effort; a revoke failure is reported but does not block) and always deletes the local credentials and account record.

Limits that follow from using an unverified personal client (Google's policies, not enforced by Freddai):

- Google shows an “unverified app” warning during consent; the setup page tells you to choose Advanced, continue, and tick every Gmail, Calendar and Drive box.
- An unverified client is limited to 100 sign-ins over its lifetime.
- While the Cloud project's publishing status is “Testing”, Google expires sign-ins after 7 days; the setup steps tell you to publish to “In production”.
- Distributing one shared client to other people would require Google's verification of these restricted scopes. That is not attempted; see `docs/backlog.md`.

### Storage

- Account metadata (email, name, granted services, status, last error, connected time): `<root>/google/accounts.json`. The OAuth client ID: `<root>/google/config.json`.
- Refresh tokens, cached access tokens and the client secret: macOS Keychain, service `com.freddai.google`. On other platforms an owner-only file, `<root>/google/secrets.json`, with mode 0600 on Unix. On Windows the code sets no extra permission and the file relies on the user's profile directory. The setup page says which store is in use.
- Trigger definitions: `<root>/google/triggers.json`; poll cursors, de-duplication IDs and undelivered items: `<root>/google/trigger-state.json`.

### Agent tools

The MCP bridge (`docs/mcp-bridge.md`) adds Google tools. `freddai_google_accounts` is always listed; every other Google tool appears only while at least one account is connected. Tools take an optional `account` (required when several are connected) and fail naming the account and service if that service was not granted. Gmail, Calendar and Drive content is treated as untrusted data.

- Gmail: search, read a message or thread, save an attachment, list labels, create/update/list/delete drafts (with local-file and Drive attachments), send, and modify (labels, archive, mark read, trash).
- Calendar: list calendars, list events, free/busy, and create/update/delete events (attendees, Meet link, update notifications).
- Drive: search, read as text, download or export, upload (resumable above 5 MB, up to 200 MB) and create folders.
- Triggers: list, save and pause/resume/delete Google triggers, using the same save path and delivery validation as the UI.

### Triggers

A Google trigger starts an agent run when `gmail_message_received`, `calendar_event_changed` or `drive_file_changed` happens in a connected account. An optional filter narrows matches (Gmail search syntax, or text matched against a Calendar event or Drive file name) and an optional resource picks a Calendar or Drive folder. Delivery fields (project, agent account, new chat or existing thread, notifications, prompt) are shared with schedules; new triggers are saved paused.

Freddai polls; it uses no Pub/Sub and no webhook. Gmail history is checked every 60 seconds, Calendar syncTokens and Drive changes every 120 seconds, with exponential backoff up to 15 minutes on failures and `Retry-After` honoured for 429/503. Enabling or resuming a trigger, or changing what it watches, discards undelivered items and arms it from that moment, and the first poll of a watched source records a baseline, so mail, events and files that already existed never fire; a lost cursor re-baselines, records the reason in `last_error` and fires nothing. Items found since the last delivery are batched into one message (the first ten are listed, then “and N more”), and no new delivery is made while the previous one is still queued. The message wraps event data between `BEGIN EXTERNAL EVENT DATA` and `END EXTERNAL EVENT DATA` lines and states that it is data, not instructions; delivery request IDs start with `gtrigger-`. Status (last checked, last fired, fire count, last error) is shown in the UI and returned by the list tool. Triggers keep running while the Freddai host is running; closing the GUI alone does not stop them.

### Approval behavior

Freddai holds outward Google actions for approval only for Commander. There, `freddai_gmail_send`, calendar create/update/delete that name attendees or set `send_updates` other than `none`, enabling or editing an enabled trigger, and trigger resume or delete become proposals that run only after the user approves (`freddai_propose` accepts the Google trigger tools). Drafts, label changes, Drive writes and calendar changes without attendees run directly and are logged. Ordinary project threads, including a thread started by a Google trigger from an email someone else wrote, are not held by Freddai: a send runs as soon as the agent calls it, limited only by the provider's own permission mode. This gap is recorded in `docs/backlog.md` as a decision.

2026-11-10: Sending email requires the user's explicit permission: an agent may send only an email the user has explicitly told it can be sent. This is enforced by instruction, in the `freddai_gmail_send` tool description, the MCP server instructions and the text delivered with every Google trigger, plus the existing Commander proposal hold. It is not a technical hold for ordinary threads; that hold, and validated workflows that may send automatically, are deferred.

### Platforms

macOS desktop and the Windows/Linux desktop hosts run the sign-in, store, poller and tools. iPhone, iPad and Android use the same shared Connected accounts screen over the existing encrypted companion transport, and can list and disconnect accounts and manage triggers. They cannot enter the client ID and secret or connect an account, because the sign-in redirect lands on the computer running the host; client secrets and tokens never cross the companion transport (the transport rejects `google_config_get`, `google_config_set`, `google_connect_start` and `google_connect_status`). Companions show an update notice if the connected host does not advertise the `google_accounts` capability. The existing-thread picker in the companion trigger editor is currently empty (see backlog).

### Failure behavior

A refresh token Google no longer accepts (`invalid_grant`) sets the account to `needs_reauth`: tools refuse with a message to reconnect in Settings → Connected accounts, and the UI shows “Needs reconnecting”. Other failures set `error` with `last_error`, which the UI shows. Every failed request, refresh, status change, poll failure and skipped firing emits a named `google.*` or `google.trigger.*` event on stderr, never containing tokens, codes, secrets or message content. There is no silent fallback: a failed Google call returns the error to the agent.

Release builds preserve Unix owner-only preview grant files while using portable file creation on Windows. Platform command registration excludes macOS-only recording APIs on Windows and Linux. Clean CI explicitly installs the separately locked relay dependencies. Windows host acceptance selects its synthetic project by ID so Commander project creation does not invalidate the large-snapshot check. Version 1.0.0 is distributed through signed desktop release artifacts and the published updater feed; publication and device verification are recorded separately.

Desktop Google capability detection shares its portable helper on Windows and Linux as well as Apple platforms; the helper has no platform-specific dependencies.

## Usage refresh recovery (2026-11-10)

Codex quota reads that fail with an authorization/expired-token error ask the same isolated CLI process to refresh managed authentication with `account/read {refreshToken:true}`, then retry the metadata read once within the original timeout. Failed renewal asks the user to reconnect that specific account; raw provider bodies are never displayed. Reset-credit consumption is never automatically retried by this recovery. Concurrent footer/settings/companion reads for a provider configuration share a serialized read and a two-second result cache to prevent overlapping token refreshes. No model turns or sign-in flows are started.

Settings Refresh also refreshes the footer. Failed settings refreshes preserve the last successful values and show the failure alongside them. Companion snapshots carry refresh errors so iPhone, iPad and Android mark even recently cached readings stale immediately and expose the reason. Unsupported quota readers (including Antigravity) say “Usage not supported” instead of unexplained question marks. Unknown values are never fabricated.

Protocol reference: https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app-server (account/read, refreshToken, account/rateLimits/read).

Verification: six UI regression tests, eleven Rust usage/protocol tests, two countdown/limiting-window tests, and 27 native companion tests passed; workspace TypeScript checks and diff checks passed. macOS was rebuilt with `pnpm native:install`, Developer ID signed, installed, normally restarted and its mapped executable verified in `/Applications/Freddai.app`. Both previously running threads remained running. The existing background host is still pending its idle upgrade, so the new host recovery path is not yet active there. A separate live metadata read through the new reader confirmed Codex LC's credential cannot be renewed and needs reconnecting; no model turn or login was started. RS and both Claude accounts returned real readings during diagnosis, but this does not establish ongoing provider availability.

Each physical Apple device was targeted with `FREDDAI_IOS_DEVICE` and `pnpm ios:install`; Apple Development signatures verified. DiPhone installed and launched; its process path matched the new installation URL. The 2021 iPad Pro 12 and 2022 iPad mini both installed successfully, but iOS denied launch because they were locked; unlock each and open Freddai to finish verification. Android APK rebuilt and its existing debug signature verified; adb listed no devices, so physical installation/launch requires connecting and unlocking an authorized Android device with USB debugging enabled. No simulator result substitutes for these outstanding device checks.

### Recording cue rails (2026-11-10)

Dashboard and composer recording cues use the same gesture contract on macOS, iPhone, iPad, Android, and browser companions: slide left 96 logical pixels/points to cancel, slide up 64 to arm sending, and let the dominant left direction take precedence. A hold of at least 300 ms sends on release; a quick tap keeps recording, with explicit Send and Cancel buttons. Browser permission/readiness work still stops when the originating press is released before capture begins.

The X moves only horizontally, and the upward arrow moves only vertically. Neither follows diagonal finger movement on the perpendicular axis. Switching direction or returning to the origin resets the inactive cue, including its translation and scale. Reduced-motion preferences suppress cue movement. The shared desktop/browser RecordingFeedback component owns timer, waveform and cue rendering; recording-gesture.ts owns thresholds and projection. UIKit's shared RecordingSwipe helper and Android's ComposerRecorder implement the equivalent native behavior for dashboard and composer. UIKit limits enlargement near a horizontal screen edge to keep the send rail stationary and its button visible. Android computes rest centres with scaled dimensions so enlargement does not feed back into translation.

Regression evidence: `pnpm ios:test:layout` initially reproduced diagonal motion at four phone/tablet widths (send X translation -31.5, cancel Y translation -27). The recording-specific native tests now pass, including safe-area bounds, rail projection, composer feedback, and reset. Thirteen focused React tests pass. An offline browser fixture verifies rail projection/reset and viewport fit at widths 320, 390, 744, 1024, and 1440. Workspace typechecking passes. Full native suite results and physical delivery are reported separately; no real microphone capture, transcription, account, or live agent turn is used as a fixture.
